\displaystyle\gamma^{(m)}(x_{1},\dots,x_{m})

\displaystyle=\kappa_{m}\left(\sin(N(x_{1})),\dots,\sin(N(x_{m}))\right).

Therefore,
\gamma_{\text{red}}^{(m)}(y_{1},\dots,y_{m-1})=\kappa_{m}\left(\sin(N(y_{1})),\dots,\sin(N(y_{m-1})),\sin(N(0))\right).
The heuristic for the remainder of the proof is as follows. Assume that
m=3
. Then, as
\sigma\to 0
, we have

\displaystyle\gamma_{\text{red}}^{(3)}(y_{1},y_{2})

\displaystyle=\kappa_{3}\left(\sin(N(y_{1})),\sin(N(y_{2})),\sin(N(0))\right)

\displaystyle\simeq\kappa_{3}\left(1+N(y_{1})+\frac{1}{2}N(y_{1})^{2},1+N(y_{2})+\frac{1}{2}N(y_{2})^{2},1+N(0)+\frac{1}{2}N(0)^{2}\right).

Then, using the multi-linearity of the cumulants, we obtain a sum of terms, whose main order with respect to
\sigma\to 0
is:

\sigma^{1+1+1}\kappa_{3}\left(N_{1},N_{2},N_{3}\right)+\sigma^{1+1+2}\kappa_{3}\left(N_{1},N_{2},N_{3}^{2}\right),

where
(N_{1},N_{2},N_{3})
is Gaussian. Accordingly, the first order term
\kappa_{3}\left(N_{1},N_{2},N_{3}\right)
vanishes, and the scaling
\sigma^{4}
appears. Below, we make the previous heuristic rigorous.

We only consider the case
m=4
, the case
m=3
being similar. Accordingly, in the following, we fix
m=4
and denote
y_{4}=0
. Note that if
[4]=L\sqcup L^{\prime}
with two disjoint non-empty sets
L
and
L^{\prime}
such that the family of random variables
(\sin(N(y_{l})))_{l\in L}
and
(\sin(N(y_{l})))_{l\in L^{\prime}}
are independent, then
\gamma_{\text{red}}^{(4)}(y_{1},\dots,y_{m-1})\equiv 0
(see, e.g.,
[
61
]
, Section 3.1. Property (ii)). Since the Gaussian field
(N(x))_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}
has covariance function having support included in
B(0,\rho/2)
, it implies that if
\gamma_{\text{red}}^{(m)}(y_{1},\dots,y_{m-1})\neq 0
then there exists a cycle
\pi:[4]\mapsto[4]
such that for all
s\in[d]
and
j\in[4]
, then
|y_{j}\cdot e_{s}-y_{\pi(j)}\cdot e_{s}|\leq\rho
. Accordingly,

\displaystyle|\gamma_{\text{red}}^{(4)}(\mathbb{R}^{3d})|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\text{cycle }\pi:[4]\mapsto[4]}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3d}}\prod_{i=1}^{d}\prod_{j=1}^{4}\mathds{1}_{|y_{j}\cdot e_{i}-y_{\pi(j)}\cdot e_{i}|\leq\rho_{i}}dy_{1}dy_{2}dy_{2}\sup_{z\in\mathbb{R}^{3d}}|\gamma_{\text{red}}^{(4)}(z)|

\displaystyle=(4-1)!\left(\prod_{i=1}^{d}2\rho\right)^{3}\sup_{z\in\mathbb{R}^{3d}}|\gamma_{\text{red}}^{(4)}(z)|.

(29)

It remains to bound the supremum of
h
. Note that
N=\sigma N_{0}
in distribution, where
N_{0}
is a Gaussian field with covariance function
c_{0}
. Moreover, Rolle’s theorem implies that for all
j\in[4]
there exists a random variable
Y_{j}\in\mathbb{R}
, such that
\sin(N(y_{j}))=\sigma N_{0}(y_{j})-\sigma^{3}N_{0}(y_{j})^{3}\cos(Y_{j})/6
. Using the multi-linearity of the cumulants we get

\displaystyle\gamma_{\text{red}}^{(4)}(y_{1},y_{2},y_{3})=\sum_{P\subset[4]}\sigma^{|P^{c}|+3|P|}\left(-\frac{1}{6}\right)^{|P|}\kappa_{4}(N_{0}(y_{l}),\cos(Y_{l^{\prime}})N_{0}(y_{l^{\prime}})^{3};l\in P^{c},l^{\prime}\in P),

where the sum runs over all the subsets
P
of
[4]
. Since the fourth order joint cumulants of a Gaussian field are zero, the sums runs in fact over the non empty subsets
P
. Hence,
|P^{c}|+3|P|=4+2|P|\geq 6
. Using
\sigma\leq 1
, we have

\displaystyle|\gamma_{\text{red}}^{(4)}(y_{1},y_{2},y_{3})|\leq\sigma^{6}\sum_{P\subset[4]}|\kappa_{4}(N_{0}(y_{l}),\cos(Y_{l^{\prime}})N_{0}(y_{l^{\prime}})^{3};l\in P^{c},l^{\prime}\in P)|

(30)

We denote
Z_{l}=N_{0}(x_{l})^{3}
is
l\in P
and
Z_{l}=N_{0}(x_{l})
if
l\in P^{c}
. By Hölder-inequality, we have

\displaystyle|\kappa_{4}(N_{0}(y_{l}),

\displaystyle\cos(Y_{l^{\prime}})N_{0}(y_{l^{\prime}})^{3};l\in P^{c},l^{\prime}\in P)|\leq\sum_{\pi\subset\Pi[4]}(|\pi|-1)!\prod_{b\in\pi}\prod_{l\in b}\mathbb{E}\left[|Z_{l}|^{|b|}\right]^{1/|b|},

which is a numerical constant. Recalling (
7
) and (
30
), this yields
|\gamma_{\text{red}}^{(4)}|(\mathbb{R}^{3d})\leq K\sigma^{6}\rho^{3d}
. With similar arguments, we obtain
|\gamma_{\text{red}}^{(3)}|(\mathbb{R}^{2d})\leq K\sigma^{4}\rho^{2d}
.
∎

The next lemma derives the structure factor and the pair correlation function of the arcsin-Cox processes considered in this section.

Lemma 7.3
.

Let
(N(x))_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}
be a stationary centered Gaussian field with bounded covariance function
c
having a compact support. We consider a Cox point process
\Phi_{Cox}
with intensity field
(1+\sin(N(x)))_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}
.
Then, the pair correlation function
g_{c}
of
\Phi_{Cox}
is

\displaystyle\forall x\in\mathbb{R}^{d},~g_{c}(x)=1+\frac{e^{-c(0)}}{2}\left(e^{c(x)}-e^{-c(x)}\right)

Moreover, the structure factor
S_{c}
of
\Phi_{Cox}
is

\displaystyle\forall k\in\mathbb{R}^{d},~S_{c}(k)=1+\frac{e^{-c(0)}}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(e^{c(x)}-e^{-c(x)}\right)e^{-\bm{i}k\cdot x}dx.

Proof.

The intensity of
\Phi_{Cox}
is
\mathbb{E}[1+\sin(N(0))]=1
. Moreover, its pair correlation function
g_{c}
is
[
32
]
:

\displaystyle g_{c}(x)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[(1+\sin(N(x)))(1+\sin(N(0)))\right]

\displaystyle=1+2\mathbb{E}\left[\sin(N(0))\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\sin(N(x))\sin(N(0))\right]=1+\mathbb{E}\left[\sin(N(x))\sin(N(0))\right].

The Moivre’s formula yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sin(N(x))\sin(N(0))\right]

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[e^{\bm{i}(N(x)+N(0))}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[e^{\bm{i}(N(x)-N(0))}\right]\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{e^{-c(0)}}{2}\left(e^{c(x)}-e^{-c(x)}\right).

Since
c
is compactly supported,
g_{c}-1\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. This concludes the proof, recalling (
3
).
∎

We are now in position to prove Theorem
4.1
.

Proof of Theorem
4.1
.

Let
W\in\mathcal{W}
. We prove the lower bound when estimating
S
at frequency zero.

r_{W}:=\inf_{\widehat{S}}\sup_{S\in\Theta(\beta,L)}\sup_{\Phi\sim\mathcal{L}_{(S,M)}}\sup_{k_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathbb{E}\left[h(|\Phi\cap W|^{\frac{\beta}{2\beta+d}}|\widehat{S}(k_{0};\Phi\cap W)-S(k_{0})|)\right]\\
\geq\inf_{\widehat{S}}\sup_{S\in\Theta(\beta,L)}\sup_{\Phi\sim\mathcal{L}_{(S,M)}}\mathbb{E}\left[h(|\Phi\cap W|^{\frac{\beta}{2\beta+d}}|\widehat{S}(0;\Phi\cap W)-S(0)|)\right].

The newt step is to reduce the proof to the square window setting. Since
\mathcal{W}
is regular, there exists
\eta>0
such that for all
W\in\mathcal{W}
there exists
R_{W}
such that
W\subset[-R_{W},R_{W}]^{d}
and
|[-R_{W},R_{W}]^{d}|\leq\eta|W|
. Let
\widehat{S}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\text{Conf}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\to\mathbb{R}
be any estimator. Using the fact that
h
is increasing and
|[-R_{W},R_{W}]^{d}|\leq\eta|W|
, we have

\sup_{S\in\Theta(\beta,L)}\sup_{\Phi\sim\mathcal{L}_{(S,M)}}\mathbb{E}\left[h(|W|^{\frac{\beta}{2\beta+d}}|\widehat{S}(0;\Phi\cap W)-S(0)|)\right]\\
\geq\sup_{S\in\Theta(\beta,L)}\sup_{\Phi\sim\mathcal{L}_{(S,M)}}\mathbb{E}\left[h_{\eta}(|[-R_{W},R_{W}]^{d}|^{\frac{\beta}{2\beta+d}}|\widehat{S}_{W}(0;\Phi\cap[-R_{W},R_{W}]^{d})-S(0)|)\right],

where
h_{\eta}:=h(\cdot/\eta^{\frac{2\beta}{2\beta+d}})
and
\widehat{S}_{W}(\cdot~;X):=\widehat{S}(\cdot~;X\cap W)
. Note that in the last line, we used that
W\cap[-R_{W},R_{W}]^{d}=W
. Accordingly,

\displaystyle r_{W}\geq\inf_{\widehat{S}}\sup_{S\in\Theta(\beta,L)}\sup_{\Phi\sim\mathcal{L}_{(S,M)}}\mathbb{E}\left[h_{\eta}(|[-R_{W},R_{W}]^{d}|^{\frac{\beta}{2\beta+d}}|\widehat{S}(0;\Phi\cap[-R_{W},R_{W}]^{d})-S(0)|)\right].

(31)

To derive a lower bound for this quantity, we follow the method presented in Chapter 2 of
[
77
]
, and specifically apply Theorem 2.2 therein.

To alleviate the following computations, we use the notation
R:=R_{W}
. Let
\Phi_{PPP}
be a Poisson point process of intensity
1
. Let
\phi:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{+}
be a non identically null, infinitely differentiable function with support included in
B(0,1/4)
. Then,
c_{0}=\phi\ast\phi/\|\phi\ast\phi\|_{\infty}
is positive definite and has support included in
B(0,1/2)
. Moreover
0\leq c_{0}\leq 1
. Let
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
with
1/\varepsilon\in\mathbb{N}
, that will be chosen small enough during in the proof. Let

\rho:=\varepsilon R/\left\lceil\frac{R}{R^{d/(d+2\beta)}}\right\rceil,\quad\sigma^{2}=(R^{d})^{-(d+\beta)/(2\beta+d)}.

We consider a stationary centered Gaussian field
(N(x))_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}
with covariance function
c(x)=\sigma^{2}c_{0}(x/\rho)
and a Cox point process
\Phi_{Cox}
with intensity field
(1+\sin(N(x)))_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}
. In order to apply Theorem 2.2 of
[
77
]
we prove that:

1.

The structure factor
S_{c}
of
\Phi_{Cox}
is
\Theta(\beta,L)
,

2.

|S_{c}(0)-1|\geq A|[-R,R]^{d}|^{-\beta/(2\beta+d)}
for some constant
A>0
that does not depend on
R
,

3.

\chi^{2}(\Phi_{PPP}\cap[-R,R]^{d},\Phi_{Cox}\cap[-R,R]^{d})\leq\alpha
where
\alpha<\infty
does also not depend on
R
,

4.

The total variations of the reduced factorial cumulant measures of
\Phi_{Cox}
satisfies

\max_{m\in\{2,3,4\}}|\gamma_{red}^{(m)}|(\mathbb{R}^{d(m-1)})\leq M.

For the moment, assume that all previous points are satisfied. Then, using (
31
), we obtain

\displaystyle r_{W}\geq\inf_{\widehat{S}}\max_{j=0,1}\mathbb{E}_{\Phi_{j}\cap[-R,R]^{d}}\left[h_{\eta}(|[-R,R]^{d}|^{\frac{\beta}{2\beta+d}}|\widehat{S}(0;\Phi_{j}\cap[-R,R]^{d})-S(0)|)\right],

where
\Phi_{0}:=\Phi_{PPP}
and
\Phi_{1}:=\Phi_{Cox}
. Note that the expectations are with respect to the distributions of
\Phi_{j}\cap[-R,R]^{d}
. Then, employing the method of Chapter 2, Section 2 of
[
77
]
, we obtain
r_{W}\geq c_{1}\inf_{\Psi}\max_{j=0,1}\mathbb{P}_{\Phi_{j}\cap[-R,R]^{d}}[\Psi\neq j],

where the infimum is over all tests
\Psi:\text{Conf}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\to\{0,1\}
, and
c_{1}
is a constant that does not depend on
R
. Using Theorem 2.2 of
[
77
]
, we obtain
r_{W}\geq c_{1}\exp(-\alpha)/2>0
, which does not depend on
W
. This yields (
6
).

Now, we prove that points
1
,
2
,
3
and
4
are satisfied. We consider the point
1
. According to Lemma
7.3
, the structure factor
S_{c}
of
\Phi_{Cox}
is

\displaystyle S_{c}(k)

\displaystyle=1+\frac{e^{-\sigma^{2}c_{0}(0)}}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(e^{\sigma^{2}c_{0}(x/\rho)}-e^{-\sigma^{2}c_{0}(x/\rho)}\right)e^{-\bm{i}k\cdot x}dx

\displaystyle=1+\rho^{d}\frac{e^{-\sigma^{2}c_{0}(0)}}{2}\int_{B(0,1)}\left(e^{\sigma^{2}c_{0}(x)}-e^{-\sigma^{2}c_{0}(x)}\right)e^{-\bm{i}\rho k\cdot x}dx.

(32)

Since
c_{0}
has compact support, the Lebesgue dominated convergence theorem ensures that
S_{c}
is infinitely differentiable. Let
l\geq 0
be the smallest integer that is strictly smaller than
\beta
. In particular,
S_{c}
is
l
-times differentiable. Moreover, for all
n=(n_{1},\dots,n_{d})\in\mathbb{N}^{d}
such that
n_{1}+\dots+n_{d}=l
, we have for
(k,k^{\prime})\in\mathbb{R}^{2d}
:

\displaystyle|\partial_{n}^{l}S_{c}(k)-\partial_{n}^{l}S_{c}(k^{\prime})|

\displaystyle\leq\rho^{d+l}\int_{B(0,1)}\frac{1}{2}\left(e^{\sigma^{2}c_{0}(x)}-e^{-\sigma^{2}c_{0}(x)}\right)|x|^{l}\left|e^{\bm{i}\rho k\cdot x}-e^{\bm{i}\rho k^{\prime}\cdot x}\right|dx

\displaystyle\leq e\rho^{d+l}\sigma^{2}\int_{B(0,1)}c_{0}(x)|x|^{l}2^{1-(\beta-l)}\rho^{\beta-l}\left|k-k^{\prime}\right|^{\beta-l}|x|^{\beta-l}dx

\displaystyle\leq 2e\rho^{d+\beta}\sigma^{2}\left(\int_{B(0,1)}c_{0}(x)|x|^{\beta}dx\right)|k-k^{\prime}|^{\beta-l}.

The choices of
\rho
and
\sigma^{2}
ensure:

\displaystyle\rho^{d+\beta}\sigma^{2}\leq\varepsilon^{d+\beta}\frac{R^{d+\beta}}{\left(R/R^{d/(2\beta+d)}\right)^{d+\beta}}(R^{d})^{-(d+\beta)/(2\beta+d)}=\varepsilon^{d+\beta}

(33)

By reducing
\varepsilon
if necessary, we have
S_{c}\in\Theta(\beta,L)
. Concerning point
2
, with (
7
), we get

\displaystyle|S_{c}(0)-1|

\displaystyle=\rho^{d}\frac{e^{-\sigma^{2}c_{0}(0)}}{2}\int_{B(0,1)}\left(e^{\sigma^{2}c_{0}(x)}-e^{-\sigma^{2}c_{0}(x)}\right)dx

\displaystyle\geq\rho^{d}\sigma^{2}e^{-c_{0}(0)}\int_{B(0,1)}e^{\sigma^{2}c_{0}(x)}c_{0}(x)dx\geq\rho^{d}\sigma^{2}e^{-c_{0}(0)}\|c_{0}\|_{1}.

Note that

\displaystyle\rho^{d}\sigma^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\varepsilon^{d}\frac{R^{d}}{\left(R/R^{d/(2\beta+d)}+1\right)^{d+\beta}}(R^{d})^{-\frac{d+\beta}{2\beta+d}}\geq 2^{-\beta-d}\varepsilon^{d}(R^{d})^{-\frac{\beta}{2\beta+d}}.

Subsequently, point
2
holds with
A=2^{-\beta-d}\varepsilon^{d}e^{-c_{0}(0)}\|c_{0}\|_{1}.
To verify point
3
, we use Lemma
7.1
. If
\varepsilon=1/n_{0}
with
n_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
and
n_{0}\geq 5
, then its assumptions are satisfied. Indeed, with computation similar to (
33
), we get

8^{d}\sigma^{4}\rho^{2d}\leq 8^{d}\varepsilon^{2d}(R^{d})^{-2\beta/(2\beta+d)}\leq 8^{d}\varepsilon^{2d}<1.

Hence Lemma
7.1
ensures that

\displaystyle\chi^{2}(\Phi_{PPP}\cap[-R,R]^{d},\Phi_{Cox}\cap[-R,R]^{d})\leq\left(1-8^{d}\sigma^{4}\rho^{2d}\right)^{-\frac{R^{d}}{\rho^{d}}}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left(1-8^{d}\varepsilon^{2d}(R^{d})^{-\frac{2\beta}{2\beta+d}}\right)^{-\frac{(R^{d})^{2\beta/(2\beta+d)}}{\varepsilon^{d}}}\leq\sup_{x\in[1,\infty)}(1-2d8^{d}\varepsilon^{2d}/x)^{-x/\varepsilon^{d}}:=\alpha.

Accordingly, point (
3
) holds. It remains to verify point
4
. Firstly, using
\sinh(x)\leq xe^{x}
for
x\geq 0
, we have

\displaystyle|\gamma_{\text{red}}^{(2)}|(\mathbb{R}^{d})

\displaystyle=\frac{e^{-\sigma^{2}c_{0}(0)}\rho^{d}}{2}\int_{B(0,1)}(e^{\sigma^{2}c_{0}(x)}-e^{-\sigma^{2}c_{0}(x)})dx

\displaystyle\leq\rho^{d}\sigma^{2}e\int_{B(0,1)}c_{0}(x)dx\leq\varepsilon^{2d}e\int_{B(0,1)}c_{0}(x)dx.

Moreover, according to Lemma
7.2
,

|\gamma_{\text{red}}^{(3)}|(\mathbb{R}^{2d})\leq K\sigma^{4}\rho^{2d},

|\gamma_{\text{red}}^{(4)}|(\mathbb{R}^{3d})\leq K\sigma^{6}\rho^{3d},

for some numerical constant
K<\infty
. Since,

\sigma^{6}\rho^{3d}\leq\varepsilon^{3d}(R^{d})^{3d/(2\beta+d)}R^{-3(d+\beta)/(2\beta+d)}\leq\varepsilon^{3d},\quad\sigma^{4}\rho^{2d}\leq\varepsilon^{2d},

then point
4
holds, if
\varepsilon
is small enough. Theorem 2.2. of
[
77
]
concludes the proof.
∎

Proof of Theorem
4.2
.

Let
W\in\mathcal{W}
. Arguing as in the beginning of the proof of Theorem
4.1
, we obtain

\displaystyle r

\displaystyle:=\inf_{\widehat{g}}\sup_{g\in\Theta(\beta,L)}\sup_{\Phi\sim\mathcal{L}_{(g,M)}}\sup_{x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathbb{E}\left[h(|W|^{\frac{\beta}{2\beta+d}}|\widehat{g}(x_{0};\Phi\cap W)-g(x_{0})|)\right]

\displaystyle\geq\inf_{\widehat{g}}\sup_{g\in\Theta(\beta,L)}\sup_{\Phi\sim\mathcal{L}_{(g,M)}}\mathbb{E}\left[h_{\eta}(|[-R,R]^{d}|^{\frac{\beta}{2\beta+d}}|\widehat{g}(0;\Phi\cap[-R,R]^{d})-g(0)|)\right],

where
\eta
does not depends on
W
,
h_{\eta}:=h(\cdot/\eta^{\beta/(2\beta+d)})
and
R=R_{W}>0
. As in the proof of Theorem
4.1
, the result will follow from the method of Chapter 2 of
[
77
]
.

Let
\Phi_{PPP}
be a Poisson point process of intensity
1
. Let
\phi:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{+}
be a non identically null, infinitely differentiable function with support included in
B(0,1/4)
. Then,
c_{0}=\phi\ast\phi/\|\phi\ast\phi\|_{\infty}
is positive definite and has support included in
B(0,1/2)
. Moreover
0\leq c_{0}\leq 1
. Let
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
, that will be chosen small enough during in the proof. Consider

\rho:=1/\left\lceil R^{d/(d+2\beta)}\right\rceil,\quad\sigma^{2}=\varepsilon R^{-d\beta/(2\beta+d)}.

We consider a stationary centered Gaussian field
(N(x))_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}
with covariance function
c(x)=\sigma^{2}c_{0}(x/\rho)
and a Cox point process
\Phi_{Cox}
with intensity field
(1+\sin(N(x)))_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}
. In order to apply Theorem 2.2 of
[
77
]
we prove that:

1.

The pair correlation function
g_{c}
of
\Phi_{Cox}
is
\Theta(\beta,L)
,

2.

|g_{c}(0)-1|\geq A|[-R,R]^{d}|^{-\beta/(2\beta+d)}
for some constant
A>0
that does not depend on
R
,

3.

\chi^{2}(\Phi_{PPP}\cap[-R,R]^{d},\Phi_{Cox}\cap[-R,R]^{d})\leq\alpha
where
\alpha<\infty
does also not depend on
R
,

4.

The total variations of the reduced factorial cumulant measures of
\Phi_{Cox}
satisfies

\max_{m\in\{2,3,4\}}|\gamma_{red}^{(m)}|(\mathbb{R}^{d(m-1)})\leq M.

We check point
1
. Recall from Lemma
7.3
, that

\forall x\in\mathbb{R}^{d},~g_{c}(x)=1+\frac{e^{-\sigma^{2}c_{0}(0)}}{2}\left(e^{\sigma^{2}c_{0}(x/\rho)}-e^{-\sigma^{2}c_{0}(x/\rho)}\right).

Let
n=(n_{1},\dots,n_{d})\in\mathbb{N}^{d}
and
l=n_{1}+\dots+n_{d}
. According to Faà-di-Bruno formula,

\displaystyle\partial_{n}^{l}(g_{c}(x)-1)=\frac{e^{-\sigma^{2}c_{0}(0)}}{2}\sum_{\pi\in[l]}\left(e^{\sigma^{2}c_{0}(\frac{x}{\rho})}\prod_{b\in\pi}\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\rho^{|b|}}\partial_{b}c_{0}(\frac{x}{\rho})+(-1)^{|\pi|}e^{-\sigma^{2}c_{0}(\frac{x}{\rho})}\prod_{b\in\pi}\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\rho^{|b|}}\partial_{b}c_{0}(\frac{x}{\rho})\right),

where in the partition
\pi
, the numbers
1,\dots,n_{1}
correspond to a differentiation with respect to
x_{1}
, and
n_{1}+1,\dots,n_{1}+n_{2}
to
x_{2}
and so on. This yields, using
\sigma\leq 1
,

\displaystyle\|\partial_{n}^{l}(g_{c}-1)\|_{\infty}\leq e\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\rho^{l}}\sum_{\pi\in[m]}\prod_{b\in\pi}\|\partial_{b}c_{0}\|_{\infty}.

Let
l\geq 0
be the smallest integer that is strictly smaller than
\beta
. Then, for
(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{2d}
,

\displaystyle|\partial_{n}^{l}g_{c}(x)-\partial_{n}^{l}g_{c}(x)|

\displaystyle\leq(2\|\partial_{n}^{l}(g_{c}-1)\|_{\infty})^{1-(\beta-l)}(\|\nabla\partial_{n}^{l}(g_{c}-1)\|_{\infty}|x-y|)^{\beta-l}

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\left(\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\rho^{l}}\right)^{1-(\beta-l)}\left(\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\rho^{l+1}}\right)^{\beta-l}|x-y|^{\beta-l}=C_{1}\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\rho^{\beta}}|x-y|^{\beta-l},

for some constant
C_{1}<\infty
that depends only on
c_{0}
.
The choices of
\sigma^{2}
and
\rho
ensure that
c_{1}\sigma^{2}/\rho^{\beta}\leq L
for
\varepsilon
small enough. This yields point
1
. Concerning point
2
, we have, using
\sinh(x)\geq x
for
x\geq 0
,

\displaystyle g_{c}(0)-1=\frac{e^{-\sigma^{2}c_{0}(0)}}{2}\left(e^{\sigma^{2}c_{0}(0)}-e^{-\sigma^{2}c_{0}(0)}\right)\geq\frac{e^{-\varepsilon}}{2}\sigma^{2}c_{0}(0)=A|[-R,R]^{d}|^{-\beta/(2\beta+d)},

with
A=\exp(-\varepsilon)\varepsilon^{2}c_{0}(0)/2>0
. We deduce point
3
from Lemma
7.1
:

\displaystyle\chi^{2}(\Phi_{PPP}\cap[-R,R]^{d},\Phi_{Cox}\cap[-R,R]^{d})

\displaystyle\leq(1-\varepsilon^{2}8^{d}R^{-(d+\beta)/(2\beta+d)})^{\varepsilon R^{(d+\beta)/(2\beta+d)}}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{x\geq 1}(1-\varepsilon^{2}8^{d}/x)^{\varepsilon x}=:\alpha,

provided that
\varepsilon^{2}8^{d}<1
. Finally, since
\sigma
and
\rho
are smaller than 1, Lemma
7.2
ensures that point
4
is satisfied. This concludes the proof, using Theorem 2.2. of
[
77
]
.

∎

Appendix A
Proofs related to Section
5.2

A.1
Variance study

The next lemma provides an upper bound on the variance of the multitaper estimators. Its main argument relies on Bessel’s inequality:

\sum_{i\in I}|\langle f_{i},h\rangle|^{2}\leq\|h\|_{2}^{2},

for any orthonormal family of function
(f_{i})_{i\in I}
of
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
h\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. This allows us to exploit the orthogonality of the tapers in a non-asymptotic regime. This argument will be used extensively throughout the proofs.

Lemma A.1
.

Let
\Phi
satisfying Assumption
3.1
with intensity
\lambda=1
. Let
W
be a subset of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
I
be a discrete subset of
\mathbb{N}^{d}
and let
k_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. We consider a family of orthonormal functions
(f_{i})_{i\in I}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{|I|}
. Then,

\operatorname{Var}[\widehat{S}(k_{0})]\leq\frac{2\|S\|_{\infty}^{2}}{|I|}\left(1+\left(\sum_{i\in I}\|f_{i}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus W}\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)^{2}+\left(\frac{1}{|I|}\sum_{i\in I}\|f_{i}\|_{4}^{2}\right)^{2}|\gamma_{\text{red}}^{4}|,

(34)

where
|\gamma_{\text{red}}^{4}|:=\lambda+7|\gamma_{\text{red}}^{(2)}|(\mathbb{R}^{d})+6|\gamma_{\text{red}}^{(3)}|(\mathbb{R}^{2d})+|\gamma_{\text{red}}^{(4)}|(\mathbb{R}^{3d}).

Proof.

Recall that
\widehat{S}(k_{0})=|I|^{-1}\sum_{i\in I}|C_{i}(k_{0})|^{2}
where
(C_{i}(k_{0}))_{i\in I}
is defined in (
9
). Using the bi-linearity of the covariance, we get

\displaystyle\operatorname{Var}[\widehat{S}(k_{0})]=\frac{1}{|I|^{2}}\sum_{(i_{1},i_{2})\in I^{2}}\kappa_{2}(|C_{i_{1}}(k_{0})|^{2},|C_{i_{2}}(k_{0})|^{2}).

According to Corollary
G.1
, we have

\displaystyle\kappa_{2}(|C_{i_{1}}(k_{0})|^{2},|C_{i_{2}}(k_{0})|^{2})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\sigma\in\Pi[4],\\
\sigma\vee\tau=\mathbf{1}_{4}\end{subarray}}\prod_{b\in\sigma}\kappa(D_{i_{j}}(k_{0});j\in b),

where
\tau=12|34
,
\vee
denotes the least-upper-bound between partitions (see Section
G
) and

D_{i_{1}}(k_{0})=C_{i_{1}}(k_{0}),~D_{i_{2}}(k_{0})=\overline{C_{i_{1}}(k_{0})},~D_{i_{3}}(k_{0})=C_{i_{2}}(k_{0}),~D_{i_{4}}(k_{0})=\overline{C_{i_{2}}(k_{0})}.

Note that we are considering cumulants of centered random variables, so the sum runs over the partitions with blocks of size larger than
2
. Hence:

\displaystyle\kappa_{2}(|C_{i_{1}}

\displaystyle(k_{0})|^{2},|C_{i_{2}}(k_{0})|^{2})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\sigma\in\Pi_{2}[4],\\
\sigma\vee\tau=\mathbf{1}_{4}\end{subarray}}\prod_{b\in\sigma}\kappa(E_{i_{j}}(k_{0});j\in b)

\displaystyle=\kappa(T_{i_{1}}(k_{0}),\overline{T_{i_{1}}(k_{0})},T_{i_{2}}(k_{0}),\overline{T_{i_{2}}(k_{0})})+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\sigma\in\Pi_{2}[4],\\
\sigma\vee\tau=\mathbf{1}_{4},|\sigma|=2\end{subarray}}\prod_{b\in\sigma}\operatorname{Cov}\left[E_{i_{b_{1}}}(k_{0}),E_{i_{b_{2}}}(k_{0})\right],

(35)

where
\Pi_{2}[4]
denotes the sets of partitions of
[4]
having only blocks of size larger than
2
and

E_{i_{1}}(k_{0})=T_{i_{1}}(k_{0}),~E_{i_{2}}(k_{0})=\overline{T_{i_{1}}(k_{0})},~E_{i_{3}}(k_{0})=T_{i_{2}}(k_{0}),~E_{i_{4}}(k_{0})=\overline{T_{i_{2}}(k_{0})}.

We bound the cumulant corresponding to the block of order
4
using Lemma
D.2
:

\displaystyle|\kappa

\displaystyle(T_{i_{1}}(k_{0}),\overline{T_{i_{1}}(k_{0})},T_{i_{2}}(k_{0}),\overline{T_{i_{2}}(k_{0})})|\leq\|f_{i_{1}}\|_{4}^{2}\|f_{i_{2}}\|_{4}^{2}\sum_{\pi\in\Pi[4]}|\gamma_{\text{red}}^{(|\pi|)}(\mathbb{R}^{d(|\pi|-1)}).

(36)

Subsequently,

\frac{1}{|I|^{2}}\sum_{(i_{1},i_{2})\in I^{2}}|\kappa(T_{i_{1}}(k_{0}),\overline{T_{i_{1}}(k_{0})},T_{i_{2}}(k_{0}),\overline{T_{i_{2}}(k_{0})})|\leq\left(\frac{1}{|I|}\sum_{i\in I}\|f_{i}\|_{4}^{2}\right)^{2}|\gamma_{\text{red}}^{4}|.

(37)

We now focus on the covariance terms. Note that
\tau=12|34
, so the only two partitions
\sigma\in\Pi_{2}[4]
such that
\sigma\vee\tau=\mathbf{1}_{4}
are
13|24
and
14|23
. Accordingly,

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\sigma\in\Pi[4],\\
\sigma\vee\tau=\mathbf{1}_{4},|\sigma|=2\end{subarray}}\prod_{b\in\sigma}\operatorname{Cov}\big[E_{i_{b_{1}}}(k_{0}),

\displaystyle E_{i_{b_{2}}}(k_{0})\big]=\operatorname{Cov}\left[E_{i_{1}}(k_{0}),E_{i_{3}}(k_{0})\right]\operatorname{Cov}\left[E_{i_{2}}(k_{0}),E_{i_{4}}(k_{0})\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+\operatorname{Cov}\left[E_{i_{1}}(k_{0}),E_{i_{4}}(k_{0})\right]\operatorname{Cov}\left[E_{i_{2}}(k_{0}),E_{i_{3}}(k_{0})\right]

\displaystyle=\left|\operatorname{Cov}\left[T_{i_{1}}(k_{0}),T_{i_{2}}(k_{0})\right]\right|^{2}+\left|\operatorname{Cov}\left[T_{i_{1}}(k_{0}),T_{i_{2}}(-k_{0})\right]\right|^{2}.

According to Lemma
D.3
, we have

\frac{1}{|I|^{2}}\sum_{(i_{1},i_{2})\in I^{2}}\left(\left|\operatorname{Cov}\left[T_{i_{1}}(k_{0}),T_{i_{2}}(k_{0})\right]\right|^{2}+\left|\operatorname{Cov}\left[T_{i_{1}}(k_{0}),T_{i_{2}}(-k_{0})\right]\right|^{2}\right)\\
\leq\frac{2\|S\|_{\infty}^{2}}{|I|}\left(1+\left(\sum_{i\in I}\|f_{i}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus W}\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{1/2}\right)^{2},

(38)

which concludes the proof of this lemma.

∎

A.2
Bias study

Next lemma studies the bias for the multitaper estimator. Note that the factor
\sqrt{d}
appears only when
\beta>1
and is due to the assumption that
S\in\Theta(\beta,L)
, which concerns the coefficient of the gradient of
S
and not their
\ell^{2}([d])
norm.

Lemma A.2
.

Let
\Phi
satisfying Assumption
3.1
with intensity
\lambda=1
. Let
W
be a subset of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
I
be a discrete subset of
\mathbb{N}^{d}
,
k_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and let
(f_{i})_{i\in I}
be a family of functions with unit
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
norm. Assume that
S\in\Theta(\beta,L)
with
(\beta,L)\in(0,\infty)^{2}
(see Definition
1
). Assume that
\beta\leq 2
. Then,

\left|\mathbb{E}\left[\widehat{S}(k_{0})\right]-S(k_{0})\right|\leq\frac{L\sqrt{d}}{|I|}\sum_{i\in I}\|f_{i}\|_{\dot{H}^{\beta/2}}^{2}+\frac{4\|S\|_{\infty}}{|I|}\sum_{i\in I}\|f_{i}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus W}\|_{2}.

(39)

Proof.

Assume first that
\beta\leq 1
. Using the fact that
\|f_{i}\|_{2}=1
for all
i\in I
, we get

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}\left[\widehat{S}(k_{0})\right]-S(k_{0})\right|\leq A+B,

where

\displaystyle A:=\frac{1}{|I|}\sum_{i\in I}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left||\mathcal{F}[f_{i}\mathds{1}_{W}](k)|^{2}-|\mathcal{F}[f_{i}](k)|^{2}\right|\left(S(k_{0}+k)+S(k_{0})\right)dk,

(40)

\displaystyle B=\frac{1}{|I|}\sum_{i\in I}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\mathcal{F}[f_{i}](k)|^{2}\left|S(k_{0}+k)-S(k_{0})\right|dk.

Since
S
is
\beta
-Hölder with
\beta\leq 1
, we have

\displaystyle B\leq\frac{1}{|I|}\sum_{i\in I}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\mathcal{F}[f_{i}](k)|^{2}L|k|^{\beta}dk=\frac{L}{|I|}\sum_{i\in I}\|f_{i}\|_{\dot{H}^{\beta/2}}^{2}.

Using the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and the Plancherel theorem we obtain

\displaystyle A

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\|S\|_{\infty}}{|I|}\sum_{i\in I}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left|\mathcal{F}[f_{i}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus W}](k)|\right|\left||\mathcal{F}[f_{i}\mathds{1}_{W}](k)|+|\mathcal{F}[f_{i}](k)|\right|dk

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\|S\|_{\infty}}{|I|}\sum_{i\in I}\|\mathcal{F}[f_{i}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus W}]\|_{2}\||\mathcal{F}[f_{i}\mathds{1}_{W}]|+|\mathcal{F}[f_{i}]|\|_{2}\leq\frac{4\|S\|_{\infty}}{|I|}\sum_{i\in I}\|f_{i}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus W}\|_{2},

which concludes the proof when
\beta\leq 1
. Assume now that
\beta\in(1,2]
. Hence
S
is differentiable and using the fact that
k\mapsto|\mathcal{F}[f_{i}\mathds{1}_{W}](k)|^{2}\nabla S(k_{0})\cdot k
is even, we have

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}\left[\widehat{S}(k_{0})\right]-S(k_{0})\right|=\left|\frac{1}{|I|}\sum_{i\in I}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\mathcal{F}[f_{i}\mathds{1}_{W}](k)|^{2}\left(S(k_{0}+k)-S(k_{0})-\nabla S(k_{0})\cdot k\right)dk\right|\leq A+B^{\prime},

where
A
is defined in (
40
) and

B^{\prime}:=\frac{1}{|I|}\sum_{i\in I}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\mathcal{F}[f_{i}](k)|^{2}\left|S(k_{0}+k)-S(k_{0})-\nabla S(k_{0})\cdot k\right|dk.

Since
S
is
\beta
-Hölder with
\beta\geq 1
, we have

\displaystyle\left|S(k_{0}+k)-S(k_{0})-\nabla S(k_{0})\cdot k\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\int_{0}^{1}\left(\nabla S(k_{0}+tk)-\nabla S(k_{0})\right)\cdot kdt\right|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{1}\sqrt{d}Lt^{\beta-1}|k|^{\beta-1}|k|dt\leq L\sqrt{d}|k|^{\beta}.

(41)

Then, concluding as in the proof of the case
\beta\leq 1
, we obtain the result when
\beta\in(1,2]
.
∎

A.3
Proofs of Theorem
5.1
and Corollary
5.1

In this section, gathering Lemmas
A.1
and
A.2
, we prove Theorem
5.1
. Then, we specify Theorem
5.1
for the Hermite tapers with Corollary
5.1
.

Proof of Theorem
5.1
.

The bias variance decomposition writes

\displaystyle\text{s}:=\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\widehat{S}(k_{0})-S(k_{0})\right|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\operatorname{Var}[\widehat{S}(k_{0})]+\left|\mathbb{E}\left[\widehat{S}(k_{0})\right]-S(k_{0})\right|^{2}.

According to Lemma
A.1
and Lemma
A.2
, we have, by triangular inequality

\displaystyle s\leq\Bigg(\frac{2\|S\|_{\infty}^{2}}{|I|}\left(1+\left(\sum_{i\in I}\|f_{i}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus W}\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)^{2}+\left(\frac{1}{|I|}\sum_{i\in I}\|f_{i}\|_{4}^{2}\right)^{2}|\gamma_{\text{red}}^{4}|(\mathbb{R}^{3d})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\left(\frac{\sqrt{d}L}{|I|r^{\beta}}\sum_{i\in I}\|f_{i}\|_{\dot{H}^{\beta/2}}^{2}+\frac{4\|S\|_{\infty}}{|I|}\sum_{i\in I}\|f_{i}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus W}\|_{2}\right)^{2}\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sqrt{2}\|S\|_{\infty}}{|I|^{\frac{1}{2}}}+\frac{1}{|I|}\sum_{i\in I}\|f_{i}\|_{4}^{2}|\gamma_{\text{red}}^{4}|(\mathbb{R}^{3d})^{\frac{1}{2}}+\frac{\sqrt{d}L}{|I|}\sum_{i\in I}\|f_{i}\|_{\dot{H}^{\beta/2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\sqrt{2}\|S\|_{\infty}\left(\frac{1}{|I|}\sum_{i\in I}\|f_{i}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus W}\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\frac{4\|S\|_{\infty}}{|I|}\sum_{i\in I}\|f_{i}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus W}\|_{2}.

We conclude using
\sqrt{2}+4\leq 6
and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality:

\sum_{i\in I}\|f_{i}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus W}\|_{2}\leq|I|^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\sum_{i\in I}\|f_{i}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus W}\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

∎

Proof of Corollary
5.1
.

The change of variable
x\leftrightarrow x/r
and Lemma
F.1
applied with
\rho=R/r
give
W\text{-}loc\leq Ce^{-c^{\prime}(R/r)^{3\theta}}\leq Ce^{-c^{\prime}|I|^{\frac{3\theta}{2d}}},

where
0<c^{\prime},C<\infty
are constants. In the second inequality we used (
13
). With (
14
), we obtain

W\text{-}loc\leq Ce^{-c|W|^{\frac{3\beta\theta}{d(2\beta+d)}}},

for constants that do not depend on
r,I
and
W
. Moreover, Lemma
F.2
and Lemma
F.3
yield

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\text{-}loc\leq\frac{L\sqrt{d}|I|^{\beta/(2d)}}{r^{\beta}},\qquad\frac{1}{|I|}\sum_{i\in I}\|\psi_{i}\|_{4}^{2}\leq c_{\psi}^{d}\frac{\log(|I|^{\frac{1}{d}})^{\frac{d}{2}}}{r^{d/2}|I|^{1/4}},

where
c_{\psi}\in(0,\infty)
is a numerical constant. Condition (
13
) implies that
r\geq R/(\sqrt{2}|I|^{1/(2d)})
.
Subsequently, we get, with Theorem
5.1

\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\widehat{S}(k_{0})-S(k_{0})\right|^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\\
\frac{\sqrt{2}\|S\|_{\infty}}{|I|^{\frac{1}{2}}}+\frac{L\sqrt{d}}{2^{\frac{\beta}{2}}}\left(\frac{|I|}{|W|}\right)^{\frac{\beta}{d}}+\left(\frac{c_{\psi}}{\sqrt{2}}\right)^{d}|\gamma_{\text{red}}^{4}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{\log(|I|^{\frac{1}{d}})^{\frac{d}{2}}}{|W|^{\frac{1}{2}}}+6C\|S\|_{\infty}e^{-c|W|^{\frac{3\beta\theta}{d(2\beta+d)}}}.

(42)

Using condition (
14
) we conclude the proof with elementary computations.
∎

Appendix B
Proof related to Section
5.3

The results of Section
5.3
rely on the control the cumulants of the multitaper estimator. Lemma
B.1
, which constitutes a first step for a workable bound on the cumulants of
\widehat{S}(k_{0})
, is the core of the proof of Theorem
5.2
. It relies on the Isserlis-Wick formula of Corollary
G.1
that allows to express the joint cumulants of square of random variables as a sum of products of joint cumulants of these random variables. The key point of this lemma is to provide a bound on the cumulants of the multitaper estimator where the tapers indexes are summed (see
\Sigma
defined in (
18
)). To this end, we make essential use of the combinatorial Lemma
B.2
, which crucially exploits the connectivity condition of the partitions involved.

Lemma B.1
.

Let
\Phi
satisfying Assumption
3.1
with intensity
\lambda=1
and such that
S\in\Theta(\beta,L)
with
\beta\in(0,2]
and
L<\infty.
Let
W
be a subset of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
I
be a discrete subset of
\mathbb{N}^{d}
. Assume that
(f_{i})_{i\in I}
is family of orthonormal functions that are
L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\cap L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. If
\beta>1
, assume for all
(i,i^{\prime})\in I^{2}
,
\mathcal{F}[f_{i}](k)\mathcal{F}[f_{i^{\prime}}](k)=\mathcal{F}[f_{i}](-k)\mathcal{F}[f_{i^{\prime}}](-k)
for all
k\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Let
k_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Then, for all
m\geq 2
,

\displaystyle|\kappa_{m}(\widehat{S}(k_{0}))|\leq 2^{m-1}(m-1)!\frac{S(k_{0})^{m}}{|I|^{m-1}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\sigma\in\Pi[2m],\\
\sigma\vee\tau=\mathbf{1}_{2m},|\sigma|\geq 2\end{subarray}}\prod_{b\in\sigma}K_{|b|},

(43)

where, for
\Sigma
defined in (
18
):

\displaystyle K_{1}=0,~K_{2}=\Sigma,\quad\forall s\geq 3,~K_{s}=\left(\frac{1}{|I|}\sum_{i\in I}a_{s}(i)^{2}\right)^{\frac{s}{2}}\sum_{\pi\in\Pi[s]}|\gamma_{\text{red}}^{(|\pi|)}|(\mathbb{R}^{d(|\pi|-1)}).

(44)

Finally,
a_{s}(i):=\|f_{i}\|_{s}
for
s\geq 4
and

\displaystyle a_{3}(i):=\left(3\sup_{i\in I}\|f_{i}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus W}\|_{3}\sup_{i\in I}\|f_{i}\|_{3}^{2}+3\sup_{i\in I}\|\mathcal{F}[f_{i}]\mathds{1}_{|\cdot|\geq|k_{0}|/3}\|_{1}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}\wedge\|f_{i}\|_{3}.

Proof.

Using the
m
-linearity of the cumulants we get

\displaystyle\kappa_{m}(\widehat{S}(k_{0}))=\frac{1}{|I|^{m}}\sum_{(i_{1},\dots,i_{m})\in I^{m}}\kappa(|C_{i_{1}}(k_{0})|^{2},\dots,|C_{i_{m}}(k_{0})|^{2}).

Recall that
(C_{i}(k_{0}))_{i\in I}
are defined in (
9
). Then, using Corollary
G.1
and arguing as in the proof of Lemma
A.1
, we obtain

\displaystyle\kappa(|C_{i_{1}}(k_{0})|^{2},\dots,|C_{i_{m}}(k_{0})|^{2})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\sigma\in\Pi_{2}[2m],\\
\sigma\vee\tau=\mathbf{1}_{2m}\end{subarray}}\prod_{b\in\sigma}\kappa(E_{i_{j}}(k_{0});j\in b),

(45)

where
\Pi_{2}[2m]
denotes the sets of partitions of
[2m]
having only blocks of size larger than
2
,
E_{i_{2p-1}}(k_{0})=T_{i_{p}}(k_{0})
and
E_{i_{2p}}(k_{0})=\overline{T_{i_{p}}(k_{0})}
for
p\in[m]
, and
\tau=12|\dots|(2m-1)2m.

We bound the cumulants corresponding to the blocks of size larger than
3
using Lemma
D.2
:

\displaystyle\Big|\kappa(E_{i_{j}}(

\displaystyle k_{0});j\in b)\Big|\leq\prod_{j\in b}\|f_{i_{\lceil j/2\rceil}}\|_{|b|}\sum_{\pi\in\Pi[|b|]}|\gamma_{\text{red}}^{(|\pi|)}|(\mathbb{R}^{d(|\pi|-1)}).

Moreover, when
|b|=3
, Lemma
D.5
yields

\Big|\kappa(E_{i_{j}}(k_{0});j\in b)\Big|\\
\leq 3\sum_{\pi\in\Pi[3]}|\gamma_{\text{red}}^{(|\pi|)}|(\mathbb{R}^{d(|\pi|-1)})\left(\sup_{i\in I}\|f_{i}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus W}\|_{3}\sup_{i\in I}\|f_{i}\|_{3}^{2}+\sup_{i\in I}\|\mathcal{F}[f_{i}]\mathds{1}_{|\cdot|\geq|k_{0}|/3}\|_{1}\right).

Indeed, the sum of frequencies involved in the functions
f_{i}
is of the form

k_{0}\sum_{s=1}^{3}\epsilon_{s},

with
(\epsilon_{s})_{s\in[3]}\in\{-1,1\}^{3}
. Hence
|k_{0}\sum_{s=1}^{3}\epsilon_{s}|\geq|k_{0}|
.
Accordingly,

|\kappa_{m}(|C_{i_{1}}(k_{0},r)|^{2},\dots,|C_{i_{m}}(k_{0})|^{2})|\\
\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\sigma\in\Pi_{2}[2m],\\
\sigma\vee\tau=\mathbf{1}_{2m}\end{subarray}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in\sigma\\
|b|\geq 3\end{subarray}}\prod_{j\in b}a_{|b|}(i_{\lceil j/2\rceil})\sum_{\pi\in\Pi[|b|]}|\gamma_{\text{red}}^{(|\pi|)}|(\mathbb{R}^{d(|\pi|-1)})\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in\sigma\\
|b|=2\end{subarray}}|\kappa(E_{i_{j}}(k_{0});j\in b)|.

We have thus obtained:

\displaystyle|\kappa_{m}(\widehat{S}(k_{0}))|\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\sigma\in\Pi_{2}[2m],\\
\sigma\vee\tau=\mathbf{1}_{2m}\end{subarray}}\left(\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in\sigma\\
|b|\geq 3\end{subarray}}\sum_{\pi\in\Pi[|b|]}|\gamma_{\text{red}}^{(|\pi|)}|(\mathbb{R}^{d(|\pi|-1)})\right)A_{\sigma},

with

\displaystyle A_{\sigma}:=\frac{1}{|I|^{m}}\sum_{(i_{1},\dots,i_{m})\in I^{m}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in\sigma\\
|b|\geq 3\end{subarray}}\prod_{j\in b}a_{|b|}(i_{\lceil j/2\rceil})\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in\sigma\\
|b|=2\end{subarray}}|\kappa(E_{i_{j}}(k_{0});j\in b)|.

To continue the proof, we introduce some notations. Denote by
\mathcal{B}_{2}(\sigma)
the blocks of size
2
of
\sigma
. Let
\varepsilon_{p}=1
if
p
is even and
-1
otherwise. Moreover, denote

\mathcal{B}_{2}^{+}(\sigma):=\{b=(b_{1},b_{2})\in\mathcal{B}_{2}(\sigma)|~\varepsilon_{b_{1}}\varepsilon_{b_{1}}=+1\},

and
\mathcal{B}_{2}^{-}(\sigma):=\mathcal{B}_{2}(\sigma)\setminus\mathcal{B}_{2}^{+}(\sigma).
Finally, for
b=(b_{1},b_{2})\in\mathcal{B}_{2}(\sigma)
, let

\delta_{b}:=\mathds{1}_{i_{\lceil b_{1}/2\rceil}=i_{\lceil b_{2}/2\rceil}}.

We introduce these sets since we anticipate, for
b=(b_{1},b_{2})\in\mathcal{B}_{2}^{+}(\sigma)
,

\displaystyle\kappa(E_{i_{j}}(k_{0});j\in b)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathcal{F}[f_{i_{\lceil\frac{b_{1}}{2}\rceil}}\mathds{1}_{W}](k+\varepsilon_{b_{1}}k_{0})\overline{\mathcal{F}[f_{i_{\lceil\frac{b_{2}}{2}\rceil}}\mathds{1}_{W}]}(k+\varepsilon_{b_{2}}k_{0})S(k)dk

\displaystyle\simeq S(k_{0})\langle f_{i_{\lceil\frac{b_{1}}{2}\rceil}},f_{i_{\lceil\frac{b_{2}}{2}\rceil}}\rangle=S(k_{0})\delta_{b},

whereas for
b=(b_{1},b_{2})\in\mathcal{B}_{2}^{-}(\sigma)
, with
b_{1}
even and
b_{2}
odd,

\displaystyle\kappa(E_{i_{j}}(k_{0});j\in b)\simeq S(k_{0})\langle\mathcal{F}[f_{i_{\lceil\frac{b_{1}}{2}\rceil}}](\cdot+k_{0}),\mathcal{F}[f_{i_{\lceil\frac{b_{2}}{2}\rceil}}](\cdot-k_{0})\rangle.

To take into account this previous heuristic, we denote, for
b\in\mathcal{B}_{2}^{+}(\sigma)
,

\Delta_{b}:=\kappa(E_{i_{j}}(k_{0});j\in b)-S(k_{0})\delta_{b}.

In the term
A_{\sigma}
, we expand the product over the blocks of size two of
\sigma
as follows:

\displaystyle\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in\sigma\\
|b|=2\end{subarray}}|\kappa(E_{i_{j}}(k_{0});j\in b)|

\displaystyle=\prod_{b\in\mathcal{B}_{2}^{-}(\sigma)}|\kappa(E_{i_{j}}(k_{0});j\in b)|\prod_{b\in\mathcal{B}_{2}^{+}(\sigma)}\left(S(k_{0})\delta_{b}+\Delta_{b}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{P\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{B}_{2}^{+}(\sigma))}\prod_{b\in\mathcal{B}_{2}^{-}(\sigma)}|\kappa(E_{i_{j}}(k_{0});j\in b)|\prod_{b\in P}S(k)\delta_{b}\prod_{b\in P^{c}}\Delta_{b},

where
\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{B}_{2}^{+}(\sigma))
denote the subsets of
\mathcal{B}_{2}^{+}(\sigma)
.
This yields:

A_{\sigma}=\frac{1}{|I|^{m}}\sum_{P\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{B}_{2}^{+}(\sigma))}S(k_{0})^{|P|}B_{\sigma,P},

where

B_{\sigma,P}:=\sum_{(i_{1},\dots,i_{m})\in I^{m}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in\sigma\\
|b|\geq 3\end{subarray}}\prod_{j\in b}a_{|b|}(i_{\lceil j/2\rceil})\prod_{b\in\mathcal{B}_{2}^{-}(\sigma)}|\kappa(E_{i_{j}}(k_{0});j\in b)|\prod_{b\in P}\delta_{b}\prod_{b\in P^{c}}\Delta_{b},

where we recall that
\delta_{b}
and
\Delta_{b}
depend on indexes
i_{1},\dots,i_{m}
. We distinguish several cases. Assume that
\sigma
contains only blocks of size
2
and that
|P|=m
. In that case, according to Lemma
B.2
,

B_{\sigma,P}=|I|.

Otherwise, Lemma
B.2
yields:

\displaystyle B_{\sigma,P}\leq\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in\sigma\\
|b|\geq 3\end{subarray}}\left(\sum_{i\in I}a_{|b|}(i)^{2}\right)^{\frac{|b|}{2}}\left(\sum_{(i,i^{\prime})\in I^{2}}|a_{2}^{-}(i,i^{\prime})|^{2}\right)^{\frac{|\mathcal{B}_{2}^{-}(\sigma)|}{2}}\left(\sum_{(i,i^{\prime})\in I^{2}}|a_{2}^{+}(i,i^{\prime})|^{2}\right)^{\frac{|P^{c}|}{2}},

where

\displaystyle a_{2}^{-}(i,i^{\prime}):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathcal{F}[f_{i}\mathds{1}_{W}](k+k_{0})\overline{\mathcal{F}[f_{i^{\prime}}\mathds{1}_{W}]}(k-k_{0})S(k)dk,

\displaystyle a_{2}^{+}(i,i^{\prime}):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathcal{F}[f_{i}\mathds{1}_{W}](k+k_{0})\overline{\mathcal{F}[f_{i^{\prime}}\mathds{1}_{W}]}(k+k_{0})S(k)dk-S(k_{0})\mathds{1}_{i=i^{\prime}}.

Note that, using
S(\cdot)=S(-\cdot)
and changes of variable, we have
|a_{2}^{\pm}(i,i^{\prime})|=|a_{2}^{\pm}(i^{\prime},i)|
. If
\sigma
contains at least one block of size larger than three, we have, using the binomial theorem:

\displaystyle A_{\sigma}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{|I|^{m}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in\sigma\\
|b|\geq 3\end{subarray}}\left(\sum_{i\in I}a_{|b|}(i)^{2}\right)^{\frac{|b|}{2}}\left(\sum_{(i,i^{\prime})\in I^{2}}|a_{2}^{-}(i,i^{\prime})|^{2}\right)^{\frac{|\mathcal{B}_{2}^{-}(\sigma)|}{2}}\left(S(k_{0})+\left(\sum_{(i,i^{\prime})\in I^{2}}|a_{2}^{+}(i,i^{\prime})|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)^{|\mathcal{B}_{2}^{+}(\sigma)|}

\displaystyle\leq\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in\sigma\\
|b|\geq 3\end{subarray}}\left(\frac{1}{|I|}\sum_{i\in I}a_{|b|}(i)^{2}\right)^{\frac{|b|}{2}}\prod_{b\in\mathcal{B}_{2}(\sigma)}\Sigma_{0},

where

\displaystyle\Sigma_{0}:=\frac{S(k_{0})}{|I|}+\frac{1}{|I|}\left(\sum_{(i,i^{\prime})\in I^{2}}|a_{2}^{+}(i,i^{\prime})|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\frac{1}{|I|}\left(\sum_{(i,i^{\prime})\in I^{2}}|a_{2}^{-}(i,i^{\prime})|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Indeed, for the term
|I|^{m}
, we use the fact that

2m=2|\mathcal{B}_{2}(\sigma)|+\sum_{b\in\sigma,~|b|\geq 3}|b|.

If
\sigma
contains only blocks of size
2
, we obtain:

\displaystyle A_{\sigma}\leq\frac{S(k_{0})^{m}}{|I|^{m-1}}+\frac{1}{|I|^{m}}\left(\sum_{(i,i^{\prime})\in I^{2}}|a_{2}^{-}(i,i^{\prime})|^{2}\right)^{\frac{|\mathcal{B}_{2}^{-}(\sigma)|}{2}}\sum_{P\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{B}_{2}^{+}(\sigma)),|P|<m}S(k_{0})^{|P|}\left(\sum_{(i,i^{\prime})\in I^{2}}|a_{2}^{+}(i,i^{\prime})|^{2}\right)^{\frac{|P^{c}|}{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{S(k_{0})^{m}}{|I|^{m-1}}+\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in\sigma\\
|b|\geq 3\end{subarray}}\left(\frac{1}{|I|}\sum_{i\in I}a_{|b|}(i)^{2}\right)^{\frac{|b|}{2}}\prod_{b\in\mathcal{B}_{2}(\sigma)}\Sigma_{0}.

Gathering both cases and using the fact that
|\{\sigma\in\Pi[2m]|~|\sigma|=m,~\sigma\vee\tau=\mathbf{1}_{2m}\}|=2^{m-1}(m-1)!
(exercise 3.5 page 92 of
[
54
]
), we get

|\kappa_{m}(\widehat{S}(k_{0}))|\leq 2^{m-1}(m-1)!\frac{S(k_{0})^{m}}{|I|^{m-1}}\\
+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\sigma\in\Pi_{2}[2m],\\
\sigma\vee\tau=\mathbf{1}_{2m}\end{subarray}}\left(\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in\sigma\\
|b|\geq 3\end{subarray}}\sum_{\pi\in\Pi[|b|]}|\gamma_{\text{red}}^{(|\pi|)}|(\mathbb{R}^{d(|\pi|-1)})\right)\left(\frac{1}{|I|}\sum_{i\in I}a_{|b|}(i)^{2}\right)^{\frac{|b|}{2}}\prod_{b\in\mathcal{B}_{2}(\sigma)}\Sigma_{0},

where in the second term we use the fact that
\mathds{1}_{|\sigma|=m}+\mathds{1}_{|\sigma|<m}=1
. The end of the proof consists in bounding
\Sigma_{0}
by
\Sigma
(see (
18
)). According to Lemma
D.4
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|I|}\left(\sum_{(i,i^{\prime})\in I^{2}}|a_{2}^{-}(i,i^{\prime})|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq 2\|S\|_{\infty}\left(\left(\frac{1}{|I|}\sum_{i\in I}\|f_{i}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus W}\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\left(\frac{1}{|I|}\sum_{i\in I}\|\mathcal{F}[f_{i}]\mathds{1}_{|\cdot|\geq|k_{0}|}\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right).

We also have, according to Lemma
D.3
.

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|I|}\left(\sum_{(i,i^{\prime})\in I^{2}}|a_{2}^{-}(i,i^{\prime})|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{\|S\|_{\infty}}{|I|^{\frac{1}{2}}}+\|S\|_{\infty}\left(\frac{1}{|I|}\sum_{i\in I}\|f_{i}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus W}\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

For the sum related to
a_{2}^{+}
, we obtain, using the triangular inequality, the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, the Plancherel theorem and the fact that
(f_{i})_{i\in I}
is orthogonal:

\displaystyle|a_{2}^{+}(i,i^{\prime})|\leq\|S\|_{\infty}\left(\|f_{i}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus W}\|_{2}+\|f_{i^{\prime}}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus W}\|_{2}\right)+|\langle\mathcal{F}[f_{i}],\mathcal{F}[f_{i^{\prime}}](S(\cdot-k_{0})-S(k_{0}))\rangle|.

Assume that
\beta\leq 1
. Then, using again the fact that
(f_{i})_{i\in I}
is orthogonal we have:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|I|}\left(\sum_{(i,i^{\prime})\in I^{2}}|a_{2}^{+}(i,i^{\prime})|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq L\left(\frac{1}{|I|^{2}}\sum_{i\in I}\|f_{i}\|_{\dot{H}^{\beta}}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+2\|S\|_{\infty}\left(\frac{1}{|I|}\sum_{i\in I}\|f_{i}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus W}\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Assume that
\beta\in(1,2]
. In that case
\mathcal{F}[f_{i}]\mathcal{F}[f_{i^{\prime}}]
is even. Hence
\langle\mathcal{F}[f_{i}],\mathcal{F}[f_{i^{\prime}}]\nabla S(k_{0})\cdot k\rangle=0
. Accordingly, arguing as in the proof of Lemma
A.2
, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|I|}\left(\sum_{(i,i^{\prime})\in I^{2}}|a_{2}^{+}(i,i^{\prime})|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\sqrt{d}L\left(\frac{1}{|I|^{2}}\sum_{i\in I}\|f_{i}\|_{\dot{H}^{\beta}}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+2\|S\|_{\infty}\left(\frac{1}{|I|}\sum_{i\in I}\|f_{i}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus W}\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

The previous bounds yields
\Sigma_{0}\leq\Sigma
, which concludes the proof.
∎

The next lemma exploits the connectivity condition of partitions arising in the computation of joint cumulants of the squares of the linear statistics associated with the tapers. This condition allows for combinatorial simplifications due to the orthogonality of the tapers. The key point is to provide a bound involving the sums over indices
i,i^{\prime}\in I
of the sequences
(a_{2}^{b}(i,i^{\prime}))_{b\in\mathcal{B}_{2}(\sigma)}
. Indeed,
a_{2}^{b}(i,i^{\prime})
typically corresponds to a scalar product between the taper
f_{i}
and a function depending on
b
. In this context, Bessel’s inequality yields a sharper bound on
\sum_{(i,i^{\prime})\in I^{2}}|a_{2}^{b}(i,i^{\prime})|^{2}
than the trivial bound
\sum_{(i,i^{\prime})\in I^{2}}|a_{2}^{b}(i,i^{\prime})|^{2}\leq|I|^{2}\sup_{(i,i^{\prime})}|a_{2}^{b}(i,i^{\prime})|^{2}
, for which the use of the combinatorial structure would not have been necessary. However, this refinement is essential to show that the non-asymptotic correlation term
a_{2}
in Theorem
5.2
is negligible at sufficiently large frequencies.

Lemma B.2
.

Let
I
be a discrete subset of
\mathbb{N}^{d}
. Let
m\geq 2
and
\sigma
be a partition of
[2m]
having block of size at least
2
. Assume that
\sigma\vee\tau=\mathbf{1}_{m}
, where
\tau=12|\dots|(2m-1)2m
. Denote by
\mathcal{B}_{2}(\sigma)
the blocks of size
2
of
\sigma
. For a partition
b\in\mathcal{B}_{2}(\sigma)
, we denote
b_{1}<b_{2}
such that
b=(b_{1},b_{2})
.
Let
s\leq m
. Consider
P
a subset of cardinal
s
of
\mathcal{B}_{2}(\sigma)
.
Finally consider non-negative numbers
(a_{n}(i))_{n\geq 3,i\in I}
and
(a_{2}^{b}(i,i^{\prime}))_{b\in\mathcal{B}_{2}(\sigma),(i,i^{\prime})\in I^{2}}
.
Denote:

B_{\sigma,P}:=\sum_{(i_{1},\dots,i_{m})\in I^{m}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in\sigma\\
|b|\geq 3\end{subarray}}\prod_{j\in b}a_{|b|}(i_{\lceil j/2\rceil})\prod_{b\in\mathcal{B}_{2}(\sigma)\setminus P}a_{2}^{b}(i_{\lceil b_{1}/2\rceil},i_{\lceil b_{2}/2\rceil})\prod_{b\in P}\mathds{1}_{i_{\lceil b_{1}/2\rceil}=i_{\lceil b_{2}/2\rceil}}.

Then,
B_{\sigma,P}=|I|
if
|\sigma|=m=s
. Otherwise, we have

B_{\sigma,P}\leq\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in\sigma\\
|b|\geq 3\end{subarray}}\left(\sum_{i\in I}|a_{|b|}(i)|^{2}\right)^{\frac{|b|}{2}}\prod_{b\in\mathcal{B}_{2}(\sigma)\setminus P}\left(\sum_{(i,i^{\prime})\in I^{2}}|a_{2}^{b}(i,i^{\prime})|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(46)

Proof.

For the proof, we introduce graphs to account for the connectivity condition
\sigma\vee\tau=\mathbf{1}_{[2m]}
. Let
G=G(\sigma)
be an undirected graph whose vertices are the union of the blocks of
\sigma
and the blocks of
\tau
. An edge is drawn between two vertices
b
and
b^{\prime}
if
b\cap b^{\prime}\neq\emptyset
. The condition
\sigma\vee\tau=\mathbf{1}_{[2m]}
is equivalent to
G
being connected, that is, having a single connected component (see Section 3.2 of
[
54
]
).

For a vertex
v=(v_{1},\dots,v_{|v|})
in
G
, we call
v_{1},\dots,v_{|v|}
the labels of
v
. We now define
G_{2}
as a graph having the same edges as
G
, but where each label
n
appearing in the blocks of
G
is replaced by
\lceil n/2\rceil
. Since the vertices of
G
corresponding to blocks of the partition
\tau
yield in
G_{2}
vertices where the two labels are equal, we remove such vertices from
G_{2}
.

Note that the labels of
G_{2}
belong to
\{1,\dots,m\}
. Moreover, for any vertex
v
of
G_{2}
with only two labels
v_{1}
and
v_{2}
, we necessarily have
v_{1}\neq v_{2}
. To see this, assume the contrary. If
v_{1}=v_{2}
, then
v
comes from a vertex of
G
of the form
(2n-1,2n)
for some
n\in[m]
, corresponding to a block of
\sigma
. In that case, the block
(2n-1,2n)
is only connected to the vertex of
G
corresponding to the block
(2n-1,2n)
of
\tau
, and conversely. Therefore,
G
would not be connected, which contradicts the assumption.

The graph
G_{2}
satisfies two key properties:
G_{2}
is connected (since
G
is connected) and each number
n\in[m]
appears exactly twice in the labels of
G_{2}
.

For the following, we introduce additional notations. Consider a graph
G_{0}
whose labels belong to
[m]
. Denote by
\mathcal{V}_{2}(G_{0})
the set of vertices of
G_{0}
with exactly two labels, and by
\mathcal{V}_{3}(G_{0})
the set of vertices of
G_{0}
with more than three labels. We also denote by
P_{\mathcal{V}}(G_{0})
the (possibly empty) set of vertices of
G_{0}
corresponding to the blocks of
\sigma
belonging to
P
.

In the following, products of the form
\prod_{j\in v}
, where
v\in\mathcal{V}_{2}(G_{0})\cup\mathcal{V}_{3}(G_{0})
, iterate over the labels of the vertex
v
. We denote by
\mathcal{L}(G_{0})
the set of distinct labels of
G_{0}
(without repetition). Finally, for a vertex
v
of
G_{0}
with exactly two labels, we write
v=(v_{1},v_{2})
with
v_{1}<v_{2}
. With the previous notations, we can rewrite
B_{\sigma,P}
as

B_{\sigma,P}:=\sum_{i_{l}\in I;l\in\mathcal{L}(G_{2})}\prod_{v\in\mathcal{V}_{3}(G_{2})}\prod_{j\in v}a_{|v|}(i_{j})\prod_{v\in\mathcal{V}_{2}(G_{2})\setminus P_{\mathcal{V}}(G_{2})}a_{2}^{v}(i_{v_{1}},i_{v_{2}})\prod_{v\in P_{\mathcal{V}}(G_{2})}\mathds{1}_{i_{v_{1}}=i_{v_{2}}}.

Consider the case where
\sigma
contains at least one block of size larger than three, i.e.
|\sigma|<m
.

We proceed inductively. If
P_{\mathcal{V}}
is empty, we do nothing. Otherwise, we take into account one constraint
\mathds{1}_{i_{v_{1}}=i_{v_{2}}}
in the sum (for instance, the one such that
(v_{1},v_{2})
is the smallest for the lexicographical order). Specifically, we remove the vertex
v=(v_{1},v_{2})
in
G_{2}
and change the value of the label
v_{1}
appearing in the other vertex
v^{\prime}
of
G_{2}
by
v_{2}
. We obtain a new graph
G_{2}^{\prime}
. Recall that every label appears only twice in
G_{2}
, so
v_{1}
appears in only one block distinct of
(v_{1},v_{2})
). The case
v^{\prime}=(v_{2},v_{1})
is impossible since
\sigma
contains at least one block of size larger than three. Indeed, otherwise
v,v^{\prime}
would be a connected component of
G_{2}
, distinct from
G_{2}
, which contradicts the fact that
G_{2}
is connected. Hence,
v
was connected to two (and only two) distinct vertices
v^{\prime}
and
v^{\prime\prime}
. We add in
G_{2}^{\prime}
an edge between
v^{\prime}
and
v^{\prime\prime}
.

With that procedure,
G_{2}^{\prime}
remains connected. Moreover,
G_{2}^{\prime}
has one fewer block than
G_{2}
and contains the labels of
G_{2}
, except for
v_{1}
. Since we removed and added once the label
v_{2}
, every label in
G_{2}^{\prime}
appears exactly twice. Finally, we have
|P_{\mathcal{V}}(G_{2}^{\prime})|=|P_{\mathcal{V}}(G_{2})|-1
. The previous discussion yields

B_{\sigma,P}=\sum_{i_{l}\in I,\ l\in\mathcal{L}(G_{2}^{\prime})}\prod_{v\in\mathcal{V}_{3}(G_{2}^{\prime})}\prod_{j\in v}a_{|v|}(i_{j})\prod_{v\in\mathcal{V}_{2}(G_{2}^{\prime})\setminus P_{\mathcal{V}}(G_{2}^{\prime})}a_{2}^{v}(i_{v_{1}},i_{v_{2}})\prod_{v\in P_{\mathcal{V}}(G_{2}^{\prime})}\mathds{1}_{i_{v_{1}}=i_{v_{2}}}.

Note that the number of terms in the product over
\mathcal{V}_{2}(G_{2}^{\prime})\setminus P_{\mathcal{V}}(G_{2}^{\prime})
is unchanged and still equals
|\mathcal{B}_{2}(\sigma)|-s
. Since
G_{2}^{\prime}
satisfies the same properties as
G_{2}
that we used previously, we can iterate this procedure to obtain

B_{\sigma,P}=\sum_{i_{l}\in I,\ l\in\mathcal{L}(G_{2}^{s})}\prod_{v\in\mathcal{V}_{3}(G_{2}^{s})}\prod_{j\in v}a_{|v|}(i_{j})\prod_{v\in\mathcal{V}_{2}(G_{2}^{s})\setminus P_{\mathcal{V}}(G_{2}^{s})}a_{2}^{v}(i_{v_{1}},i_{v_{2}}),

where
G_{2}^{s}
is a graph having
m-s
labels, each appearing exactly twice.
To upper bound the last quantity, we establish the following intermediate result. Let
N\geq 2
and
(c^{n}(i,i^{\prime}))_{n\leq\mathbb{N},(i,i^{\prime})\in I^{2}}
a sequence of non-negative numbers. Then

\displaystyle\sum_{(i_{1},\dots,i_{N})\in I^{N}}\prod_{n=1}^{N}c^{n}(i_{n},i_{n+1})\leq\prod_{n=1}^{N}\left(\sum_{(i,i^{\prime})\in I^{2}}c^{n}(i,i^{\prime})^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},

(47)

with the convention
i_{N+1}=i_{1}
. We prove it by induction. For
N=2
, it follows from the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality:

\displaystyle\sum_{i_{1},i_{2}\in I^{N}}c^{1}(i_{1},i_{2})c^{2}(i_{2},i_{1})

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i_{1}\in I}\left(\sum_{i^{\prime}\in I}c^{1}(i_{1},i^{\prime})^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\sum_{i\in I}c^{2}(i,i_{1})^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\sum_{(i,i^{\prime})\in I^{2}}c^{1}(i,i^{\prime})^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\sum_{(i,i^{\prime})\in I^{2}}c^{2}(i,i^{\prime})^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Fix
N\geq 2
. Assume that the inequality (
47
) is true when the product of its left-hand side is over
N
terms. Then, by induction we have

\displaystyle\sum_{(i_{1},\dots,i_{N+1})\in I^{N+1}}\prod_{n=1}^{N+1}c^{n}(i_{n},i_{n+1})=\sum_{(i_{1},\dots,i_{N})\in I^{N}}\prod_{n=1}^{N-1}c^{n}(i_{n},i_{n+1})\left(\sum_{i_{N+1}\in I}c^{N}(i_{N},i_{N+1})c^{N+1}(i_{N+1},i_{1})\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\prod_{n=1}^{N-1}\left(\sum_{(i,i^{\prime})\in I^{2}}c^{n}(i,i^{\prime})^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\sum_{(i,i^{\prime})\in I^{2}}\left(\sum_{i_{N+1}\in I}c^{N}(i,i_{N+1})c^{N+1}(i_{N+1},i^{\prime})\right)^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\prod_{n=1}^{N+1}\left(\sum_{(i,i^{\prime})\in I^{2}}c^{n}(i,i^{\prime})^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},

which proves (
47
).

We now have the tools to upper bound
B_{\sigma,P}
. We introduce

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{3}:=\{l\in\mathcal{L}(G_{2}^{s})|~l\text{ appears only in vertices having more than 3 labels}\}.

Then,
B_{\sigma,P}=B_{\sigma,P}^{3}B_{\sigma,P}^{23}
where

\displaystyle B_{\sigma,P}^{3}:=\sum_{i_{l};l\in\mathcal{L}_{3}}\prod_{v\in\mathcal{V}_{3}(G_{2}^{s})}\prod_{j\in v}a_{|b|}(i_{j})^{\mathds{1}_{j\in\mathcal{L}_{3}}},

\displaystyle B_{\sigma,P}^{23}:=\sum_{i_{l};l\in\mathcal{L}\setminus\mathcal{L}_{3}}\prod_{v\in\mathcal{V}_{3}(G_{2}^{s})}\prod_{j\in v}a_{|v|}(i_{j})^{\mathds{1}_{j\in\mathcal{L}\setminus\mathcal{L}_{3}}}\prod_{v\in\mathcal{V}_{2}(G_{2}^{s})\setminus P_{\mathcal{V}}(G_{2}^{s})}a_{2}^{v}(i_{v_{1}},i_{v_{2}}),

We now bound
B_{\sigma,P}^{3}
. For each label
l\in\mathcal{L}_{3}
, denote by
v(l)
and
v^{\prime}(l)
the (possibly identical) vertices of size larger than three in which
l
appears. Using the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality, we get:

\displaystyle B_{\sigma,P}^{3}=\prod_{l\in\mathcal{L}_{3}}\sum_{i\in I}a_{|v(l)|}(i)\,a_{|v^{\prime}(l)|}(i)\leq\prod_{l\in\mathcal{L}_{3}}\left(\sum_{i\in I}|a_{|v(l)|}(i)|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\sum_{i\in I}|a_{|v^{\prime}(l)|}(i)|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Next, consider the following product:

p:=\prod_{v\in\mathcal{V}_{3}(G_{2}^{s})}\prod_{j\in v}a_{|v|}(i_{j})^{\mathds{1}_{j\in\mathcal{L}\setminus\mathcal{L}_{3}}}.

The number of terms in
p
is equal to
2m-2|\mathcal{B}_{2}(\sigma)|-2|\mathcal{L}_{3}|
. Since this number is even, we can group the terms of
p
two by two. Moreover, because each label appearing in
p
appears exactly once, we can perform this grouping such that each pair contains distinct labels.

Using the fact that each label of
G_{2}^{s}
appears exactly twice, we can rewrite
B_{\sigma,P}
in the form of the left-hand side of (
47
). Applying this result yields:

\displaystyle B_{\sigma,P}^{23}\leq\prod_{l\in\mathcal{L}_{23}}\left(\sum_{i\in I}|a_{|v_{3}(l)|}(i)|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\prod_{v\in\mathcal{V}_{2}(G_{2}^{s})\setminus P_{\mathcal{V}}(G_{2}^{s})}\left(\sum_{(i,i^{\prime})\in I^{2}}a_{2}^{v}(i,i^{\prime})^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},

(48)

where

\mathcal{L}_{23}:=\left\{l\in\mathcal{L}(G_{2}^{s})\mid l\text{ appears only in one vertex of size }2\right\},

and for each
l\in\mathcal{L}_{23}
,
v_{3}(l)
denotes the vertex of size larger than three in which it appears. Note that, to obtain (
48
), we used, after applying (
47
), the fact that for vertices of size larger than three:

\sum_{(i^{\prime},i)\in I^{2}}\left(a_{n}(i)^{2}\,a_{n^{\prime}}(i^{\prime})\right)^{2}=\left(\sum_{i\in I}a_{n}(i)^{2}\right)\left(\sum_{i^{\prime}\in I}a_{n^{\prime}}(i^{\prime})^{2}\right),\quad\text{for}\ n,n^{\prime}\geq 3,

together with the fact that the grouping of terms in
p
is performed so that each pair contains distinct labels.

Since the effect on
\mathcal{V}_{2}(G_{2})\setminus\mathcal{P}(G_{2})
and
\mathcal{V}_{3}(G_{2})
of the procedure to go from
G_{2}
to
G_{2}^{s}
was only to change their labels, we have

\prod_{v\in\mathcal{V}_{2}(G_{2}^{s})\setminus P_{\mathcal{V}}(G_{2}^{s})}\left(\sum_{(i,i^{\prime})\in I^{2}}a_{2}^{v}(i,i^{\prime})^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}=\prod_{b\in\mathcal{B}_{2}(\sigma)}\left(\sum_{(i,i^{\prime})\in I^{2}}a_{2}^{b}(i,i^{\prime})^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},

and

\prod_{l\in\mathcal{L}_{3}}\left(\sum_{i\in I}|a_{|v(l)|}(i)|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\sum_{i\in I}|a_{|v^{\prime}(l)|}(i)|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\prod_{l\in\mathcal{L}_{23}}\left(\sum_{i\in I}|a_{|v_{3}(l)|}(i)|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
=\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in\sigma;\\
|b|\geq 3\end{subarray}}\prod_{j\in b}\left(\sum_{i\in I}a_{|b|}(i)^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}=\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in\sigma;\\
|b|\geq 3\end{subarray}}\left(\sum_{i\in I}a_{|b|}(i)^{2}\right)^{\frac{|b|}{2}}.

It yields (
46
) when
|\sigma|<m
.

Now, assume that
\sigma
contains only blocks of size two, i.e.
|\sigma|=m
. Then,

B_{\sigma,P}=\sum_{i_{l}\in I;l\in\mathcal{L}(G_{2})}\prod_{v\in\mathcal{V}_{2}(G_{2})\setminus P_{\mathcal{V}}(G_{2})}a_{2}^{v}(i_{v_{1}},i_{v_{2}})\prod_{v\in P_{\mathcal{V}}(G_{2})}\mathds{1}_{i_{v_{1}}=i_{v_{2}}}.

Using similar connectivity arguments as in the case
|\sigma|<m
, we reduce by
s
the dimension
|\mathcal{L}(G_{2})|
of the sum over the indices
i_{l}
for
l\in\mathcal{L}(G_{2})
, by introducing a sequence of graphs
G_{2}^{2},\dots,G_{2}^{s}
, provided that
G_{2}^{2},\dots,G_{2}^{s-1}
do not reduce to two vertices. This situation cannot occur if
s\leq m-1
, since only one vertex is removed at each step and we start with a graph having
m
vertices. Accordingly, if
s\leq m-1
, using (
47
), we obtain (
46
), as in the case
|\sigma|=m
. Finally, when
m=s
, the term
B_{\sigma,P}
reduces to

B_{\sigma,P}=\sum_{i_{l}\in I,;l\in\mathcal{L}(G_{2})}\prod_{v\in G_{2}}\mathds{1}_{i_{v_{1}}=i_{v_{2}}}.

Reducing the dimension of the sum by
s-1
, using the previously mentioned method, yields:

B_{\sigma,P}=\sum_{i_{1}\in I,i_{2}\in I}\mathds{1}_{i_{1}=i_{2}}\mathds{1}_{i_{2}=i_{1}}=|I|.

This concludes the proof of the lemma.

∎

The next lemma shows that the Brillinger-mixing assumption on the total variation of reduced factorial cumulant measures extends to the total variation of the cumulant measures of linear statistics.This result is specific to point processes, where the discrete nature of the linear statistics implies that their cumulants of order
m\geq 2
involve factorial cumulants of orders less than or equal to
m
.

Lemma B.3
.

Let
s\geq 1
and
q,\gamma>0
. Then

\displaystyle\sum_{\pi\in\Pi[s]}q^{|\pi|-1}((|\pi|-1)!)^{1+\gamma}\leq 2^{s}(1+q)^{s-1}((s-1)!)^{1+\gamma}.

Proof.

We denote by
S(s,l)
the Stirling number of the second kind. We have

\displaystyle\sum_{\pi\in\Pi[s]}q^{|\pi|-1}((|\pi|-1)!)^{1+\gamma}\leq((s-1)!)^{\gamma}\sum_{l=1}^{s}(l-1)!q^{l-1}S(s,l).

According to equation (4.5) of
[
71
]
,
S(s,l)=\sum_{n_{1}+\dots+n_{l}=s}\frac{s!}{n_{1}!\dots n_{l}!l!},

where the sum is over the decompositions of the integer
s
into positive integers. Hence,

\displaystyle\sum_{\pi\in\Pi[s]}q^{|\pi|-1}((|\pi|-1)!)^{1+\gamma}

\displaystyle\leq((s-1)!)^{1+\gamma}s\sum_{l=1}^{s}\frac{1}{l}q^{l-1}\sum_{n_{1}+\dots+n_{l}=s}\frac{1}{n_{1}!\dots n_{l}!}

\displaystyle\leq((s-1)!)^{1+\gamma}s\sum_{l=1}^{s}\frac{1}{l}q^{l-1}\sum_{n_{1}+\dots+n_{l}=s}1=((s-1)!)^{1+\gamma}s\sum_{l=1}^{s}\frac{1}{l}q^{l-1}\binom{s-1}{l-1},

where in the second line we used equation (4.2) of
[
71
]
. To conclude, we use

\displaystyle s\sum_{l=1}^{s}\frac{1}{l}q^{l-1}\binom{s-1}{l-1}=\sum_{l=1}^{s}q^{l-1}\binom{s}{l}\leq 2^{s}(1+q)^{s-1}.

∎

The following lemma controls the cumulants appearing on the right-hand side of (
43
), by introducing suitable random variables: a Gaussian distribution to handle non-asymptotic correlations between tapers in second-order cumulants, and a Gamma distribution to account for higher-order cumulants. Its key idea is to separate second-order contributions from higher-order ones.

Lemma B.4
.

Let
\Phi
satisfying Assumption
3.1
with intensity
\lambda=1
,
I
be a discrete subset of
\mathbb{N}
and
(f_{i})_{i\in I}
be a family of
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Assume that
\Phi
satisfies Assumption
5.1
with parameters
(c,q,\gamma)\in(0,\infty)^{3}
. Assume (
17
). We denote

\mathcal{A}:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\sigma\in\Pi[2m],\\
\sigma\vee\tau=\mathbf{1}_{2m},|\sigma|\geq 2\end{subarray}}\prod_{b\in\sigma}K_{|b|},

(49)

where
(K_{s})_{s\geq 0}
is defined in (
44
). If (
15
) is satisfied with
c_{f}\in(1,\infty)
,
\eta_{1},\eta_{2}\geq 0
and
\rho>0
, then

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{4^{m}m!}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\right)^{\gamma}\left(\frac{m!}{2\sqrt{2\pi}}(4\Sigma)^{m}+\frac{(m!)^{2}}{2\sqrt{2\pi}}\left(32\frac{\Lambda^{2}c^{1/2}(1+q)^{3/2}\log(|I|^{\frac{1}{d}})^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\rho^{d/2}|I|^{2\eta_{1}+\eta_{2}/2}}\right)^{m}\right),

(50)

where
\Lambda
is defined in (
19
). If (
15
) is satisfied with
c_{f}\in(1,\infty)
,
\eta_{1},\eta_{2}\geq 0
and
\rho>0
, then

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{4^{m}m!}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\right)^{\gamma}\left(\frac{m!}{2\sqrt{2\pi}}(4\Sigma)^{m}+\frac{(m!)^{2}}{2\sqrt{2\pi}}\left(32\frac{\lceil c_{f}\rceil^{2}c^{2/3}(1+q)^{4/3}}{\rho^{d/3}|I|^{2\eta_{1}}}\right)^{m}\right).

(51)

Proof.

Using the fact that
\Phi
satisfies Assumption
5.1
, Lemma
B.3
, and the inequality
k_{1}!\dots k_{n}!\leq(k_{1}+\dots+k_{n})!
for all integers
k_{1},\dots,k_{n}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\prod_{|b|\geq 3}\sum_{\pi\in\Pi[|b|]}|\gamma_{\text{red}}^{(|\pi|)}|(\mathbb{R}^{d(|\pi|-1)})

\displaystyle\leq\prod_{|b|\geq 3}c2^{|b|}(1+q)^{|b|-1}((|b|-1)!)^{1+\gamma}

\displaystyle\leq((2m)!)^{\gamma}\prod_{|b|\geq 3}c2^{|b|}(1+q)^{|b|-1}(|b|-1)!.

We define
\kappa_{1}=0,~\kappa_{2}=\Sigma
and

\displaystyle\kappa_{3}=c2^{4}(1+q)^{2}\left(3\left(\sup_{i\in I}\|f_{i}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus W}\|_{3}\sup_{i\in I}\|f_{i}\|_{3}^{2}+\sup_{i\in I}\|\mathcal{F}[f_{i}]\mathds{1}_{|\cdot|\geq|k_{0}|/3}\|_{1}\right)\wedge\frac{c_{f}}{\rho^{d/2}|I|^{3\eta_{1}}}\right),

\displaystyle\forall s\geq 4,~\kappa_{s}=c_{f}\frac{c\rho^{d}\log(|I|^{\frac{1}{d}})}{(1+q)|I|^{\eta_{2}}}\left(2\frac{1+q}{\rho^{\frac{d}{2}}|I|^{\eta_{1}}}\right)^{s}(s-1)!.

Then, using Assumptions (
15
) and (
16
), we get

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}\leq\left(\frac{4^{m}m!}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\right)^{\gamma}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\sigma\in\Pi_{2}[2m],\\
\sigma\vee\tau=\mathbf{1}_{2m}\end{subarray}}\prod_{b\in\sigma}\kappa_{|b|},

(52)

where in the last line we used the fact that for
m\geq 2
,
(2m)!\leq 4^{m}(m!)^{2}/\sqrt{2\pi}
, obtained by Stirling approximation
[
33
]
.

We prove (
50
). To do so, we consider

X=\Sigma^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{N}+2\frac{(c(1+q)^{3})^{1/4}\log(|I|^{\frac{1}{d}})^{\frac{1}{4}}}{\rho^{d/4}|I|^{\eta_{1}+\eta_{2}/4}}\Gamma,

where
\mathcal{N}
is a standard Gaussian random variable and
\Gamma
is an Gamma random variable with parameters

\left(\Lambda,1\right)
. Moreover,
\mathcal{N}
and
\mathcal{E}
are independents. We also denote
\overline{X}=X-\mathbb{E}[X]
. We introduced
\overline{X}
because
\kappa_{s}\leq\kappa_{s}(\overline{X})
, for all
s\geq 1
.
Indeed,
\kappa_{1}=\kappa_{1}(\overline{X})=0
,

\kappa_{2}=\Sigma\leq\Sigma+\Lambda\left(2\frac{(c(1+q)^{3})^{1/4}\log(|I|^{\frac{1}{d}})^{\frac{1}{4}}}{\rho^{d/4}|I|^{\eta_{1}+\eta_{2}/4}}\right)^{2}=\kappa_{2}(\overline{X}).

For
s=3
, we have

\displaystyle\kappa_{3}

\displaystyle=\kappa_{3}\left(\frac{\rho^{d/4}|I|^{\eta_{1}+\eta_{2}/4}}{2(c(1+q)^{3})^{1/4}\log(|I|^{\frac{1}{d}})^{\frac{1}{4}}}\right)^{3}\frac{1}{2}\left(2\frac{(c(1+q)^{3})^{1/4}\log(|I|^{\frac{1}{d}})^{\frac{1}{4}}}{\rho^{d/4}|I|^{\eta_{1}+\eta_{2}/4}}\right)^{3}2!\leq\kappa_{3}(\overline{X}),

where in the second line we used
c\rho^{d}\log(|I|^{\frac{1}{d}})\geq(1+q)|I|^{\eta_{2}}
. Finally, for
s\geq 4
, using again
c\rho^{d}\log(|I|^{\frac{1}{d}})\geq(1+q)|I|^{\eta_{2}}
, we have

\displaystyle\kappa_{s}

\displaystyle=c_{f}\frac{c\rho^{d}\log(|I|^{\frac{1}{d}})}{(1+q)|I|^{\eta_{2}}}\left(2\frac{1+q}{\rho^{\frac{d}{2}}|I|^{\eta_{1}}}\right)^{s}(s-1)!\leq c_{f}\left(\frac{c\rho^{d}\log(|I|^{\frac{1}{d}})}{(1+q)|I|^{\eta_{2}}}\right)^{s/4}\left(2\frac{1+q}{\rho^{\frac{d}{2}}|I|^{\eta_{1}}}\right)^{s}(s-1)!\leq\kappa_{s}(\overline{X}).

Hence, with equation 3.2.6 of
[
61
]
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}\leq\left(\frac{4^{m}m!}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\right)^{\gamma}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\sigma\in\Pi_{2}[2m],\\
\sigma\vee\tau=\mathbf{1}_{2m}\end{subarray}}\prod_{b\in\sigma}\kappa_{s}(\overline{X})\leq\left(\frac{4^{m}m!}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\right)^{\gamma}\mathbb{E}[\overline{X}^{2m}].

(53)

Let
a=\Sigma^{\frac{1}{2}}
and
b=2(c(1+q)^{3})^{1/4}\log(|I|^{\frac{1}{d}})^{\frac{1}{4}}/(\rho^{d/4}|I|^{\eta_{1}+\eta_{2}/4})
. We have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\overline{X}^{2m}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left(a\mathcal{N}+b\left(\Gamma-\mathbb{E}[\Gamma]\right)\right)^{2m}\right]=\sum_{j=0}^{2m}\binom{2m}{j}a^{j}b^{2m-j}\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{N}^{j}]\mathbb{E}[\left(\Gamma-\mathbb{E}[\Gamma]\right)^{2m-j}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{m}\binom{2m}{2j}a^{2j}b^{2m-2j}\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{N}^{2j}]\mathbb{E}[\left(\Gamma-\mathbb{E}[\Gamma]\right)^{2m-2j}].

Let
(\mathcal{E}_{n})_{n\in[\Lambda]}
be i.i.d. standard exponential random variables. Then, for even
j\geq 0
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\left(\Gamma-\mathbb{E}[\Gamma]\right)^{j}]^{1/j}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\sum_{n=1}^{\Lambda}\mathcal{E}_{n}-\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{E}_{n}]\right)^{j}\right]^{1/j}\leq\sum_{n=1}^{\Lambda}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\mathcal{E}_{n}-\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{E}_{n}]\right)^{j}\right]^{1/j}

\displaystyle=\Lambda\left(j!\sum_{n=0}^{j}\frac{(-1)^{n}}{n!}\right)^{1/j}\leq\Lambda\left(j!\right)^{1/j}.

Using the expression of the even moments of the Gaussian distribution, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\overline{X}^{2m}]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=0}^{m}\binom{2m}{2j}a^{2j}b^{2m-2j}\frac{(2j)!}{2^{j}j!}\Lambda^{2m-2j}(2m-2j)!=(2m)!(b\Lambda)^{2m}\sum_{j=0}^{m}\frac{1}{j!}d^{j},

where
d=a^{2}/(2b^{2}\Lambda^{2})
. Using the binomial theorem, we get

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{m}\frac{1}{j!}d^{j}=\frac{1}{m!}\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(d+t\right)^{m}e^{-t}dt\leq\frac{2^{m-1}}{m!}(d^{m}+m!).

Accordingly,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\overline{X}^{2m}]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(2m)!}{m!}2^{m-1}\frac{a^{2m}}{2^{m}}+(2m)!(b\Lambda)^{2m}2^{m-1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{m!}{2\sqrt{2\pi}}(4\Sigma)^{m}+\frac{(m!)^{2}}{2\sqrt{2\pi}}\left(32\frac{\Lambda^{2}c^{1/2}(1+q)^{3/2}\log(|I|^{\frac{1}{d}})^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\rho^{d/2}|I|^{2\eta_{1}+\eta_{2}/2}}\right)^{m},

which concludes the proof of (
50
), recalling (
53
). The proof of (
51
) is done in a similar way, by considering:

X=\Sigma^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{N}+2\frac{(c(1+q)^{2})^{1/3}}{\rho^{d/6}|I|^{\eta_{1}}}\Gamma,

where
\mathcal{N}
is a standard Gaussian random variable and
\Gamma
is an Gamma random variable with parameters

\left(\lceil c_{f}\rceil,1\right)
.
∎

To prove Theorem
5.2
, we combine the previous lemmas with Theorem
G.1
.

Proof of Theorem
5.2
.

According to lemma
B.1
and Lemma
B.4
, we have

\displaystyle|\kappa_{m}(\widehat{S}(k_{0}))|\leq A_{1}+A_{2}+A_{3}\wedge A_{4},

where

\displaystyle A_{1}:=2^{m-1}(m-1)!\frac{S(k_{0})^{m}}{|I|^{m-1}},\quad A_{2}:=\left(\frac{4^{m}m!}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\right)^{\gamma}\frac{m!}{2\sqrt{2\pi}}(4\Sigma)^{m},

\displaystyle A_{3}:=\left(\frac{4^{m}m!}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\right)^{\gamma}\frac{(m!)^{2}}{2\sqrt{2\pi}}\left(32\frac{\Lambda^{2}c^{1/2}(1+q)^{3/2}\log(|I|^{\frac{1}{d}})^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\rho^{d/2}|I|^{2\eta_{1}+\eta_{2}/2}}\right)^{m},

\displaystyle A_{4}:=\left(\frac{4^{m}m!}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\right)^{\gamma}\frac{(m!)^{2}}{2\sqrt{2\pi}}\left(32\frac{\lceil c_{f}\rceil^{2}c^{2/3}(1+q)^{4/3}}{\rho^{d/3}|I|^{2\eta_{1}}}\right)^{m}.

We have, since
m\geq 2
,

\displaystyle A_{1}\leq\frac{m!}{2}\left(\frac{2S(k_{0})}{|I|}\right)^{m-2}\frac{2S(k_{0})^{2}}{|I|}.

Then, after computations, we get

\displaystyle A_{2}\leq\left(\frac{m!}{2}\right)^{1+\gamma}\frac{16}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\left(\frac{32}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\right)^{\gamma}\Sigma^{2}(4^{1+\gamma}\Sigma)^{m-2}.

Finally,

\displaystyle A_{3}\wedge A_{4}\leq\left(\frac{m!}{2}\right)^{2+\gamma}\frac{2^{11}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\left(\frac{32}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\right)^{\gamma}B_{\infty}^{2}(32B_{\infty})^{m-2}.

Theorem
G.1
and numerical computations conclude the proof.
∎

Using the results of Section
F
, we specialize Theorem
5.2
to Hermite tapers.

Proof of Corollary
5.2
.

In the following,
K
denotes a finite constant that may change from line to line. We have:

\displaystyle|\widehat{S}(k_{0})-S(k_{0})|\leq|\widehat{S}(k_{0})-\mathbb{E}[\widehat{S}(k_{0})]|+|S(k_{0})-\mathbb{E}[\widehat{S}(k_{0})]|.

According to Lemma
A.2
and arguing as in the proof of Corollary
5.1
, the bias term is bounded as:

\displaystyle|S(k_{0})-\mathbb{E}[\widehat{S}(k_{0})]|\leq\frac{K}{|W|^{\beta/(2\beta+d)}}.

We consider the variance term
|\widehat{S}(k_{0})-\mathbb{E}[\widehat{S}(k_{0})]|
. According to Lemma
F.3
, Assumptions (
15
) and (
16
) are satisfied with
\rho=r
,
\eta_{1}=1/12
and
\eta_{2}=1/6
. To apply Theorem
5.2
, we check that condition (
17
) is satisfied.

\displaystyle\frac{(1+q)|I|^{\eta_{2}}}{c\rho^{d}\log(|I|^{\frac{1}{d}})}\leq\frac{\sqrt{2}(1+q)|I|^{\frac{2}{3}}}{c\log(2^{\frac{1}{d}})|W|}\leq\frac{\sqrt{2}(1+q)c_{3}^{\frac{2}{3}}}{c\log(2^{\frac{1}{d}})}|W|^{\frac{2}{3}\frac{2\beta}{2\beta+d}-1}=\frac{\sqrt{2}(1+q)c_{3}^{\frac{2}{3}}}{c\log(2^{\frac{1}{d}})}|W|^{-\frac{2\beta+3d}{6\beta+3d}}\leq 1,

provided that
|W|
is large enough, say
|W|\geq v_{0}
for some
v_{0}>0
. Moreover, with similar computations, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1+q}{c\rho^{d}}\leq\sqrt{2}\frac{1+q}{c}|W|^{-\frac{d+\beta}{2\beta+d}}\leq 1,

by increasing
v_{0}
if necessary.
Accordingly, one can apply Theorem
5.2
. It remains to bound
\Sigma
and
B_{\infty}
.With the arguments of the proof of Corollary
5.1
(see Section
A.3
), we obtain

\displaystyle\Sigma

\displaystyle\leq\frac{S(k_{0})}{|I|}+K\left(\frac{L}{|I|^{\frac{1}{2}}}\left(\frac{|I|}{|W|}\right)^{\frac{\beta}{d}}+\|S\|_{\infty}e^{-c^{\prime}|I|^{\frac{3\theta}{2d}}}\right)+2\|S\|_{\infty}\sup_{i\in I}\|\psi_{i}\mathbf{1}_{|\cdot|\geq|k_{0}|}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq K\frac{L+\|S\|_{\infty}}{|W|^{2\beta/(2\beta+d)}}+K\|S\|_{\infty}\left(\sup_{i\in I}\|\psi_{i}\mathbf{1}_{|\cdot|\geq|k_{0}|}\|_{2}\wedge\frac{1}{|W|^{\beta/(2\beta+d)}}\right).

Note that when
\beta\geq 1
, we use the fact that
|I|\geq c_{d}|\{i\in\mathbb{N}^{d}|~|i|_{\infty}<i_{\max}\}|
, for a constant
c_{d}>0
depending only on the dimension
d
. Assume that
|k_{0}|\geq c_{3}^{\frac{1}{d}}2|W|^{-\frac{1}{2\beta+d}}
. Then, using assumptions (
21
) and (
22
), we prove that
r|k_{0}|\geq\sqrt{2}|I|^{1/(2d)}\geq\sqrt{i_{\text{max}}+i_{\text{max}}^{1/3+\theta}}
. So, we can apply Lemma
F.1
, to get

\displaystyle\sup_{i\in I}\|\mathcal{F}[f_{i}]\mathbf{1}_{|\cdot|\geq|k_{0}|}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq Ke^{-c^{\prime}(r|k_{0}|)^{3\theta}}\leq Ke^{-c^{\prime}(\sqrt{2}|I|^{\frac{1}{2d}})^{3\theta}}\leq\frac{K}{|W|^{2\beta/(2\beta+d)}}.

Accordingly, if
|k_{0}|\geq c_{3}^{\frac{1}{d}}2|W|^{-\frac{1}{2\beta+d}}
, then
\Sigma\leq K(L+\|S\|_{\infty})/|W|^{2\beta/(2\beta+d)}.

Without any assumption on
k_{0}
, we have
\Sigma\leq K(L+\|S\|_{\infty})/|W|^{\beta/(2\beta+d)}.

To upper bound
B_{\infty}
, we start with the case
|k_{0}|\geq c_{3}^{\frac{1}{d}}2|W|^{-\frac{1}{2\beta+d}}
. Using again Lemma
F.1
and assumptions (
21
), (
22
), we prove that

\displaystyle\sup_{i\in I}\|\psi_{i}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus W}\|_{3}\sup_{i\in I}\|\psi_{i}\|_{3}^{2}+\sup_{i\in I}\|\psi_{i}\mathds{1}_{|\cdot|\geq|k_{0}|/3}\|_{1}\leq Ke^{-K_{1}|W|^{\alpha}},

for positive constants
\alpha>0
and
K_{1}>0
. Subsequently, with assumptions (
21
) and (
22
), we obtain, for some
\alpha^{\prime}>0
,

\displaystyle\Lambda\leq K\left\lceil c_{f}\vee e^{-K_{1}|W|^{\alpha}}|W|^{\alpha^{\prime}}\right\rceil\leq K.

This yields, using
2\eta_{1}+\eta_{2}/2=1/4
,

\displaystyle B_{\infty}\leq K\frac{c^{1/2}(1+q)^{3/2}\log(|W|)^{\frac{d}{2}}}{\rho^{d/2}|I|^{1/4}}\leq K\frac{c^{1/2}(1+q)^{3/2}\log(|W|)^{\frac{d}{2}}}{|W|^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

Finally, without any assumption on
k_{0}
, we get

\displaystyle B_{\infty}\leq K\frac{c^{\frac{2}{3}}(1+q)^{\frac{4}{3}}}{r^{d/3}|I|^{\frac{1}{6}}}\leq K\frac{c^{\frac{2}{3}}(1+q)^{\frac{4}{3}}}{|W|^{\frac{1}{3}}}.

This concludes the proof of this lemma.
∎

Appendix C
Proofs related to Section
6

To prove Theorem
6.1
, we introduce the following convenient transformation of the data-driven criterion. With the notations of Section
6
and
5
, we consider, for all
I\subset\mathbb{N}^{d}
:

\displaystyle\Delta_{I}:=\widehat{\mathcal{R}}(I)-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\left(\widehat{S}_{I}(k_{j})-S_{\frac{1}{2}}(k_{j})\right)^{2}-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\widehat{S}_{p}(\widetilde{k_{j}})^{2}-2\widehat{S}_{p}(\widetilde{k_{j}})S_{\frac{1}{2}}(\widetilde{k_{j}}).

(54)

For the following proofs, it is convenient to rewrite
\Delta_{I}
as:

\Delta_{I}=\frac{2}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}(\widehat{S}_{I}(k_{j})-S_{\frac{1}{2}}(k_{j}))(S_{\frac{1}{2}}(\widetilde{k_{j}})-\widehat{S}_{p}(\widetilde{k_{j}})),

(55)

provided that
S(k_{j})=S(\widetilde{k_{j}})
for all
j\in[N]
. Theorem
6.1
is a direct consequence of the following lemma.

Lemma C.1
.

Suppose the assumptions of Theorem
6.1
hold. Then,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\max_{I\in\mathcal{I}}|\Delta_{I}|\right]=o\left(|\mathcal{I}|^{\frac{1}{2}}|W|^{-\frac{2\beta}{2\beta+d}}\right),\text{ as }|W|\to\infty.

The proof of the previous lemma is the main objective of this section. Before proceeding with it, we first show how to deduce Theorem
6.1
.

Proof of Theorem
6.1
.

By definition, we have, for all
I\in\mathcal{I}
,

\widehat{\mathcal{R}}(\widehat{I})\leq\widehat{\mathcal{R}}(I).

This yields:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\left(\widehat{S}_{\widehat{I}}(k_{j})-S_{\frac{1}{2}}(k_{j})\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\left(\widehat{S}_{I}(k_{j})-S_{\frac{1}{2}}(k_{j})\right)^{2}+\Delta_{I}-\Delta_{\widehat{I}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\left(\widehat{S}_{I}(k_{j})-S_{\frac{1}{2}}(k_{j})\right)^{2}+2\max_{I^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}}|\Delta_{I^{\prime}}|.

Taking expectations in the previous inequality and applying Lemma
C.1
completes the proof.
∎

To prove Lemma
C.1
, we upper bound both the expectation and the variance of
\Delta_{I}
. We introduce the next notation to highlight the dominant terms in the forthcoming bounds.

Definition 7
.

In this section,
\mathcal{E}(|I|,|I_{p}|)
denotes a non negative exponentially small quantity such that

\mathcal{E}(|I|,|I_{p}|,\theta)\leq K\left(e^{-|I_{p}|^{3\theta/(2d)}/K}+e^{-|I|^{3\theta/(2d)}/K}\right),

for a constant
K\in(0,\infty)
and where
\theta\in(0,2/3)
is the parameter of equation (
27
). When the context is clear, we use the abbreviation
\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{E}(|I|,|I_{p}|,\theta).

The next four lemmas provide upper bounds on the terms appearing in
\mathbb{E}[\Delta_{I}]
and
\operatorname{Var}[\Delta_{I}]
. The following computations rely on the two general underlying ideas, summarized as follows:

1.

In general, cumulants of order
m\geq 4
of linear statistics associated with a family of tapers are controlled by the product of the
L^{m}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
-norms of the tapers (see Lemma
D.2
). However, when one of the Hermite tapers
\psi_{i}
has an index
i
appearing only once in a cumulant, and when we sum the square of this cumulant over
i\in I
, we instead use Lemma
D.6
to exploit the orthogonality of the tapers.

2.

For cumulants of order two or three, two cases are distinguished. If the cumulant involves tapers index
i\in I
and/or
i_{p}\in I_{p}
with
|I|
and
|I_{p}|
small enough we exploit frequency decorrelation via Lemmas
D.4
and
D.5
. Otherwise, we take advantage of the orthogonality between tapers using Lemmas
D.3
and
D.6
.

Lemma C.2
.

Suppose that the assumptions of Theorem
6.1
hold. Then,

\displaystyle\forall I\in\mathcal{I},~|\mathbb{E}[\Delta_{I}]|\leq K\Bigg(\left(\frac{|I||I_{p}|}{|W|^{2}}\right)^{\frac{\beta}{d}}+\frac{\log(|W|)^{d}}{|W|}+\frac{1}{|W||k_{0}|^{d}}+\mathcal{E}\Bigg),

where
K<\infty
does not depend on
I
,
r
,
W
,
N
and
|k|
.

Proof.

According to (
55
),

\Delta_{I}=\frac{2}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}(\widehat{S}_{I}(k_{j})-S_{\frac{1}{2}}(k_{j}))(S_{\frac{1}{2}}(\widetilde{k_{j}})-\widehat{S}_{p}(\widetilde{k_{j}})).

For
s\in\{1,2\}
, denote by
C_{i}^{s}
the quantity of equation (
9
) computed with
\Phi_{s}
. Hence,

\mathbb{E}[\Delta_{I}]=\frac{2}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\frac{1}{|I||I_{p}|}\sum_{(i_{1},i_{2})\in I\times I_{p}}\mathbb{E}[(|C_{i_{1}}^{1}(k_{j})|^{2}-S_{\frac{1}{2}}(k_{j}))(S_{\frac{1}{2}}(\widetilde{k_{j}})-|C_{i_{2}}^{2}(\widetilde{k_{j}})|^{2})].

Using Corollary
G.1
, we get

|\mathbb{E}[\Delta_{I}]|\leq\frac{2}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\frac{1}{|I||I_{p}|}\sum_{(i_{1},i_{2})\in I\times I_{p}}(A+B+C+D),

where

\displaystyle A:=|\mathbb{E}[|C_{i_{1}}^{1}(k_{j})|^{2}]-S_{\frac{1}{2}}(k_{j})||\mathbb{E}[|C_{i_{2}}^{2}(\widetilde{k_{j}})|^{2}]-S_{\frac{1}{2}}(\widetilde{k_{j}})|,

\displaystyle B:=|\mathbb{E}[C_{i_{1}}^{1}(k_{j})C_{i_{2}}^{2}(\widetilde{k_{j}})]|^{2},~C:=|\mathbb{E}[C_{i_{1}}^{1}(k_{j})\overline{C_{i_{2}}^{2}(\widetilde{k_{j}})}]|^{2},

\displaystyle D:=\kappa(C_{i_{1}}^{1}(k_{j}),\overline{C_{i_{1}}^{1}(k_{j})},C_{i_{2}}^{2}(\widetilde{k_{j}}),\overline{C_{i_{2}}^{2}(\widetilde{k_{j}})}).

During the proof, we denote by
K
a finite constant that may change from line to line. We start by bounding the sum concerning
A
. According to Lemmas
A.2
,
F.2
and
F.1
, we have:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|I||I_{p}|}

\displaystyle\sum_{(i_{1},i_{2})\in I\times I_{p}}A\leq\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\rho,J)\in\\
\{(r,I),(r_{p},I_{p})\}\end{subarray}}\left(\frac{\sqrt{d}L}{2|J|\rho^{\beta}}\sum_{i\in J}\|\psi_{i}\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{\beta}{2}}}^{2}+\frac{4}{|J|}\sum_{i\in J}\|\psi_{i}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus W/\rho}\|_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq K\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\rho,J)\in\\
\{(r,I),(r_{p},I_{p})\}\end{subarray}}\left(\left(\frac{|J|^{\frac{1}{2d}}}{\rho}\right)^{\beta}+e^{-c^{\prime}(R/\rho)^{3\theta}}\right).

In the second line we used assumption (
27
). Using again this assumption and the fact that
|J|\leq|W|
, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|I||I_{p}|}\sum_{(i_{1},i_{2})\in I\times I_{p}}A\leq K\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\rho,J)\in\\
\{(r,I),(r_{p},I_{p})\}\end{subarray}}\left(\left(\frac{|J|}{|W|}\right)^{\frac{\beta}{d}}+\mathcal{E}\right)\leq K\left(\frac{(|I||I_{p}|)^{\frac{\beta}{d}}}{|W|^{2\frac{\beta}{d}}}+\mathcal{E}\right).

(56)

Now, we consider
B
and
C
. The idea is to exploit the exponential decorrelation between linear statistics associated with tapers evaluated at distinct frequencies when the number of tapers is small. When this condition does not hold, we instead rely on the variance reduction resulting from the orthogonality of the tapers. Assume that
\rho c_{2}|k_{0}|\geq\sqrt{2}|J|^{\frac{1}{2d}}
for
(\rho,J)\in\{(r,I),(r_{p},I_{p})\}
. According to Lemma
D.4
, we have

\displaystyle B^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq K\left(\sum_{s=1}^{2}\|\rho_{s}^{-d/2}\psi_{i_{s}}(\cdot/\rho_{s})\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus[-R,R]^{d}}\|_{2}+\|\rho_{s}^{-d/2}\mathcal{F}[\psi_{i_{s}}(\cdot/\rho_{s})]\mathds{1}_{|\cdot|\geq|k_{j}-\widetilde{k_{j}}|/2}\|_{2}\right)

\displaystyle=K\left(\sum_{s=1}^{2}\|\psi_{i_{s}}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus[-R/\rho_{s},R/\rho_{s}]^{d}}\|_{2}+\|\mathcal{F}[\psi_{i_{s}}]\mathds{1}_{|\cdot|\geq\rho_{s}|k_{j}-\widetilde{k_{j}}|/2}\|_{2}\right),

where
\rho_{1}=r
and
\rho_{2}=r_{p}
. The
k_{0}
-allowed assumption ensures that
|k_{j}-\tilde{k_{j}}|\geq c_{2}|k_{0}|
. Using Lemma
F.1
, we get, for some
c^{\prime}>0
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|I||I_{p}|}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1}\in I\\
i_{2}\in I_{p}\end{subarray}}B\leq K\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\rho,J)\in\\
\{(r,I),(r_{p},I_{p})\}\end{subarray}}e^{-c^{\prime}(R/\rho)^{3\theta}}+e^{-c^{\prime}(c_{2}\rho|k_{0}|)^{3\theta}}.

Moreover, using assumption (
27
), we obtain

(\rho|k_{0}|)^{2}\geq\sqrt{2}|J|^{\frac{1}{2d}}\rho|k_{0}|\geq\sqrt{2}|W|^{\frac{1}{d}}|k_{0}|.

This yields,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|I||I_{p}|}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1}\in I\\
i_{2}\in I_{p}\end{subarray}}B\leq K\left(\frac{1}{|W||k|^{d}}+\mathcal{E}\right),

(57)

when
\rho c_{2}|k_{0}|\geq\sqrt{2}|J|^{\frac{1}{2d}}
for
(\rho,J)\in\{(r,I),(r_{p},I_{p})\}

Assume now that
\rho c_{2}|k_{0}|\leq\sqrt{2}|J|^{\frac{1}{2d}}
for
(\rho,J)=(r,I)
or
(\rho,J)=(r_{p},I_{p})
. According to Lemma
D.3
and Lemma
F.1
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|I||I_{p}|}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1}\in I\\
i_{2}\in I_{p}\end{subarray}}B
