\displaystyle 158|246|37,~158|247|36,~158|367|24,~158|467|23,~167|235|48,~167|238|45,~167|245|38

\displaystyle 167|248|35,~167|358|24,~167|458|23,~168|235|47,~168|237|45,~168|245|37,~168|247|35

\displaystyle 168|357|24,~168|457|23,~235|467|18,~235|468|17,~236|457|18,~236|458|17,~237|458|16

\displaystyle 237|468|15,~238|457|16,~238|467|15,~245|367|18,~245|368|17,~246|357|18,~246|358|17

\displaystyle 247|358|16,~247|368|15,~248|357|16,~248|367|15,~257|368|14,~257|468|13,~258|367|14

\displaystyle 258|467|13,~267|358|14,~267|458|13,~268|357|14,~268|457|13.

The cumulants related to these partitions can be written (leveraging the connectivity condition
\sigma\vee 12|34|56|78=1
),

\displaystyle q_{\textbf{XI}}:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{s}\in J_{s}\\
s\in[4]\end{subarray}}\kappa(D_{i_{1}},D_{i_{2}},D_{i_{3}})\kappa(\overline{D_{i_{1}}},\overline{D_{i_{2}}},D_{i_{4}})\kappa(\overline{D_{i_{3}}},\overline{D_{i_{4}}}),

where
(J_{s})_{s\in[4]}
takes values in
\{I,I_{p}\}
and exactly two
J_{s}
are equal to
I
. Moreover, if
i\in I
, then
D_{i}=C_{i}^{1}(k)
for a proper frequency
k\in\{\pm k_{j_{1}},\pm k_{j_{2}}\}
, and
i\in I_{p}
, then
D_{i}=C_{i}^{2}(k)
for a proper frequency
k\in\{\pm\widetilde{k_{j_{1}}},\widetilde{\pm k_{j_{2}}}\}
. Applying four times the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we get

\displaystyle|q_{\textbf{XI}}|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{s}\in J_{s};\\
s\in\{2,3,4\}\end{subarray}}\sqrt{\sum_{i_{1}\in J_{1}}|\kappa(D_{i_{1}},D_{i_{2}},D_{i_{3}})|^{2}}\sqrt{\sum_{i_{1}\in J_{1}}|\kappa(\overline{D_{i_{1}}},\overline{D_{i_{2}}},D_{i_{4}})|^{2}}|\kappa(D_{i_{3}},D_{i_{4}})|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{3}\in J_{3}\\
i_{4}\in J_{4}\end{subarray}}\sqrt{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1}\in J_{1}\\
i_{2}\in J_{2}\end{subarray}}|\kappa(D_{i_{1}},D_{i_{2}},D_{i_{3}})|^{2}}\sqrt{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1}\in J_{1}\\
i_{2}\in J_{2}\end{subarray}}|\kappa(\overline{D_{i_{1}}},\overline{D_{i_{2}}},D_{i_{4}})|^{2}}|\kappa(D_{i_{3}},D_{i_{4}})|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i_{4}\in J_{4}}\sqrt{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{s}\in J_{s};\\
s\in\{1,2,3\}\end{subarray}}|\kappa(D_{i_{1}},D_{i_{2}},D_{i_{3}})|^{2}}\sqrt{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1}\in J_{1}\\
i_{2}\in J_{2}\end{subarray}}|\kappa(\overline{D_{i_{1}}},\overline{D_{i_{2}}},D_{i_{4}})|^{2}}\sqrt{\sum_{i_{3}\in J_{3}}|\kappa(D_{i_{3}},D_{i_{4}})|^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{s}\in J_{s};\\
s\in\{1,2,3\}\end{subarray}}|\kappa(D_{i_{1}},D_{i_{2}},D_{i_{3}})|^{2}}\sqrt{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{s}\in J_{s};\\
s\in\{1,2,4\}\end{subarray}}|\kappa(\overline{D_{i_{1}}},\overline{D_{i_{2}}},D_{i_{4}})|^{2}}\sqrt{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{3}\in J_{3}\\
i_{4}\in J_{4}\end{subarray}}|\kappa(D_{i_{3}},D_{i_{4}})|^{2}}.

Arguing as in the case of the partitions of category
X
, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{1\leq j_{1},j_{2}\leq N}|\kappa_{\textbf{XI}}|\leq K\left(\frac{\log(|W|)^{d}}{|W|^{2}|k_{0}|^{d}}+\frac{1}{|k_{0}|^{2d}|W|^{2}}+\frac{1}{|I||I_{p}|}\left(\left(\frac{(|I|\vee|I_{p}|)^{\frac{1}{d}}}{|W|^{\frac{1}{d}}|k_{0}|}\right)^{\zeta}+\frac{1}{N}\right)\right)+\mathcal{E}.

It remains to consider the partitions of category
XII
. They consist in

\displaystyle 16|28|35|47,~68|14|27|35,~16|27|48|35,~16|27|38|45,~38|27|15|46,~28|14|36|57

\displaystyle 58|27|13|46,~45|67|18|23,~16|23|58|47,~25|46|38|17,~24|68|15|37,~14|68|25|37

\displaystyle 38|14|57|26,~58|13|47|26,~58|46|17|23,~16|28|45|37,~18|46|23|57,~45|68|17|23

\displaystyle 27|15|48|36,~38|67|14|25,~67|15|23|48,~38|24|15|67,~16|23|48|57,~18|35|47|26

\displaystyle 16|24|37|58,~58|14|27|36,~67|24|18|35,~13|48|57|26,~68|15|23|47,~38|17|45|26

\displaystyle 45|28|13|67,~24|18|36|57,~28|13|46|57,~58|14|37|26,~16|24|38|57,~68|13|25|47

\displaystyle 28|15|46|37,~68|27|13|45,~67|13|25|48,~67|28|14|35,~24|68|17|35,~28|36|15|47

\displaystyle 18|25|36|47,~45|18|37|26,~58|24|36|17,~25|36|17|48,~17|48|35|26,~18|46|25|37

The cumulants related to these partitions can be written, leveraging the connectivity condition
\sigma\vee 12|34|56|78=1
,

\displaystyle q_{\textbf{XII}}:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{s}\in J_{s}\\
s\in[4]\end{subarray}}\kappa(D_{i_{1}},D_{i_{2}})\kappa(\overline{D_{i_{2}}},D_{i_{3}})\kappa(\overline{D_{i_{3}}},D_{i_{4}})\kappa(\overline{D_{i_{4}}},\overline{D_{i_{1}}}).

where
(J_{s})_{s\in[4]}
takes values in
\{I,I_{p}\}
, and exactly two of the
J_{s}
are equal to
I
. Moreover, if
i\in I
, then
D_{i}=C_{i}^{1}(k)
for an appropriate frequency
k\in\{\pm k_{j_{1}},\pm k_{j_{2}}\}
, and if
i\in I_{p}
, then
D_{i}=C_{i}^{2}(k)
for a suitable frequency
k\in\{\pm\widetilde{k_{j_{1}}},\pm\widetilde{k_{j_{2}}}\}
. Applying the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality four times, we obtain

\displaystyle|q_{\textbf{XII}}|\leq\sqrt{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1}\in J_{1}\\
i_{2}\in J_{2}\end{subarray}}|\kappa(D_{i_{1}},D_{i_{2}})|^{2}}\sqrt{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{2}\in J_{2}\\
i_{3}\in J_{3}\end{subarray}}|\kappa(\overline{D_{i_{2}}},D_{i_{3}})|^{2}}\sqrt{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{3}\in J_{3}\\
i_{4}\in J_{4}\end{subarray}}|\kappa(\overline{D_{i_{3}}},D_{i_{4}})|^{2}}\sqrt{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1}\in J_{1}\\
i_{4}\in J_{4}\end{subarray}}|\kappa(D_{i_{1}},D_{i_{4}})|^{2}}.

Note that, due to the connectivity condition, at least one block contains two frequencies, one indexed by
j_{1}
and the other by
j_{2}
. Since the partition is connected to
12|34|56|78
, the difference between these two frequencies is at least
|k|c_{7}
with
c_{7}>0
. Accordingly, we use the
k_{0}
-assumption and Lemma
D.3
to get

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{1\leq j_{1},j_{2}\leq N}|q_{\textbf{XII}}|\leq K\prod_{s=1}^{4}(|J_{s}|\wedge|J_{s+1}|)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\left(\frac{(|I|\vee|I_{p}|)^{\frac{1}{d}}}{|W|^{\frac{1}{d}}|k|^{d}}\right)^{\zeta}+\frac{1}{N}\right)+\mathcal{E},

with
J_{5}:=J_{1}
. Using
\prod_{s=1}^{4}(|J_{s}|\wedge|J_{s+1}|)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq|I||I_{p}|
, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{1\leq j_{1},j_{2}\leq N}|\kappa_{\textbf{XII}}|\leq K\frac{1}{|I||I_{p}|}\left(\left(\frac{(|I|\vee|I_{p}|)^{\frac{1}{d}}}{|W|^{\frac{1}{d}}|k|^{d}}\right)^{\zeta}+\frac{1}{N}\right)+\mathcal{E}.

Gathering all the previous bounds, we conclude the proof of this lemma.
∎

With the previous lemmas, are now in position to prove Lemma
C.1
.

Proof of Lemma
C.1
.

By Jensen’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\max_{I\in\mathcal{I}}|\Delta_{I}|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\max_{I\in\mathcal{I}}|\Delta_{I}|^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\sqrt{|\mathcal{I}|\max_{I\in\mathcal{I}}(\mathbb{E}[\Delta_{I}]^{2}+\operatorname{Var}[\Delta_{I}])}.

We prove that
\max_{I\in\mathcal{I}}(\mathbb{E}[\Delta_{I}]^{2}+\operatorname{Var}[\Delta_{I}])=o(|W|^{-4\beta/(2\beta+d)})
. Throughout the proof,
K
denotes a generic constant that may vary from line to line. We start with
\max_{I\in\mathcal{I}}\mathbb{E}[\Delta_{I}]^{2}
. According to Lemma
C.2
, we have

|\mathbb{E}[\Delta_{I}]|\leq K\Bigg(\frac{(|I||I_{p}|)^{\frac{\beta}{d}}}{|W|^{2\frac{\beta}{d}}}+\frac{\log(|W|)^{d}}{|W|}+\frac{1}{|W||k_{0}|^{d}}+\mathcal{E}(|I|,|I_{p}|)\Bigg).

Since
|I|\wedge|I_{p}|\geq\log(|W|)^{2d/(3\theta)+\eta}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}\leq Ke^{-\frac{1}{K}\log(|W|)^{1+3\theta\eta/(2d)}}=o\left(|W|^{-\frac{4\beta}{2\beta+d}}\right).

(68)

We always have

\log(|W|)^{d}/|W|=o(|W|^{-2\beta/(2\beta+d)})
. Moreover,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|W||k_{0}|^{d}}=A_{W}^{-d}\frac{1}{|W||W|^{-d/(2\beta+d)}}=A_{W}^{-d}\frac{1}{|W|^{2\beta/(2\beta+d)}}=o(|W|^{-2\beta/(2\beta+d)}),

since
A_{W}\to\infty
as
|W|\to\infty
. Since
|I|\leq c_{5}|W|^{2\beta/(2\beta+d)}
and
|I_{p}|=o(|W|^{2\beta/(2\beta+d)})
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{(|I||I_{p}|)^{\frac{\beta}{d}}}{|W|^{2\frac{\beta}{d}}}=o\left(\left(\frac{|W|^{\frac{4\beta}{2\beta+d}}}{|W|^{2}}\right)^{\beta/d}\right)=o(|W|^{-2\beta/(2\beta+d)}).

All previous
o
are uniform over
I\in\mathcal{I}
. Accordingly
\max_{I\in\mathcal{I}}\mathbb{E}[\Delta_{I}]^{2}=o(|W|^{-4\beta/(2\beta+d)}).

Concerning the variance, using Corollary
G.1
, we obtain

\displaystyle\operatorname{Var}[\Delta_{I}]=\frac{4}{N^{2}}\sum_{1\leq j_{1},j_{2}\leq N}(A+B+C),

with

\displaystyle A:=\kappa(\widehat{S}_{I}(k_{j_{1}}),\widehat{S}_{0}(\tilde{k_{j_{1}}}),\widehat{S}_{I}(k_{j_{2}}),\widehat{S}_{0}(\tilde{k_{j_{2}}})),

\displaystyle B:=\mathbb{E}[\widehat{S}_{I}(k_{j_{1}})-S_{\frac{1}{2}}(k_{j_{1}})]\kappa(\widehat{S}_{0}(\tilde{k_{j_{1}}}),\widehat{S}_{I}(k_{j_{2}}),\widehat{S}_{0}(\tilde{k_{j_{2}}}))+\mathbb{E}[\widehat{S}_{0}(\tilde{k_{j_{1}}})-S_{\frac{1}{2}}(\tilde{k_{j_{1}}})]\kappa(\widehat{S}_{I}(k_{j_{1}}),\widehat{S}_{I}(k_{j_{2}}),\widehat{S}_{0}(\tilde{k_{j_{2}}}))

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}[\widehat{S}_{I}(k_{j_{2}})-S_{\frac{1}{2}}(\tilde{k_{j_{2}}})]\kappa(\widehat{S}_{I}(k_{j_{1}}),\widehat{S}_{0}(\tilde{k_{j_{1}}}),\widehat{S}_{0}(\tilde{k_{j_{2}}}))-\mathbb{E}[\widehat{S}_{0}(\tilde{k_{j_{2}}})-S_{\frac{1}{2}}(\tilde{k_{j_{2}}})]\kappa(\widehat{S}_{I}(k_{j_{1}}),\widehat{S}_{0}(\tilde{k_{j_{1}}}),\widehat{S}_{I}(k_{j_{2}})),

\displaystyle C:=\kappa(\widehat{S}_{I}(k_{j_{1}}),\widehat{S}_{I}(k_{j_{2}}))\kappa(\widehat{S}_{0}(\tilde{k_{j_{1}}}),\widehat{S}_{0}(\tilde{k_{j_{2}}}))+\kappa(\widehat{S}_{I}(k_{j_{1}}),\widehat{S}_{0}(\tilde{k_{j_{2}}}))\kappa(\widehat{S}_{0}(\tilde{k_{j_{1}}}),\widehat{S}_{I}(k_{j_{2}})).

According to Lemma
C.3
,

\displaystyle\frac{4}{N^{2}}\sum_{1\leq j_{1},j_{2}\leq N}|C|\leq K\left(a_{1}+\frac{\log(|W|)^{2d}}{|W|^{2}}+\mathcal{E}\right),

with

\displaystyle a_{1}:=\frac{1}{|I||I_{p}|}\left(\left(\frac{(|I_{p}|\vee|I|)^{\frac{1}{d}}}{|W|^{\frac{1}{d}}|k_{0}|}\right)^{d-1}+\frac{1}{N}\right).

By (
68
), we have
\mathcal{E}=o\left(|W|^{-4\beta/(2\beta+d)}\right).
Moreover, we have
\log(|W|)^{2d}/|W|^{2}=o\left(|W|^{-4\beta/(2\beta+d)}\right).

Using the assumptions concerning the cardinal of the tapers index set of
\mathcal{I}
, we get

\displaystyle a_{1}

\displaystyle\leq K\left(\frac{1}{(|I|\wedge|I_{p}|)^{1+1/d}}\left(\frac{1}{|k||W|^{\frac{1}{d}}}\right)^{d-1}+\frac{1}{N(|I|\wedge|I_{p}|)^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq K\left(\frac{A_{W}^{d-1-\eta(1+1/d)}}{|W|^{\frac{2\beta}{2\beta+d}\frac{d+1}{d}}}\left(\frac{1}{A_{W}|W|^{\frac{1}{d}-\frac{1}{2\beta+d}}}\right)^{d-1}+\frac{A_{W}^{\frac{2d(d-1)}{d+1}-\eta(1+1/d)}}{N|W|^{4\beta/(2\beta+d)}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq KA_{W}^{-\eta(1+1/d)}|W|^{-\frac{4\beta}{2\beta+d}}=o\left(|W|^{-\frac{4\beta}{2\beta+d}}\right),

since
N\geq A_{W}^{2d(d-1)/(d+1)}
and
A_{W}\to\infty
as
|W|\to\infty
.
According to Lemma
C.4
,

\displaystyle\frac{4}{N^{2}}\sum_{1\leq j_{1},j_{2}\leq N}|B|\leq K\left(b(I,I_{p})+b(I_{p},I)+b+\mathcal{E}(|I|,|I_{p}|)\right),

where
b(I,I_{p}),b(I_{p},I)
and
b
are defined in the statement of Lemma
C.4
. For
b
, we have, using the fact that
4\beta/(2\beta+d)\leq 4/3<5/3
when
\beta\leq 2
and
d\geq 2
:

\displaystyle b

\displaystyle=\frac{\log(|W|)^{d}}{|W|^{2}|k_{0}|^{d}}+\frac{\log(|W|)^{d}}{|W|^{\frac{5}{3}}}+\frac{1}{|k_{0}|^{2d}|W|^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\log(|W|)^{d}|W|^{\frac{d}{2\beta+d}}}{|W|^{2}A_{W}^{d}}+\frac{|W|^{\frac{2d}{2\beta+d}}}{A_{W}^{2d}|W|^{2}}+o(|W|^{-\frac{4\beta}{2\beta+d}})

\displaystyle=\frac{\log(|W|)^{d}}{|W|^{\frac{d}{2\beta+d}}A_{W}^{d}}|W|^{-\frac{4\beta}{2\beta+d}}+A_{W}^{-2d}|W|^{-\frac{4\beta}{2\beta+d}}+o(|W|^{-\frac{4\beta}{2\beta+d}})=o(|W|^{-\frac{4\beta}{2\beta+d}}).

We bound
b(I,I_{p})
. We have
b(I,I_{p})\leq K(b_{1}+b_{2}),

with

\displaystyle b_{1}:=\left(\frac{|I|}{|W|}\right)^{\frac{\beta}{d}}|I_{p}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{1}{|I||I_{p}|}\left(\frac{|I_{p}|^{\frac{1}{d}}}{|W|^{\frac{1}{d}}|k_{0}|}\right)^{d-1},

\displaystyle b_{2}:=\left(\frac{|I|}{|W|}\right)^{\frac{\beta}{d}}\left(\frac{1}{|W|\log(|W|)^{\eta}}+\frac{1}{N|I||I_{p}|^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right).

Using the computations for
a_{1}
and

\displaystyle\left(\frac{|I|}{|W|}\right)^{\frac{\beta}{d}}|I_{p}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq K|W|^{-\frac{\beta}{d}\frac{d}{2\beta+d}}|W|^{\frac{\beta}{2\beta+d}}=K,

we obtain
b_{1}=o(|W|^{-4\beta/(2\beta+d)}).
Concerning
b_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle b_{2}\leq\frac{K}{|W|^{\frac{3\beta+d}{2\beta+d}}\log(|W|)^{\eta}}+\frac{K}{|W|^{\frac{\beta}{2\beta+d}}}\frac{A_{W}^{\frac{3}{2}\frac{d(d-1)}{d+1}}}{A_{W}^{\frac{2d(d-1)}{d+1}}|W|^{\frac{3}{2}\frac{2\beta}{2\beta+d}}}=o(|W|^{-\frac{4\beta}{2\beta+d}}),

since
\beta\leq d
and
A_{W}\to\infty
as
|W|\to\infty
. The bound for
b(I_{p},I)
is obtained by exchanging
I
and
I_{p}
. It remains to consider
A
. According to Lemma
C.5
:

\displaystyle\frac{4}{N^{2}}\sum_{1\leq j_{1},j_{2}\leq N}|A|\leq K\left(a_{1}+b\right)+\mathcal{E}.

We have already proved that
a_{1}+b+\mathcal{E}=o(|W|^{-4\beta/(2\beta+d)})
.
Since all the previous
o
are uniform over
I\in\mathcal{I}
, we have obtained
\max_{I\in\mathcal{I}}\operatorname{Var}[\Delta_{I}]^{2}=o(|W|^{-4\beta/(2\beta+d)}).

This concludes the proof.
∎

Appendix D
Technical results concerning joint cumulants of linear statistics of point processes

This section gathers bounds on the joint cumulants of linear statistics of point processes. Since Section
6
requires handling joint statistics involving thinnings of a given point process, the results presented here are established in this general framework. The structure of the section is as follows. Lemma
D.1
is related to second order cumulants. Lemma
D.2
provides a general but coarse bound on high-order cumulants, without using the orthogonality of the tapers or any decorrelation between frequencies. Lemmas
D.3
and
D.6
exploit the orthogonality of the tapers, while Lemmas
D.4
and
D.5
address decorrelation between frequencies.

Lemma D.1
.

Let
\Phi
be a stationary point process with intensity
\lambda
, pair correlation function
g
and structure factor
S
. Let
p\in[0,1]
. Let
\Phi_{p}
and
\Phi_{p}^{\prime}
be two independent
p
-thinning of
\Phi
. We denote
\Phi_{1-p}:=\Phi\setminus\Phi_{p}
and
\Phi_{1-p}^{\prime}:=\Phi\setminus\Phi_{p}^{\prime}
. Let
f_{1},f_{2}\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\cap L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).
Then,

\displaystyle a:=\operatorname{Cov}\left[\sum_{x\in\Phi_{p}}f_{1}(x),\sum_{x\in\Phi_{p}}f_{2}(x)\right]=\lambda p\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathcal{F}[f_{1}](k)\overline{\mathcal{F}[f_{2}](k)}(1-p+pS(k))dk,

\displaystyle b:=\operatorname{Cov}\left[\sum_{x\in\Phi_{p}}f_{1}(x),\sum_{x\in\Phi_{1-p}}f_{2}(x)\right]=(2\pi)^{\frac{d}{2}}\lambda^{2}p(1-p)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathcal{F}[f_{1}](k)\overline{\mathcal{F}[f_{2}](k)}\mathcal{F}[g-1](k)dk,

\displaystyle c:=\operatorname{Cov}\left[\sum_{x\in\Phi_{p}}f_{1}(x),\sum_{x\in\Phi_{p}^{\prime}}f_{2}(x)\right]=\lambda^{2}p^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathcal{F}[f_{1}](k)\overline{\mathcal{F}[f_{2}](k)}S(k)dk,

\displaystyle d:=\operatorname{Cov}\left[\sum_{x\in\Phi_{p}}f_{1}(x),\sum_{x\in\Phi_{1-p}^{\prime}}f_{2}(x)\right]=\lambda^{2}p(1-p)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathcal{F}[f_{1}](k)\overline{\mathcal{F}[f_{2}](k)}S(k)dk.

Proof.

The proof of this lemma proceeds by conditioning on
\Phi
and applying the law of total covariance. The argument for
a
, which is a standard result, follows the same steps as those used for
b
,
c
, and
d
, and is therefore omitted. We focus on the latter three, which are less classical.
Note that

\displaystyle b:=\operatorname{Cov}\left[\sum_{x\in\Phi}B_{x}f_{1}(x),\sum_{x\in\Phi}(1-B_{x})f_{2}(x)\right],

for i.i.d. Bernouilli random variables
(B_{x})_{x\in\Phi}
of parameter
p
. Using the law of the total covariance, we obtain

\displaystyle b

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\operatorname{Cov}\left[\sum_{x\in\Phi}B_{x}f_{1}(x),\sum_{x\in\Phi}(1-B_{x})f_{2}(x)|\Phi\right]\right]+\operatorname{Cov}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{x\in\Phi}B_{x}f_{1}(x)|\Phi\right],\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{x\in\Phi}(1-B_{x})f_{2}(x)|\Phi\right]\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{x,y\in\Phi}f_{1}(x)\overline{f_{2}(x)}\operatorname{Cov}(B_{x},1-B_{y})\right]+p(1-p)\operatorname{Cov}\left[\sum_{x\in\Phi}f_{1}(x),\sum_{x\in\Phi}f_{2}(x)\right].

According to the Plancherel theorem and using (
4
), we have

\displaystyle b

\displaystyle=(2\pi)^{\frac{d}{2}}\lambda p(1-p)\left(-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathcal{F}[f_{1}](k)\overline{\mathcal{F}[f_{2}](k)}dk+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathcal{F}[f_{1}]\overline{\mathcal{F}[f_{2}]}(1+\lambda\mathcal{F}[g-1])\right)

\displaystyle=(2\pi)^{\frac{d}{2}}\lambda^{2}p(1-p)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathcal{F}[f_{1}](k)\overline{\mathcal{F}[f_{2}](k)}\mathcal{F}[g-1](k)dk,

which concludes the proof for this term. Concerning
c
, we have

\displaystyle c:=\operatorname{Cov}\left[\sum_{x\in\Phi}B_{x}f_{1}(x),\sum_{x\in\Phi}B_{x}^{\prime}f_{2}(x)\right],

for i.i.d. Bernouilli random variables
(B_{x},B_{x}^{\prime})_{x\in\Phi}
of parameter
p
. In the law of the total covariance, the first term vanishes due to the independence between
(B_{x})_{x\in\Phi}
and
(B_{x}^{\prime})_{x\in\Phi}
. Hence

\displaystyle c=p^{2}\operatorname{Cov}\left[\sum_{x\in\Phi}f_{1}(x),\sum_{x\in\Phi}f_{2}(x)\right],

which gives the result for
c
using (
4
). The proof of
d
consists in considering i.i.d. Bernouilli
(B_{x}^{\prime})_{x\in\Phi}
of parameter
1-p
instead of
p
.
∎

Lemma D.2
.

Let
\Phi
be a stationary point process with intensity
\lambda
and
p\in[0,1]
. Let
m\geq 2
. Denote by
(\Phi_{p}^{j})_{1\leq j\leq m}

p
-independent-thinnings of
\Phi
, that are independent between them. Moreover, let
\Phi_{1-p}^{j}=\Phi\setminus\Phi_{p}^{j}
. Consider
(f_{i})_{i=1,\dots,m}\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\cap L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Let
\tau:[m]\to\{p,1-p\}
and
\varphi:[m]\to[m]
. Then,

\displaystyle\left|\kappa\left(\sum_{x\in\Phi_{\tau(s)}^{\varphi(s)}}f_{s}(x);s\in[m]\right)\right|\leq K\prod_{s=1}^{m}\|f_{s}\|_{m},

where
K<\infty
depends only on
p
,
\tau
,
m
,
\lambda
and
|\gamma_{\text{red}}^{(n)}|(\mathbb{R}^{d(n-1)})
for
2\leq n\leq m
. In particular if
p=1
,
\tau\equiv 1
and
\varphi\equiv 1
, we have
K=\sum_{\pi\in\Pi[m]}|\gamma_{\text{red}}^{(|\pi|)}|(\mathbb{R}^{d(|\pi|-1)})
, with the convention
|\gamma_{\text{red}}^{(1)}|(\mathbb{R}^{0})=\lambda
.

Proof.

Let
(B_{x}^{j})_{x\in\Phi,j\in[m]}
be i.i.d. Bernouilli random variables of parameter
p
. We use the notation,
\tau_{s}(y)=y\mathds{1}_{\tau_{s}=p}+(1-y)\mathds{1}_{\tau_{s}=1-p}
. According to the law of the total cumulance, we have

\displaystyle\kappa

\displaystyle:=\kappa\left(\sum_{x\in\Phi_{\tau(s)}^{\varphi(s)}}f_{s}(x);s\in[m]\right)=\sum_{\pi\in\Pi[m]}\kappa\left(\kappa\left(\sum_{x\in\Phi}\tau_{s}(B_{x}^{\varphi(s)})f_{s}(x)|\Phi;s\in b\right);b\in\pi\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\pi\in\Pi[m]}\kappa\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{s}\in\Phi;\\
s\in b\end{subarray}}\kappa(\tau_{s}(B_{x_{s}}^{\varphi(s)});s\in b)f_{s}(x_{s});b\in\pi\right).

Using the independence of
(B_{x}^{j})_{x\in\Phi}
, for
j\in[m]
, we obtain

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{s}\in\Phi;\\
s\in b\end{subarray}}\kappa(\tau_{s}(B_{x_{s}}^{\varphi(s)});s\in b)f_{s}(x_{s})=\mathds{1}_{\#\{\varphi(s)|s\in b\}=1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{s}\in\Phi;\\
s\in b\end{subarray}}\kappa(\tau_{s}(B_{x_{s}}^{\varphi(s_{0})});s\in b)f_{s}(x_{s}),

where
s_{0}=\min(b)
. Moreover, we have

\displaystyle\kappa(\tau_{s}(B_{x_{s}}^{\varphi(s_{0})});s\in b)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{\#\{\tau_{s}=1|s\in b\}}\kappa(B_{x_{s}}^{\varphi(s_{0})};s\in b)=(-1)^{\#\{\tau_{s}=1|s\in b\}}\mathds{1}_{\#\{x_{s}|s\in b\}=1}\kappa_{|b|}(B),

where
B
is a Bernouilli random variable of parameter
p
. This yields

\displaystyle\kappa

\displaystyle=\sum_{\pi\in\Pi[m]}A\times B,

(69)

with

\displaystyle A:=(-1)^{\#\{\tau_{s}=1|s\in[m]\}}\prod_{b\in\pi}\mathds{1}_{\#\{\varphi(s)|s\in b\}=1}\kappa_{|b|}(B),\quad B:=\kappa\left(\sum_{x\in\Phi}\prod_{s\in b}f_{s}(x);b\in\pi\right).

The quantity
A
does not depend on the functions
(f_{i})_{1\leq i\leq m}
. Accordingly, it remains to bound
B
. Denote
g_{b}(x):=\prod_{s\in b}f_{s}(x)
. Then,

\displaystyle B=\sum_{\pi^{\prime}\in\Pi\{b;~b\in\pi\}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d|\pi^{\prime}|}}\prod_{b^{\prime}\in\pi^{\prime}}\prod_{b\in b^{\prime}}g_{b}(x_{b^{\prime}})\gamma^{(|\pi^{\prime}|)}(x_{b^{\prime}};b^{\prime}\in\pi^{\prime})=:\sum_{\pi^{\prime}\in\Pi\{b;~b\in\pi\}}B_{\pi^{\prime}},

where
\Pi\{b;~b\in\pi\}
denotes the set of partitions of
\{b;~b\in\pi\}
. If
|\pi^{\prime}|=1
, we have using the Hölder’s inequality,

\displaystyle|B_{\pi^{\prime}}|\leq\lambda\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\prod_{i\in[m]}|f_{i}(x)|dx\leq\lambda\prod_{s=1}^{m}\|f_{s}\|_{m}.

We use the same argument when
|\pi|>1
. Let
b_{0}^{\prime}\in\pi^{\prime}
be a fixed block. By stationarity, and using again the Hölder’s inequality, we obtain:

\displaystyle|B_{\pi^{\prime}}|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d(|\pi^{\prime}|-1)}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\prod_{b\in b_{0}^{\prime}}|g_{b}(x_{b_{0}^{\prime}})|\prod_{b^{\prime}\in\pi^{\prime}\setminus\{b_{0}^{\prime}\}}\prod_{b\in b^{\prime}}|g_{b}(x_{b^{\prime}}+x_{b_{0}^{\prime}})|dx_{b_{0}^{\prime}}|\gamma_{red}^{(|\pi^{\prime}|)}|(dx_{b}^{\prime};b\in\pi\setminus\{b_{0}^{\prime}\})

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d(|\pi^{\prime}|-1)}}\prod_{b\in b_{0}^{\prime}}\prod_{s\in b}\|f_{s}\|_{m}\prod_{b^{\prime}\in\pi^{\prime}\setminus\{b_{0}^{\prime}\}}\prod_{b\in b^{\prime}}\prod_{s\in b}\|f_{s}(\cdot+x_{b}^{\prime})\|_{m}|\gamma_{red}^{(|\pi^{\prime}|)}|(dx_{b}^{\prime};b^{\prime}\in\pi^{\prime}\setminus\{b_{0}^{\prime}\})

\displaystyle=|\gamma_{red}^{(|\pi|)}|(\mathbb{R}^{d(|\pi^{\prime}|-1)})\prod_{s=1}^{m}\|f_{s}\|_{m}.

(70)

This concludes the proof. To obtain the value of the constant when
p=1
, note that in that case
A
is non zero only if all the blocks of
\pi
are of size
1
.
∎

Lemma D.3
.

Let
\Phi
be a stationary point process with intensity
\lambda
and pair correlation function
g
and
p\in[0,1]
. Denote by
(\Phi_{p}^{j})_{j\in[2]}
two
p
-independent-thinnings of
\Phi
, that are independent between them. Denote
\Phi_{1-p}^{j}=\Phi\setminus\Phi_{p}^{j}
. Let
I_{1}
and
I_{2}
be two discrete subsets of
\mathbb{N}^{d}
. Let
(f_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}^{d}}
and
(h_{i})_{i\mathbb{N}^{d}}
be two orthogonal families of
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Let
\tau[2]\mapsto\{p,1-p\}^{2}
and
\varphi:[2]\mapsto[2]
. Then, we have for all set
W\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\frac{1}{|I_{1}||I_{2}|}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1}\in I_{1}\\
i_{2}\in I_{2}\end{subarray}}\left|\operatorname{Cov}\left(\sum_{x\in\Phi_{\tau(1)}^{\varphi(1)}\cap W}f_{i_{1}}(x),\sum_{x\in\Phi_{\tau(2)}^{\varphi(2)}\cap W}h_{i_{2}}(x)\right)\right|^{2}\leq\\
\frac{K}{|I_{1}|\vee|I_{2}|}\left(1+\mathds{1}_{|I_{1}|\geq|I_{2}|}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1}\in I_{1}\end{subarray}}\|f_{i_{1}}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus W}\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\mathds{1}_{|I_{1}|<|I_{2}|}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{2}\in I_{2}\end{subarray}}\|h_{i_{2}}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus W}\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)^{2}.

where
K<\infty
depends only on
p
,
\tau
,
\varphi
,
\lambda
,
\|S\|_{\infty}
and
\|g-1\|_{1}
. In particular if
p=1
,
\tau\equiv 1
and
\varphi\equiv 1
, we have
K=(\lambda\|S\|_{\infty})^{2}
.

Proof.

According to Lemma
D.1
, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{Cov}\left(\sum_{x\in\Phi_{\tau(1)}^{\varphi(1)}\cap W}f_{i_{1}}(x),\sum_{x\in\Phi_{\tau(2)}^{\varphi(2)}\cap W}h_{i_{2}}(x)\right)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathcal{F}[f_{i_{1}}\mathds{1}_{W}](k)\overline{\mathcal{F}[h_{i_{2}}\mathds{1}_{W}]}(k)s(k)dk,

where
s
is bounded. Assume that
|I_{1}|\geq|I_{2}|
. We use the decomposition

\displaystyle I_{i_{1},i_{2}}:=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathcal{F}[f_{i_{1}}\mathds{1}_{W}](k)\overline{\mathcal{F}[h_{i_{2}}\mathds{1}_{W}]}(k)s(k)dk=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathcal{F}[f_{i_{1}}](k)\overline{\mathcal{F}[\mathds{1}_{W}h_{i_{2}}]}(k)s(k)dk+\delta,

where

\displaystyle\delta=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathcal{F}[f_{i_{1}}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus W}](k)\overline{\mathcal{F}[h_{i_{2}}\mathds{1}_{W}]}(k)s(k)dk.

Using the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and the Plancherel theorem, we get

\displaystyle|\delta|\leq\|s\|_{\infty}\|f_{i_{1}}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus W}\|_{2}\|h_{i_{2}}\mathds{1}_{W}\|_{2}\leq\|s\|_{\infty}\|f_{i_{1}}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus W}\|_{2}.

Accordingly, using the fact that
(\mathcal{F}[f_{i}])_{i\in\mathbb{N}^{d}}
is orthogonal, we obtain

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1}\in I_{1}\\
i_{2}\in I_{2}\end{subarray}}|I_{i_{1},i_{2}}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1}\in I_{1}\\
i_{2}\in I_{2}\end{subarray}}|\langle\mathcal{F}[f_{i_{1}}],\mathcal{F}[\mathds{1}_{W}h_{i_{2}}]s\rangle|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1}\in I_{1}\\
i_{2}\in I_{2}\end{subarray}}\delta^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{2}\in I_{2}\end{subarray}}\|\mathcal{F}[\mathds{1}_{W}h_{i_{2}}]s\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+|I_{2}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|s\|_{\infty}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1}\in I_{1}\end{subarray}}\|f_{i_{1}}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus W}\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq|I_{2}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|s\|_{\infty}\left(1+\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1}\in I_{1}\end{subarray}}\|f_{i_{1}}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus W}\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right).

This yields

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|I_{1}||I_{2}|}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1}\in I_{1}\\
i_{2}\in I_{2}\end{subarray}}|I_{i_{1},i_{2}}|^{2}\leq\frac{\|s\|_{\infty}^{2}}{|I_{1}|\vee|I_{2}|}\left(1+\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1}\in I_{1}\end{subarray}}\|f_{i_{1}}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus W}\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)^{2}.

The proof for the case
|I_{1}|<|I_{2}|
follows by symmetry.
∎

Lemma D.4
.

Let
\Phi
be a stationary point process with intensity
\lambda
and pair correlation function
g
and
p\in[0,1]
. Denote by
(\Phi_{p}^{j})_{j\in[2]}
two
p
-independent-thinnings of
\Phi
, that are independent between them. Denote
\Phi_{1-p}^{j}=\Phi\setminus\Phi_{p}^{j}
. Let
f_{1},f_{2}
be in
L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\cap L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
with
\|f_{1}\|_{2}=\|f_{2}\|_{2}=1
and
k_{1},k_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Let
\tau[2]\mapsto\{p,1-p\}^{2}
and
\varphi:[2]\mapsto[2]
. Then, we have for all set
W\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\left|\operatorname{Cov}\left(\sum_{x\in\Phi_{\tau(1)}^{\varphi(1)}\cap W}f_{1}(x)e^{-\bm{i}k_{1}\cdot x},\sum_{x\in\Phi_{\tau(2)}^{\varphi(2)}\cap W}f_{2}(x)e^{-\bm{i}k_{2}\cdot x}\right)\right|\\
\leq K\sum_{s=1}^{2}\|f_{s}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus W}\|_{2}+\|\mathcal{F}[f_{s}]\mathds{1}_{|\cdot|\geq|k_{1}-k_{2}|/2}\|_{2},

where
K<\infty
depends only on
p
,
\tau
,
\varphi
,
\lambda
,
\|S\|_{\infty}
and
\|g-1\|_{1}
. In particular if
p=1
,
\tau\equiv 1
and
\varphi\equiv 1
, we have
K=\lambda\|S\|_{\infty}
.

Proof.

According to Lemma
D.1
, we have

\operatorname{Cov}\left(\sum_{x\in\Phi_{\tau(1)}^{\varphi(1)}\cap W}f_{1}(x)e^{-\bm{i}k_{1}\cdot x},\sum_{x\in\Phi_{\tau(2)}^{\varphi(2)}\cap W}f_{2}(x)e^{-\bm{i}k_{2}\cdot x}\right)\\
=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathcal{F}[f_{1}\mathds{1}_{W}e^{-\bm{i}k_{1}\cdot}](k)\overline{\mathcal{F}[f_{2}\mathds{1}_{W}e^{-\bm{i}k_{2}\cdot}]}(k)s(k)dk,

where
s
is bounded. The first step is to remove the two indicator functions
\mathds{1}_{W}
. We have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathcal{F}[f_{1}\mathds{1}_{W}e^{-\bm{i}k_{1}\cdot}](k)\overline{\mathcal{F}[f_{2}\mathds{1}_{W}e^{-\bm{i}k_{2}\cdot}]}(k)s(k)dk=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathcal{F}[f_{1}e^{-\bm{i}k_{1}\cdot}](k)\overline{\mathcal{F}[f_{2}e^{-\bm{i}k_{2}\cdot}]}(k)s(k)dk+\delta,

with

\delta=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathcal{F}[f_{1}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus W}e^{-\bm{i}k_{1}\cdot}](k)\overline{\mathcal{F}[f_{2}e^{-\bm{i}k_{2}\cdot}]}(k)s(k)dk\\
-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathcal{F}[f_{1}\mathds{1}_{W}e^{-\bm{i}k_{1}\cdot}](k)\overline{\mathcal{F}[f_{2}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus W}e^{-\bm{i}k_{2}\cdot}]}(k)s(k)dk.

By Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we have

\displaystyle|\delta|

\displaystyle\leq\|s\|_{\infty}\left(\|\mathcal{F}[f_{1}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus W}]\|_{2}\|\mathcal{F}[f_{2}]\|_{2}+\|\mathcal{F}[f_{1}\mathds{1}_{W}]\|_{2}\|\mathcal{F}[f_{2}]\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus W}\|_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\|s\|_{\infty}\left(\|\mathcal{F}[f_{1}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus W}]\|_{2}+\|\mathcal{F}[f_{2}]\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus W}\|_{2}\right).

It remains to upper bound

\displaystyle I:=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathcal{F}[f_{1}e^{-\bm{i}k_{1}\cdot}](k)\overline{\mathcal{F}[f_{2}e^{-\bm{i}k_{2}\cdot}]}(k)s(k)dk=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathcal{F}[f_{1}](k)\overline{\mathcal{F}[f_{2}]}(k+k_{2}-k_{1})s(k-k_{1})dk.

We split
\mathbb{R}^{d}=A_{1}\sqcup A_{2}
with
A_{1}:=\{k\in\mathbb{R}^{d}|~|k-k_{1}+k_{2}|\leq|k_{1}-k_{2}|/2\}
and
A_{2}:=\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus A_{1}
. Since for
k\in A_{1}
, we have

|k-k_{1}+k_{2}|\geq|k_{1}-k_{2}|-|k|\geq|k_{1}-k_{2}|/2,

we obtain using the Cauchy-Schwarz’s inequality and the Plancherel theorem

\displaystyle|I|\leq\|s\|_{\infty}\left(\|\mathcal{F}[f_{1}]\mathds{1}_{|\cdot|\geq|k_{1}-k_{2}|/2}\|_{2}+\|\mathcal{F}[f_{2}]\mathds{1}_{|\cdot|\geq|k_{1}-k_{2}|/2}\|_{2}\right),

which concludes the proof.
∎

Lemma D.5
.

Let
\Phi
be a stationary point process with intensity
\lambda
and pair correlation function
g
and
p\in[0,1]
. Denote by
(\Phi_{p}^{j})_{j\in[3]}
three
p
-independent-thinnings of
\Phi
, that are independent between them. Denote
\Phi_{1-p}^{j}=\Phi\setminus\Phi_{p}^{j}
. Let
f_{1},f_{2},f_{3}
be
L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\cap L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
functions and
k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Let
\tau[3]\mapsto\{p,1-p\}^{3}
and
\varphi:[3]\mapsto[3]
. Then, we have for all set
W\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\left|\kappa\left(\sum_{x\in\Phi_{\tau(s)}^{\varphi(s)}\cap W}f_{s}(x)e^{-\bm{i}k_{s}\cdot x};s\in[3]\right)\right|\leq K\sum_{s=1}^{3}\|f_{s}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus W}\|_{3}\prod_{s^{\prime}\in[3]\setminus\{s\}}\|f_{s^{\prime}}\|_{3}\\
+K\sum_{s=1}^{3}\|\mathcal{F}[f_{s}]\mathds{1}_{|\cdot|\geq|k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}|/3}\|_{1}\prod_{s^{\prime}\in[3]\setminus\{s\}}\|f_{s^{\prime}}\|_{2},

(71)

where
K<\infty
depends only on
p
,
\tau
,
\varphi
,
\lambda
and
|\gamma_{\text{red}}^{(m)}|(\mathbb{R}^{d(m-1)})
for
m\in\{2,3\}
. In particular if
p=1
,
\tau\equiv 1
and
\varphi\equiv 1
, we have
K=\sum_{\pi\in\Pi[3]}|\gamma_{\text{red}}^{(|\pi|)}|(\mathbb{R}^{d(|\pi|-1)})
, with the convention
|\gamma_{\text{red}}^{(1)}|(\mathbb{R}^{0})=\lambda
.

Proof.

Denote by
|\kappa_{W}|
the left hand-side of (
71
). According to (
69
) for
m=3
, we have

\displaystyle\kappa_{W}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\pi\in\Pi[3]}a_{\pi}\sum_{\pi^{\prime}\in\Pi\{b;~b\in\pi\}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d|\pi^{\prime}|}}\prod_{b^{\prime}\in\pi^{\prime}}\prod_{b\in b^{\prime}}\prod_{s\in b}\mathds{1}_{W}(x_{b^{\prime}})f_{s}(x_{b^{\prime}})\gamma^{(|\pi^{\prime}|)}(x_{b^{\prime}};b^{\prime}\in\pi^{\prime}),

with, denoting by
B
a Bernouilli random variable of parameter
p
,

a_{\pi}:=(-1)^{\#\{\tau_{s}=1|s\in[m]\}}\prod_{b\in\pi}\mathds{1}_{\#\{\varphi(s)|s\in b\}=1}\kappa_{|b|}(B).

The first step is to remove the truncation to
W
. Note that

\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{d|\pi^{\prime}|}\setminus W^{|\pi^{\prime}|}}(x_{b}^{\prime};x_{b}^{\prime}\in\pi^{\prime})\leq\sum_{b_{0}^{\prime}\in\pi^{\prime}}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus W}(x_{b_{0}^{\prime}}).

Denote
\Delta:=|\kappa_{W}-\kappa_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|
. Subsequently,

\displaystyle\Delta\leq\sum_{\pi\in\Pi[3]}|a_{\pi}|\sum_{\pi^{\prime}\in\Pi\{b;~b\in\pi\}}\sum_{b_{0}^{\prime}\in\pi^{\prime}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d|\pi^{\prime}|}}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus W}(x_{b_{0}^{\prime}})\prod_{b^{\prime}\in\pi^{\prime}}\prod_{b\in b^{\prime}}\prod_{s\in b}|f_{s}(x_{b^{\prime}})|\gamma^{(|\pi^{\prime}|)}(x_{b^{\prime}};b^{\prime}\in\pi^{\prime}).

By stationarity and using the Hölder’s inequality, as in (
D
), we obtain

\displaystyle\Delta\leq\sum_{\pi\in\Pi[3]}|a_{\pi}|\sum_{\pi^{\prime}\in\Pi\{b;~b\in\pi\}}|\gamma_{red}^{(|\pi^{\prime}|)}|(\mathbb{R}^{d(|\pi^{\prime}|-1)})\sum_{b_{0}^{\prime}\in\pi^{\prime}}\prod_{i\in b_{0}^{\prime}}\|f_{i}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus W}\|_{3}\prod_{i\notin b_{0}^{\prime}}\|f_{i}\|_{3}.

Since

\displaystyle\prod_{i\in b_{0}^{\prime}}\|f_{i}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus W}\|_{3}\prod_{i\notin b_{0}^{\prime}}\|f_{i}\|_{3}\leq\sum_{s=1}^{3}\|f_{s}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus W}\|_{3}\prod_{s^{\prime}\in[3]\setminus\{s\}}\|f_{s^{\prime}}\|_{3},

we have

\displaystyle\Delta\leq\left(\sum_{\pi\in\Pi[3]}|a_{\pi}|\sum_{\pi^{\prime}\in\Pi\{b;~b\in\pi\}}|\gamma_{red}^{(|\pi^{\prime}|)}|(\mathbb{R}^{d(|\pi^{\prime}|-1)})|\pi^{\prime}|\right)\sum_{s=1}^{3}\|f_{s}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus W}\|_{3}\prod_{s^{\prime}\in[3]\setminus\{s\}}\|f_{s^{\prime}}\|_{3}.

Now, we focus on

\displaystyle\kappa_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\pi\in\Pi[3]}a_{\pi}\sum_{\pi^{\prime}\in\Pi\{b;~b\in\pi\}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d|\pi^{\prime}|}}\prod_{b^{\prime}\in\pi^{\prime}}\prod_{b\in b^{\prime}}\prod_{s\in b}f_{s}(x_{b^{\prime}})\gamma^{(|\pi^{\prime}|)}(x_{b^{\prime}};b^{\prime}\in\pi^{\prime})

\displaystyle=:\sum_{\pi\in\Pi[3]}a_{\pi}\sum_{\pi^{\prime}\in\Pi\{b;~b\in\pi\}}I_{\pi^{\prime}}.

Each of the terms
I_{\pi^{\prime}}
is bounded using the same idea, but the details differ. We start with with the case
|\pi^{\prime}|=1
. Using the Fourier inversion theorem
[
29
]
we get

\displaystyle|I_{\pi^{\prime}}|

\displaystyle=\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\prod_{i=1}^{3}f_{i}(x)e^{-\bm{i}k_{i}\cdot x}dx\right|\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2d}}\left|\mathcal{F}[f_{1}](k)\mathcal{F}[f_{2}](k^{\prime})\mathcal{F}[f_{3}](k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}-k-k^{\prime})\right|dkdk^{\prime}:=I_{\infty}.

Denote
\Sigma:=k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}
. Next, we split the integrals over the domains:

\displaystyle D_{1}:=\{|k|\geq|\Sigma|/3\},~D_{2}=\{|k|\leq|\Sigma|/3,~|k^{\prime}|\geq|\Sigma|/3\},~D_{3}:=\{|k|\leq|\Sigma|/3,~|k^{\prime}|\leq|\Sigma|/3\}.

Note that on
D_{3}
, we have

|\Sigma-k-k^{\prime}|\geq|\Sigma|-|k|-|k^{\prime}|\geq|\Sigma|/3.

Hence, the triangular inequality and then the Young’s inequality for the convolution yield

\displaystyle\left|I_{\pi^{\prime}}\right|\leq I_{\infty}\leq\sum_{s=1}^{3}\|\mathcal{F}[f_{s}]\mathds{1}_{|\cdot|\geq|\Sigma|/3}\|_{1}\prod_{s^{\prime}\in[3]\setminus\{s\}}\|\mathcal{F}[f_{s^{\prime}}\|_{2}.

(72)

For the other partitions, we adapt the previous technique, leveraging on the Brillinger-mixing condition. Define

\forall k\in\mathbb{R}^{d(m-1)},~\mathcal{F}[\gamma_{\text{red}}^{(m)}](k):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d(m-1)}}e^{-\bm{i}k\cdot x}\gamma_{\text{red}}^{(m)}(dx).

Note that
\|\mathcal{F}[\gamma_{\text{red}}^{(m)}]\|_{\infty}\leq|\gamma_{\text{red}}^{(m)}|(\mathbb{R}^{d(m-1)})<\infty
by Brillinger-mixing. We consider the case when
|\pi^{\prime}|=3
. Using the Fourier inversion theorem and the Fubini’s theorem, we have

\displaystyle I_{\pi^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3d}}\prod_{i\in[3]}f_{i}(x_{i})e^{-\bm{i}k_{i}\cdot x_{i}}\gamma^{(3)}(dx_{1},dx_{2},dx_{3})

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3d}}f_{1}(x_{1})e^{-\bm{i}x_{1}\cdot k_{1}}\prod_{s=2,3}f_{s}(x_{1}+x_{s})e^{-\bm{i}(x_{1}+x_{s})\cdot k_{s}}dx_{1}\gamma_{red}^{(3)}(dx_{2},dx_{3})

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2d}}\mathcal{F}\left[f_{1}e^{-\bm{i}k_{1}\cdot}\right]\left(k^{\prime}_{2}+k^{\prime}_{3}\right)\prod_{s=2,3}\mathcal{F}\left[f_{s}e^{-\bm{i}k_{s}\cdot}\right](-k^{\prime}_{s})\mathcal{F}[\gamma_{\text{red}}^{(3)}](k^{\prime}_{2},k^{\prime}_{3})dk_{2}^{\prime}dk_{3}^{\prime}.

By change of variables, we obtain

\displaystyle I_{\pi^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2d}}\mathcal{F}\left[f_{1}\right]\left(k_{1}+k^{\prime}_{1}+k^{\prime}_{2}\right)\prod_{s=2,3}\mathcal{F}\left[f_{s}\right](k_{s}-k^{\prime}_{s})\mathcal{F}[\gamma_{\text{red}}^{(3)}](k^{\prime}_{2},k^{\prime}_{3})

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2d}}\mathcal{F}\left[f_{1}\right]\left(\sum_{s=1}^{3}k_{s}-k^{\prime}_{1}-k^{\prime}_{2}\right)\prod_{s=2,3}\mathcal{F}\left[f_{s}\right](k^{\prime}_{s})\mathcal{F}[\gamma_{\text{red}}^{(3)}](-k^{\prime}_{1}+k_{1},-k^{\prime}_{2}+k_{2})dk_{2}^{\prime}dk_{3}^{\prime}.

Accordingly,
|I_{\pi^{\prime}}|\leq|\gamma_{\text{red}}^{(3)}|(\mathbb{R}^{2d})I_{\infty},

and we apply (
72
). Finally, assume that
|\pi^{\prime}|=2
. By renumbering, it suffices to upper bound:

\displaystyle I_{\pi^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2d}}f_{1}(x)e^{-\bm{i}k_{1}\cdot x}f_{2}(x)e^{-\bm{i}k_{2}\cdot x}f_{3}(x^{\prime})e^{-\bm{i}k_{3}\cdot x^{\prime}}\gamma^{(2)}(dx,dx^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathcal{F}\left[f_{1}e^{-\bm{i}k_{1}\cdot}f_{2}e^{-\bm{i}k_{2}\cdot}\right]\left(k\right)\mathcal{F}\left[f_{3}e^{-\bm{i}k_{3}\cdot}\right](-k)\mathcal{F}[\gamma_{\text{red}}^{(2)}](k)dk

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2d}}\mathcal{F}[f_{1}](k^{\prime}+k_{1})\mathcal{F}[f_{2}](k+k_{2}-k^{\prime})\mathcal{F}[f_{3}](-k+k_{3})\mathcal{F}[\gamma_{\text{red}}^{(2)}](k)dkdk^{\prime},

where we used the Fourier inversion theorem. By change of variables, we get
|I_{\pi^{\prime}}|\leq|\gamma_{\text{red}}^{(2)}|(\mathbb{R}^{d})I_{\infty}.

Recalling (
72
), this concludes the proof. To obtain the value of the constant when
p=1
, note that in that case
A
is non zero only if all the blocks of
\pi
are of size
1
.
∎

Lemma D.6
.

Let
\Phi
be a stationary point process with intensity
\lambda
and
p\in[0,1]
. Let
m\geq 2
and denote by
(\Phi_{p}^{j})_{j\in[m]}
three
p
-independent-thinnings of
\Phi
, that are independent between them. Denote
\Phi_{1-p}^{j}=\Phi\setminus\Phi_{p}^{j}
. Let
(f_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}^{d}},(h_{s})_{s=2,\dots,m}
be
L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\cap L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
functions. Assume that
(f_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}^{d}}
is orthogonal. Let
\tau[m]\mapsto\{p,1-p\}^{m}
and
\varphi:[m]\mapsto[m]
. Then, we have for all set
W\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}^{d}}\left|\kappa\left(\sum_{x\in\Phi_{\tau(1)}^{\varphi(1)}\cap W}f_{i}(x),\sum_{x\in\Phi_{\tau(s)}^{\varphi(s)}\cap W}h_{s}(x);2\leq s\leq m\right)\right|^{2}\\
\leq K\prod_{s=2}^{m}\|h_{2}\|_{2(m-1)}^{2}+K\sum_{i\in I}\sum_{s=1}^{m}\|h_{s}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus W}\|_{m}\prod_{s^{\prime}\in[m]\setminus\{s^{\prime}\}}\|h_{s}\|_{m}.

where, we use the notation
h_{1}:=f_{i}
in the second line and where
K<\infty
depends only on
p
,
\tau
,
\varphi
,
\lambda
and
|\gamma_{\text{red}}^{(n)}|(\mathbb{R}^{d(m-1)})
for
2\leq n\leq m
.

Proof.

The first part of the proof is identical to the beginning of the proof of Lemma
D.5
. Denote
h_{1}=f_{i}
,
\Delta:=|\kappa_{W}-\kappa_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|
, where

\kappa_{W}:=\kappa\left(\sum_{x\in\Phi_{\tau(s)}^{\varphi(s)}\cap W}h_{s}(x);1\leq s\leq m\right).

To remove the truncation to
W
, we use the method of Lemma
D.5
to get

\displaystyle\Delta\leq\left(\sum_{\pi\in\Pi[m]}|a_{\pi}|\sum_{\pi^{\prime}\in\Pi\{b;~b\in\pi\}}|\gamma_{red}^{(|\pi^{\prime}|)}|(\mathbb{R}^{d(|\pi^{\prime}|-1)})|\pi^{\prime}|\right)\sum_{s=1}^{m}\|h_{s}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus W}\|_{m}\prod_{s^{\prime}\in[m]\setminus\{s\}}\|h_{s^{\prime}}\|_{m}.

Now, we focus on

\displaystyle\kappa_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\pi\in\Pi[m]}a_{\pi}\sum_{\pi^{\prime}\in\Pi\{b;~b\in\pi\}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d|\pi^{\prime}|}}\prod_{b^{\prime}\in\pi^{\prime}}\prod_{b\in b^{\prime}}\prod_{s\in b}h_{s}(x_{b^{\prime}})\gamma^{(|\pi^{\prime}|)}(x_{b^{\prime}};b^{\prime}\in\pi^{\prime})=:\sum_{\pi\in\Pi[m]}a_{\pi}\sum_{\pi^{\prime}\in\Pi\{b;~b\in\pi\}}I_{\pi^{\prime}}.

where, denoting by
B
a Bernouilli random variable of parameter
p
,

a_{\pi}:=(-1)^{\#\{\tau_{s}=1|s\in[m]\}}\prod_{b\in\pi}\mathds{1}_{\#\{\varphi(s)|s\in b\}=1}\kappa_{|b|}(B).

By triangular inequality, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in I}|\kappa_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|^{2}\leq\left(\sum_{\pi\in\Pi[m]}a_{\pi}\sum_{\pi^{\prime}\in\Pi\{b;~b\in\pi\}}\left(\sum_{i\in I}|I_{\pi^{\prime}}|^{2}\right)^{1/2}\right)^{2}.

As in Lemma
D.5
, we start by considering the partition
\pi^{\prime}
of size 1. We have

\displaystyle I_{\pi^{\prime}}=\lambda\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}f_{i}(x)\prod_{s=2}^{m}h_{s}(x)dx=\lambda\langle f_{i},\overline{\prod_{s=2}^{m}h_{s}}\rangle.

Since
(f_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}^{d}}
is orthogonal and using the Hölder’s inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}^{d}}|I_{\pi^{\prime}}|^{2}\leq\lambda^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\prod_{s=2}^{m}|h_{s}(x)|^{2}dx\leq\prod_{s=2}^{m}\|h_{2}\|_{2(m-1)}^{2}.

For the other partitions, we leverage on the Brillinger-mixing condition to adapt this argument. Let
\pi^{\prime}
with
|\pi^{\prime}|\geq 2
. Denote by
b_{0}^{\prime}\in\pi^{\prime}
the block containing
1
. By stationarity, we have

\displaystyle I_{\pi}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}f_{i}(x_{b_{0}^{\prime}})\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d(|\pi^{\prime}|-1)}}\prod_{b^{\prime}\in\pi^{\prime}}\prod_{b\in b^{\prime}}\prod_{s\in b\setminus\{1\}}h_{s}(x_{b^{\prime}}+x_{b_{0}^{\prime}})\gamma_{red}^{(|\pi^{\prime}|)}(dx_{b}^{\prime};b^{\prime}\in\pi^{\prime}\setminus\{b_{0}^{\prime}\})\right)dx_{b_{0}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=:\langle f_{i},\overline{F}\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Since
(f_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}^{d}}
is orthogonal and using the Hölder’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}^{d}}|I_{\pi^{\prime}}|^{2}\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|F(x)|^{2}dx=\int\displaylimits_{\mathbb{R}^{2d(|\pi^{\prime}|-1)}}\int\displaylimits_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\prod_{b^{\prime}\in\pi^{\prime}}\prod_{b\in b^{\prime}}\prod_{s\in b\setminus\{1\}}h_{s}(x_{b}^{\prime}+x_{b_{0}^{\prime}})\overline{h_{s}(y_{b}^{\prime}+x_{b_{0}^{\prime}})}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\gamma_{red}^{(|\pi^{\prime}|)}(dx_{b}^{\prime};b^{\prime}\in\pi{\setminus\{b_{0}^{\prime}\}})\overline{\gamma_{red}^{(|\pi^{\prime}|)}(dy_{b}^{\prime};by\in\pi{\setminus\{b_{0}^{\prime}\}})}

\displaystyle\leq\int\displaylimits_{\mathbb{R}^{2d(|\pi^{\prime}|-1)}}\prod_{b^{\prime}\in\pi^{\prime}}\prod_{b\in b^{\prime}}\prod_{s\in b\setminus\{1\}}\|h_{s}(x_{b}^{\prime}+\cdot)\|_{2(m-1)}\|h_{s}(y_{b}^{\prime}+\cdot)\|_{2(m-1)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad|\gamma_{red}^{(|\pi^{\prime}|)}|(dx_{b}^{\prime};b^{\prime}\in\pi{\setminus\{b_{0}^{\prime}\}})|\gamma_{red}^{(|\pi^{\prime}|)}|(dy_{b}^{\prime};b^{\prime}\in\pi_{\setminus\{b_{0}^{\prime}\}})

\displaystyle=|\gamma_{red}^{(|\pi|)}|(\mathbb{R}^{d(|\pi^{\prime}|-1)})^{2}\prod_{s=2}^{m}\|h_{s}\|_{2(m-2)}^{2}.

Accordingly,

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in I}|\kappa_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|^{2}\leq\left(\sum_{\pi\in\Pi[m]}a_{\pi}\sum_{\pi^{\prime}\in\Pi\{b;~b\in\pi\}}|\gamma_{red}^{(|\pi|)}|(\mathbb{R}^{d(|\pi^{\prime}|-1)})\right)^{2}\prod_{s=2}^{m}\|h_{s}\|_{2(m-2)}^{2},

which concludes the proof.

∎

Appendix E
Generalization to multitaper estimators with plug-in intensity

In this section, we consider the practical setting where the intensity is unknown. In that case, we adapt the multitaper estimators of the structure factor as follows.

Definition 8
.

Let
\Phi
be a stationary point process of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
W
be a compact set of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
I
be a subset of
\mathbb{N}^{d}
. Let
k_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
(f_{i})_{i\in I}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{|I|}
. The multitaper estimator with plug-in intensity is defined as

\widehat{S}^{\hat{\lambda}}(k_{0}):=\frac{1}{\widehat{\lambda}|I|}\sum_{i\in I}\widehat{S}_{i}^{\hat{\lambda}}(k_{0})\mathds{1}_{\widehat{\lambda}>0}+0\mathds{1}_{\widehat{\lambda}=0}

(73)

where

\displaystyle\widehat{S}_{i}^{\hat{\lambda}}(k_{0}):=\left|\sum_{x\in\Phi\cap W}e^{-\bm{i}k_{0}.x}f_{i}(x)-\widehat{\lambda}\int_{W}f_{i}(x)e^{-\bm{i}k\cdot x}dx\right|^{2},~\widehat{\lambda}:=\frac{\#\{\Phi\cap W\}}{|W|}.

Remark 3
.

Note that for estimating the spectra
\lambda S
instead of the structure factor
S
(see the discussion following equation (
3
)), we can use the following estimator

\widehat{\lambda S}^{\hat{\lambda}}(k_{0}):=\frac{1}{|I|}\sum_{i\in I}\widehat{S}_{i}^{\hat{\lambda}}(k_{0}).

The results concerning
\widehat{S}^{\hat{\lambda}}
can be readily adapted to the estimator
\widehat{\lambda S}^{\hat{\lambda}}
.

The following proposition shows that replacing the true intensity
\lambda
with its estimator
\widehat{\lambda}
in
\widehat{S}^{\widehat{\lambda}}
introduces an additional main error term of order
|I|^{-1/2}
. This stems from the fact that the Fourier transform of the tapers
(\mathcal{F}[f_{i}])_{i\in I}
converge to
\delta_{0}
, causing the integrals

\int_{W}f_{i}(x)e^{-\bm{i}k\cdot x}\,dx

to diverge for small frequencies
k
. Note that under the assumptions of Corollary
5.1
, this result implies that
\widehat{S}^{\widehat{\lambda}}
still remains minimax optimal for the
L^{1}(\mathbb{P})
risk.

Proposition E.1
.

Let
\Phi
be a stationary point process satisfying Assumption
3.1
. Let
W
be a subset of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
I
be a discrete subset of
\mathbb{N}^{d}
. We consider a family of orthonormal functions
(f_{i})_{i\in I}
of
L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\cap L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Then,

\displaystyle\sup_{k\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathbb{E}\left[|\widehat{S}^{\hat{\lambda}}(k)-S(k)|\right]\leq a_{1}\sup_{k\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathbb{E}

\displaystyle\left[|\widehat{S}^{\lambda}(k)-S(k)|^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}+\frac{a_{2}}{|I|^{\frac{1}{2}}}+\frac{a_{4}}{|W|^{\frac{1}{2}}},

(74)

where
(a_{n})_{n\in[4]}
are finite constants (see (
E
)) provided that

|\gamma_{\text{red}}^{4}|:=\lambda+7|\gamma_{\text{red}}^{(2)}|(\mathbb{R}^{d})+6|\gamma_{\text{red}}^{(3)}|(\mathbb{R}^{2d})+|\gamma_{\text{red}}^{(4)}|(\mathbb{R}^{3d})

is finite.

Proof.

Let
k_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. By triangular inequality

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}

\displaystyle\left[|\widehat{S}^{\hat{\lambda}}(k_{0})-S(k_{0})|\right]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[|\widehat{S}^{\lambda}(k_{0})-S(k_{0})|\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[|\widehat{S}^{\hat{\lambda}}(k_{0})-\widehat{S}^{\lambda}(k_{0})|\right]

\displaystyle\quad\leq\mathbb{E}\left[|\widehat{S}^{\lambda}(k_{0})-S(k_{0})|\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[|\widehat{S}^{\hat{\lambda}}(k_{0})-\widehat{S}^{\lambda}(k_{0})|\mathds{1}_{\widehat{\lambda}>0}\right]+\mathbb{P}[\widehat{\lambda}=0]\mathbb{E}[\widehat{S}^{\lambda}(k_{0})].

We bound
\mathbb{P}[\widehat{\lambda}=0]\mathbb{E}[\widehat{S}^{\lambda}(k_{0})]
. Using (
4
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\widehat{\lambda}=0)\leq\mathbb{P}[|\widehat{\lambda}-\lambda|\geq\lambda]\leq\frac{\mathbb{E}[|\widehat{\lambda}-\lambda|^{2}]}{\lambda^{2}}=\frac{1}{\lambda|W|^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\mathcal{F}[\mathds{1}_{W}](k)|^{2}S(k)dk\leq\frac{\|S\|_{\infty}}{\lambda|W|}.

Moreover, using the Plancherel theorem we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\widehat{S}^{\lambda}(k_{0})]=\frac{1}{|I|}\sum_{i\in I}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\mathcal{F}[f_{i}e^{-\bm{i}k_{0}\cdot}](k)|^{2}S(k)dk\leq\|S\|_{\infty}.

Accordingly
\mathbb{P}[\widehat{\lambda}=0]\mathbb{E}[\widehat{S}^{\lambda}(k_{0})]\leq\|S\|_{\infty}^{2}/(\lambda|W|)
.
We consider
\mathbb{E}\left[|\widehat{S}^{\hat{\lambda}}(k_{0})-\widehat{S}^{\lambda}(k_{0})|\mathds{1}_{\widehat{\lambda}>0}\right]
. By writing
\widehat{\lambda}=\widehat{\lambda}-\lambda+\lambda
in
\widehat{S}_{i}^{\widehat{\lambda}}(k_{0})
, we obtain when
\widehat{\lambda}>0
,

\widehat{S}^{\widehat{\lambda}}(k_{0})-\widehat{S}^{\lambda}(k_{0})=A+B+C,

where

\displaystyle A:=\left(\frac{\lambda}{\widehat{\lambda}}-1\right)\widehat{S}^{\lambda}(k_{0}),\qquad B=2\left(\frac{\lambda}{\widehat{\lambda}}-1\right)\frac{1}{|I|}\sum_{i\in I}\Re\left(C_{i}(k_{0})\cdot\overline{E_{i}(k_{0})}\right),

\displaystyle C:=\frac{(\widehat{\lambda}-\lambda)^{2}}{\widehat{\lambda}}\frac{1}{|I|}\sum_{i\in I}|E_{i}(k_{0})|^{2},\qquad E_{i}:=\int_{W}f_{i}(x)e^{-\bm{i}k\cdot x}dx.

and where
C_{i}(k_{0})
is defined in (
9
). We start with
C
. Since
(f_{i})_{i\in I}
is orthogonal, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|I|}\sum_{i\in I}|E_{i}(k_{0})|^{2}\leq\frac{1}{|I|}\|\mathds{1}_{W}\|_{2}^{2}=\frac{|W|}{|I|}.

(75)

Moreover, by Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{(\widehat{\lambda}-\lambda)^{2}}{\widehat{\lambda}}\mathds{1}_{\widehat{\lambda}>0}\right]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[(\widehat{\lambda}-\lambda)^{4}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\widehat{\lambda}^{2}}\mathds{1}_{\widehat{\lambda}>0}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

If
\widehat{\lambda}>0
then
\#\{\Phi\cap W\}\geq 1
, whence
\widehat{\lambda}\geq|W|^{-1}
. Accordingly,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\widehat{\lambda}^{2}}\mathds{1}_{\widehat{\lambda}>0}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[(2/\lambda)^{2}\mathds{1}_{\widehat{\lambda}\geq\lambda/2}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[|W|^{2}\mathds{1}_{\widehat{\lambda}\leq\lambda/2}\right]\leq\frac{4}{\lambda^{2}}+|W|^{2}\mathbb{P}[|\widehat{\lambda}-\lambda|\geq\lambda/2]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4}{\lambda^{2}}+\frac{16}{\lambda^{4}}|W|^{2}\mathbb{E}[|\widehat{\lambda}-\lambda|^{4}].

We express the centered moment of order four as
\mathbb{E}[|\widehat{\lambda}-\lambda|^{4}]=3\mathbb{E}[|\widehat{\lambda}-\lambda|^{2}]^{2}+\kappa_{4}(\widehat{\lambda}).
Using Lemma
D.2
we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|\widehat{\lambda}-\lambda|^{4}]\leq\frac{3\lambda^{2}\|S\|_{\infty}^{2}}{|W|^{2}}+\frac{|\gamma_{\text{red}}^{4}|\|\mathds{1}_{W}\|_{4}^{4}}{|W|^{4}}=\frac{3\lambda^{2}\|S\|_{\infty}^{2}}{|W|^{2}}+\frac{|\gamma_{\text{red}}^{4}|}{|W|^{3}}.

(76)

Accordingly,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\widehat{\lambda}^{2}}\mathds{1}_{\widehat{\lambda}>0}\right]\leq\frac{48\|S\|_{\infty}^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}+\frac{16|\gamma_{\text{red}}^{4}|}{\lambda^{4}|W|}+\frac{4\|S\|_{\infty}^{2}}{\lambda^{2}|W|^{2}}+\frac{4|\gamma_{\text{red}}^{4}|}{\lambda^{2}|W|^{3}}:=b_{1}

(77)

This yields,

\displaystyle C

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{|I|}\left(3\lambda^{2}\|S\|_{\infty}^{2}+\frac{|\gamma_{\text{red}}^{4}|}{|W|}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}b_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Now, we consider
A
. Using the Hölder-inequality we have

\displaystyle A

\displaystyle\leq\|S\|_{\infty}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{|\widehat{\lambda}-\lambda|}{\widehat{\lambda}}\mathds{1}_{\widehat{\lambda}>0}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[|\widehat{S}^{\lambda}(k_{0})-S(k_{0})|\frac{|\widehat{\lambda}-\lambda|}{\widehat{\lambda}}\mathds{1}_{\widehat{\lambda}>0}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\|S\|_{\infty}\mathbb{E}\left[|\widehat{\lambda}-\lambda|^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\mathds{1}_{\widehat{\lambda}>0}}{\widehat{\lambda}^{2}}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}+\left[|\widehat{\lambda}-\lambda|^{4}\right]^{\frac{1}{4}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\mathds{1}_{\widehat{\lambda}>0}}{\widehat{\lambda}^{4}}\right]^{\frac{1}{4}}\mathbb{E}\left[|\widehat{S}^{\lambda}(k_{0})-S(k_{0})|^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

For the first term, we have using (
77
)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[|\widehat{\lambda}-\lambda|^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\mathds{1}_{\widehat{\lambda}>0}}{\widehat{\lambda}^{2}}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{\|S\|_{\infty}}{\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}|W|^{\frac{1}{2}}}b_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}.

For the second one, using again the fact that if
\widehat{\lambda}>0
, then
\widehat{\lambda}\geq|W|^{-1}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\widehat{\lambda}^{4}}\mathds{1}_{\widehat{\lambda}>0}\right]\leq\frac{16}{\lambda^{4}}\left(1+|W|^{4}\mathbb{E}[|\widehat{\lambda}-\lambda|^{4}]\right).

Using equation (
76
), we obtain after computations

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\widehat{\lambda}^{4}}\mathds{1}_{\widehat{\lambda}>0}\right]\mathbb{E}[|\widehat{\lambda}-\lambda|^{4}]\leq 48\|S\|_{\infty}^{4}+\frac{96\|S\|_{\infty}^{2}|\gamma_{\text{red}}^{4}|}{\lambda^{2}|W|}+\frac{48\|S\|_{\infty}^{2}}{\lambda^{2}|W|^{2}}+\frac{16|\gamma_{\text{red}}^{4}|^{2}}{|W|^{2}\lambda^{4}}+\frac{16|\gamma_{\text{red}}^{4}|}{\lambda^{4}|W|^{3}}.

This yields, after numerical evaluations

\displaystyle A\leq(3\|S\|_{\infty}+\varepsilon_{W})\mathbb{E}\left[|\widehat{S}^{\lambda}(k_{0})-S(k_{0})|^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}+\frac{\|S\|_{\infty}}{\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}|W|^{\frac{1}{2}}}b_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}

where

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{W}:=\frac{4\|S\|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{4}}|\gamma_{\text{red}}^{4}|^{\frac{1}{4}}}{\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}|W|^{\frac{1}{4}}}+\frac{3\|S\|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}|W|^{\frac{1}{2}}}+\frac{2|\gamma_{\text{red}}^{4}|^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|W|^{\frac{1}{2}}\lambda}+\frac{2|\gamma_{\text{red}}^{4}|^{\frac{1}{4}}}{\lambda|W|^{\frac{3}{4}}}.

It remains to bound
B
. Using twice the Hölder-inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle B

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\widehat{\lambda}^{2}}\mathds{1}_{\widehat{\lambda}>0}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}[|\widehat{\lambda}-\lambda|^{4}]^{\frac{1}{4}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{1}{|I|}\sum_{i\in I}|C_{i}(k_{0})||E_{i}(k_{0})|\right)^{4}\right]^{\frac{1}{4}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\widehat{\lambda}^{2}}\mathds{1}_{\widehat{\lambda}>0}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}[|\widehat{\lambda}-\lambda|^{4}]^{\frac{1}{4}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{1}{|I|}\sum_{i\in I}|C_{i}(k_{0})|^{2}\right)^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{4}}\left(\frac{1}{|I|}\sum_{i\in I}|E_{i}(k_{0})|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Recalling (
75
), (
76
) and (
77
), we obtain

\displaystyle B

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\mathbb{E}[\widehat{S}^{\lambda}(k_{0})^{2}]^{\frac{1}{4}}}{|I|^{\frac{1}{2}}}\left(3\lambda^{2}\|S\|_{\infty}^{2}+\frac{|\gamma_{\text{red}}^{4}|}{|W|}\right)^{\frac{1}{4}}b_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(3\lambda^{2}\|S\|_{\infty}^{2}+\frac{|\gamma_{\text{red}}^{4}|}{|W|}\right)^{\frac{1}{4}}b_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\frac{2^{\frac{3}{2}}\|S\|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|I|^{\frac{1}{2}}}+2^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}[|\widehat{S}^{\lambda}(k_{0})-S(k_{0})|^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}+\frac{2^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|I|}\right).

Gathering the bounds on
A
,
B
and
C
, we conclude the proof of (
74
) with

\displaystyle b_{1}:=\frac{48\|S\|_{\infty}^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}+\frac{16|\gamma_{\text{red}}^{4}|}{\lambda^{4}|W|}+\frac{4\|S\|_{\infty}^{2}}{\lambda^{2}|W|^{2}}+\frac{4|\gamma_{\text{red}}^{4}|}{\lambda^{2}|W|^{3}},\quad b_{2}:=3\lambda^{2}\|S\|_{\infty}^{2}+\frac{|\gamma_{\text{red}}^{4}|}{|W|},

\displaystyle a_{1}:=1+3\|S\|_{\infty}+\varepsilon_{W}+\sqrt{2}b_{2}^{\frac{1}{4}}b_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}},\quad a_{2}:=2^{\frac{3}{2}}\|S\|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{2}}b_{2}^{\frac{1}{4}}b_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}+\left(b_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}+\sqrt{2}b_{2}^{\frac{1}{4}}\right)b_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}},

(78)

\displaystyle a_{4}:=\frac{\|S\|_{\infty}b_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}}+\frac{\|S\|_{\infty}}{\lambda|W|^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

∎

Appendix F
Results concerning the Hermite functions

The next lemma provides bounds on the tail of the Hermite functions.

Lemma F.1
.

Consider a finite set of indexes
I
of
\mathbb{N}^{d}
and denote
M_{I}=\max\{i_{1},\dots,i_{d}|i=(i_{1},\dots,i_{d})\in I\}
. Let
\rho\in(0,\infty)
be such that
\rho>\sqrt{2(M_{I}+1)+(M_{I}+1)^{1/3+\theta}}
, for
\theta>0
. Denote
W=[-\rho,\rho]^{d}
. Then, there exist two constants
0<c,C<\infty
such that

\sup_{i\in I}\|\psi_{i}\mathbf{1}_{W^{c}}\|_{1}+\sup_{i\in I}\|\psi_{i}\mathbf{1}_{W^{c}}\|_{2}^{2}\leq Ce^{-c\rho^{3\theta}},

(79)

Proof.

Recall that the
d
-dimensional Hermite functions are defined as the product of one-dimensional ones. Using the inequality:

\forall x=(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})\in\mathbb{R}^{d},~1-\prod_{i=1}^{d}\mathbf{1}_{|x_{l}|\leq\rho}\leq\sum_{l=1}^{d}\mathbf{1}_{|x_{l}|>\rho},

and the fact that one-dimensional Hermite functions have a unit
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
-norm, we reduce the proof to the case
d=1
. Let
n\leq M_{I}
. We start with the case
\rho\geq\sqrt{2}\sqrt{2n+1}
. According to Theorem B of
[
3
]
, we have for all
x\in\mathbb{R}
with
|x|\geq\rho
,
|\psi_{n}(x)|\leq Ce^{-cx^{2}}
, for some finite positive constants
c
and
C
, that will change from line to line in the following. Using the standard upper bound of the tail of a Gaussian random variable, we obtain:

\|\psi_{n}\mathbf{1}_{|\cdot|\geq\rho}\|_{1}+\|\psi_{n}\mathbf{1}_{|\cdot|\geq\rho}\|_{2}^{2}\leq Ce^{-c\rho^{2}}.

Consider now the case
\rho\leq\sqrt{2}\sqrt{2n+1}
. Since
\rho\geq\sqrt{2(n+1)+(n+1)^{1/3+\theta}}
, according to the bound on equation (2.13) of the proof of Theorem 2.3 of
[
2
]
, we have

\|\psi_{n}\mathbf{1}_{|\cdot|\geq\rho}\|_{1}+\|\psi_{n}\mathbf{1}_{|\cdot|\geq\rho}\|_{2}^{2}\leq Ce^{-cn^{3\theta/2}}\leq Ce^{-c\rho^{3\theta}}.

This concludes the proof.
∎

The next lemma studies the Sobolev norms of the Hermite functions.

Lemma F.2
.

Assume that
I=\{i\in\mathbb{N}^{d}|~|i|_{\infty}<i_{\max}\}
with
i_{\max}\geq 1
. Let
\beta\in(0,2]
. Then

\frac{1}{|I|}\sum_{i\in I}\|\psi_{i}\|_{\dot{H}^{\beta/2}}^{2}\leq|I|^{\beta/(2d)}.

(80)

Proof.

According to Hölder’s inequality, we have, since
\beta\leq 2
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\psi_{i}(k)|^{2}|k|^{\beta}dk

\displaystyle\leq\|\psi_{i}\|_{2}^{1-\beta/2}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\psi_{i}(k)|^{2}|k|^{2}dk\right)^{\beta/2}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{2}\sum_{s=1}^{d}(2i_{s}+1)\right)^{\beta/2}\leq\frac{1}{2^{\beta/2}}\sum_{s=1}^{d}(2i_{s}+1)^{\beta/2},

where we used the inclusion of
\ell^{1}([d])
in
\ell^{2/\beta}([d])
(since
2/\beta\geq 1
).
This yields (
80
):

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|I|}\sum_{i\in I}\|\psi_{i}\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2^{\beta/2}|I|}\sum_{0\leq i_{1},\dots,i_{d}<i_{\max}}\sum_{s=1}^{d}(2i_{s}+1)^{\beta/2}=\frac{i_{\max}^{d-1}}{2^{\beta/2}|I|}\sum_{s=1}^{i_{\max}-1}(2s+1)^{\beta/2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{i_{\max}^{d-1}}{2^{\beta/2}|I|}\sum_{s=1}^{i_{\max}-1}(2i_{\max})^{\beta/2}=|I|^{\beta/(2d)}.

∎

The following lemma provides upper bounds on averages of
L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
norms of the Hermite functions. The key point is to control the scaling with respect to both the number of tapers
|I|
and the order
p
of the norms.

Lemma F.3
.

Assume that
I=\{i\in\mathbb{N}^{d}|~|i|_{\infty}<i_{\max}\}
with
i_{\max}\geq 1
. Then, there exists a numerical constant
c_{\psi}\in(0,\infty)
such that,

\frac{1}{|I|}\sum_{i\in I}\|\psi_{i}\|_{3}^{2}\leq c_{\psi}^{d}\frac{1}{|I|^{\frac{1}{6}}}.

(81)

Moreover, for
p\geq 4
, we have

\frac{1}{|I|}\sum_{i\in I}\|\psi_{i}\|_{p}^{2}\leq c_{\psi}^{d}\frac{\log(|I|^{\frac{1}{d}})^{\frac{2d}{p}}}{|I|^{\frac{1}{3p}+\frac{1}{6}}},

(82)

\frac{1}{|I|}\sum_{i\in I}\|\psi_{i}\|_{p}\leq c_{\psi}^{d}\frac{\log(|I|^{\frac{1}{d}})^{\frac{d}{p}}}{|I|^{\frac{1}{6p}+\frac{1}{12}}}.

(83)

Proof.

The proof of this lemma relies on the following results (see equation (3.2) of
[
2
]
). For all
p<4
, there exists
c_{p}<\infty
such that

\forall n\geq 0,~\int_{\mathbb{R}}|\psi_{n}(x)|^{p}dx\leq\frac{c_{p}}{(n+1)^{\frac{p}{4}-\frac{1}{2}}},

(84)

for
p=4
, there exists
c_{4}<\infty
such that

\forall n\geq 0,~\int_{\mathbb{R}}|\psi_{n}(x)|^{4}dx\leq\frac{c_{4}\log(n+1)}{(n+1)^{\frac{1}{2}}},

(85)

and for
p>4
, there exists
c_{p}<\infty
such that

\forall n\geq 0,~\int_{\mathbb{R}}|\psi_{n}(x)|^{p}dx\leq\frac{c_{p}}{(n+1)^{\frac{p}{12}+\frac{1}{6}}},

(86)

We start by proving (
81
). According to (
84
) we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|I|}\sum_{i\in I}\|\psi_{i}\|_{3}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq c_{3}^{\frac{2d}{3}}\frac{1}{|I|}\sum_{i\in I}\prod_{s=1}^{d}\frac{1}{(i_{s}+1)^{1/6}}=c_{3}^{\frac{2d}{3}}\frac{1}{|I|}\left(\sum_{s=0}^{i_{\max}-1}\frac{1}{(s+1)^{1/6}}\right)^{d}.

A comparison sum-integral yields the result:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|I|}\sum_{i\in I}\|\psi_{i}\|_{4}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq c_{3}^{\frac{2d}{3}}\frac{1}{|I|}\left(\int_{0}^{i_{\max}}\frac{ds}{(s+1)^{1/6}}\right)^{d}\leq c_{4}^{\frac{2d}{3}}\frac{1}{|I|}\left(\frac{6}{5}(2i_{\max})^{5/6}\right)^{d}\leq c_{\psi}^{d}\frac{1}{|I|^{1/4}},

where
c_{\psi}\geq 62^{5/6}c_{2}^{2/3}/5
.
The subtlety of (
82
) is that the constant
c_{\psi}
does not depend on
p
. Theorem 1 of
[
13
]
ensures that there exists
D\in(1,\infty)
such that

\forall n\geq 0,~\|\psi_{n}||_{\infty}\leq\frac{D}{(n+1)^{1/12}}.

(87)

Hence, for
n\geq 0
and
p\geq 4
, using (
85
) and (
87
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}|\psi_{n}(x)|^{p}dx

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}|\psi_{n}(x)|^{p-4}|\psi_{n}(x)|^{4}dx

\displaystyle\leq D^{p-4}c_{2}\frac{\log(n+1)}{(n+1)^{(p-4)/12}(n+1)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\leq(Dc_{2})^{p}\frac{\log(n+1)}{(n+1)^{p/12+1/6}}.

Finally, concluding as in the proof of (
81
) we obtain (
82
) and (
83
) with
c_{\psi}=(1+Dc_{2})^{2}
.
∎

Appendix G
Results related to cumulants

G.1
Isserlis-Wick type formulae

We denote by
\mathbf{1}_{n}
the single block partition of
[n]
, i.e.
\mathbf{1}_{n}:=12\dots n
. For two partitions
\sigma
and
\tau
,
\sigma\vee\tau
denotes their least upper bound (refer to Chapter 3 of
[
54
]
or Chapter 2 of
[
61
]
). The next result is a direct application of equation (3.3) of
[
54
]
.

Corollary G.1
.

Let
Y_{1},\dots,Y_{m}
be a family of complex valued random variables. Then,

\kappa_{m}(|Y_{1}|^{2},\dots,|Y_{m}|^{2})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\sigma\in\Pi[2m],\\
\sigma\vee\tau=\mathbf{1}_{2m},|\sigma|\geq 2\end{subarray}}\prod_{b\in\sigma}\kappa(Z_{i};i\in b),

(88)

where the sum runs over the partition of
[2m]
and for
i\in\{1,\dots,m\}
,
Z_{2i-1}=Y_{i}
and
Z_{2i}=\overline{Y_{i}}
and
\tau=12|34|\dots|(2m-1)~2m
.

G.2
Extension of the result of Bentkus and Rudzkis under multiple Statulevičius conditions

The proof of the concentration inequality stated in Theorem
5.2
relies on the following lemma, which provides a tool to derive non-asymptotic deviation inequalities from cumulant bounds. This result is a straightforward extension of a classical inequality
[
10
]
, corresponding to the case
J=1
. Although this generalization is natural and may be known to experts, we have not found it explicitly stated in the literature. Such an extension is required to handle the three distinct contributions to the fluctuations of the multitaper estimator of the structure factor, as discussed in Section
5.3
.

Theorem G.1
.

Let
X
be a real random variable. Suppose that
\mathbb{E}[X]=0
. Let
J\geq 1
,
(\gamma_{i})_{1\leq i\leq J}\in[0,\infty)^{J}
,
(H_{i},\Delta_{i})_{1\leq i\leq J}\in(0,\infty)^{2J}
. Assume that, for all
m\geq 2
,

|\kappa_{m}(X)|\leq\sum_{j=1}^{J}\left(\frac{m!}{2}\right)^{1+\gamma_{j}}\frac{H_{j}}{\Delta_{j}^{m-2}}.

(89)

Then, for all
x>0
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(X\geq x)\leq C\max_{j=1,\dots,J}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2J}\frac{x^{2}}{H_{j}+x^{2-\alpha_{j}}/\Delta_{j}^{\alpha_{j}}}\right),

(90)

where
\alpha_{j}:=1/(1+\gamma_{j})
and
C<\infty
.

Proof.

For simplicity, we follow the proof of Theorem 2.5 of Section 5 of
[
25
]
to prove (
90
). At a price of more technicality, the following proof could be adapted to prove (
90
) with
C=1
(see
[
71
]
, proof of Lemma 2.4, pages 37-41). For
n\geq 1
, we denote the truncated exponential sum as

\exp_{n}(x):=\sum_{m=0}^{n}\frac{1}{m!}x^{m}.

Let
h>0
and
n\geq 1
that will be specified during the proof. Since
\exp_{2n}
is increasing over
\mathbb{R}^{+}
, we have by Markov’s inequality

\mathbb{P}(X\geq x)\leq\frac{\mathbb{E}[\exp_{2n}(hX)]}{\exp_{2n}(hx)}.

Then, according to equation (5.2) of
[
25
]
,

\mathbb{E}[\exp_{2n}(hX)]\leq\exp_{n}\left(\sum_{m=2}^{2n}\frac{h^{m}}{m!}|\kappa_{m}(X)|\right):=\exp_{n}(A).

Let
h_{j}=x/(H_{j}+x^{2-\alpha_{j}}/\Delta_{j}^{\alpha_{j}})
and
h=(\sum_{j=1}^{J}1/h_{j})^{-1}
.
Using (
89
), we have

A\leq\frac{h^{2}}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{J}H_{j}\sum_{m=2}^{2n}\left(\frac{m!}{2}\right)^{\gamma_{j}}\left(\frac{h}{\Delta_{j}}\right)^{m-2}.

Then, assume that
2n\leq hx
. We use
m!/2\leq(2n)^{m-2}
for
m\leq 2n
(see
[
25
]
), to get

A\leq\frac{h^{2}}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{J}H_{j}\sum_{m=2}^{2n}\left((hx)^{\gamma_{j}}\frac{h}{\Delta_{j}}\right)^{m-2}\leq\frac{h^{2}}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{J}H_{j}\frac{1}{1-(hx)^{\gamma_{j}}\frac{h}{\Delta_{j}}}.

Note that

\displaystyle(hx)^{\gamma_{j}}\frac{h}{\Delta_{j}}

\displaystyle=\frac{(xh)^{1+\gamma_{j}}}{x\Delta_{j}}\leq\frac{(xh_{j})^{1+\gamma_{j}}}{x\Delta_{j}}=\left(\frac{x^{2}}{x^{2}+H_{j}(\Delta_{j}x)^{1/(1+\gamma_{j})}}\right)^{1+\gamma_{j}}<1.

Hence,

\displaystyle 1-(hx)^{\gamma_{j}}\frac{h}{\Delta_{j}}\geq 1-\frac{x^{2}}{x^{2}+H_{j}(\Delta_{j}x)^{1/(1+\gamma_{j})}}=\frac{H_{j}(\Delta_{j}x)^{1/(1+\gamma_{j})}}{x^{2}+H_{j}(\Delta_{j}x)^{1/(1+\gamma_{j})}}=\frac{H_{j}h_{j}}{x}.

Subsequently,

A\leq\frac{h^{2}}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\frac{x}{h_{j}}=\frac{hx}{2},

and

\mathbb{P}(X\geq x)\leq\frac{\exp_{n}(hx/2)}{\exp_{2n}(hx)}.

Arguing exactly as in the end of proof of Theorem 2.5 of
[
25
]
(from equation (5.5)), we get

\mathbb{P}(X\geq x)\leq Ce^{-hx/2}\leq C\max_{j=1,\dots,J}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2J}\frac{x^{2}}{H_{j}+x^{2-\alpha_{j}}/\Delta_{j}^{\alpha_{j}}}\right),

for some numerical constant
C>0
.
∎
